# Review findings saved from the workflow journal (2026-10-21)

The adversarial reviews that completed before the run was stopped. Documents 01 and 02 were revised against their reviews (the findings below for them are from the review of the first draft). Documents 03, 04 and 05 were reviewed by both lenses but not revised; 06 has only the conformance review. Reviews of 07 to 09 (both lenses) were added on 2026-11-06 at the end of this file, and on the same day every finding in this file was applied to the nine documents; see 10-decision-records.md for the cross-domain rulings.


## review:conformance:01-system-topology

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (3.5 row "api, workflows, call-worker -> Langfuse: Learner id as user_id; prompt and completion text logged; no audio" and section 10 item 2): Every learner's prompts and completions (which contain verbatim call transcripts, life facts, chat text) are shipped to a third-party processor keyed by learner id for every learner, regardless of the data-use opt-in, and `learner/delete` (4.3) never purges Langfuse, Sentry or Resend. This is the one place the document breaks a hard constraint rather than waters one down.
  - Evidence: PRD 1.3 promise 8 (data use is opt-in, off by default); 12.5.5 (only anonymized transcripts, only with the Controls switch on, may be used to check and improve the teacher); 12.5.8 (purged from live systems within 24 h); 12.6 (no cross-learner data paths except the gloss cache and opt-in anonymized transcripts). The document's own stated use of the traces is "debugging a bad episode" and prompt improvement, which is exactly the 12.5.5 use.
  - Fix: Default Langfuse spans to metadata only: ids, model and prompt version, token counts, cost, latency, a hash of the prompt. Store prompt and completion text in the learner's own rows (Postgres or `forone-voice`-style per-learner S3) with the span id as the pointer, so debugging reads them from the home region under RLS. Send text to Langfuse only for learners with `data_use = true`, pseudonymized (no learner id, names scrubbed). Add to `learner/delete`: Langfuse delete-by-user, Sentry user scrub, Resend contact deletion, all within the 24 h step. Set Langfuse retention to 30 days. Record the processor list for counsel accordingly.
- **major** (3.6 row "On-demand episode (swap, return) under 30 s": "Segment 1 ships as soon as the editor's code checks pass on it; model checks 3 to 11 run on the full script while the learner listens to segment 1; a fail after that swaps in the fallback episode from segment 2 onward"; same pattern in the sample row ("code checks and the code law scan only") and the pilot row ("editor checks 3, 5, 10 in parallel with TTS of segment 1")): Audio is played to the learner before the gate has passed. Segment 1 can carry an unconfirmed life fact (check 4), an unsourced world fact (check 5), a law breach (check 10: guilt, lesson words, grading). Splicing a fallback episode from segment 2 onward produces an episode whose first scene belongs to a different story, breaking the stand-alone rule and law 4. For the sample, the law scan is reduced to a code word list although PRD 6.3 makes it a model check, and the sample is the one surface strangers forward.
  - Evidence: PRD 6.3 ("Every check is pass or fail"; on second fail ship the fallback episode, which is a whole episode built without life facts, arcs or callbacks); 6.3 check 10 is listed among the editor-model checks; 1.2 laws 1, 4, 5 ("A feature that breaks one does not ship"); 2.1.3 ($0.05 ceiling leaves room: a Haiku-class pass over a 250-word sample script costs well under $0.01).
  - Fix: Run the model checks 3, 4, 5, 7, 10 on segment 1 (scene 1) before `episode.streaming_started`; a Haiku-class pass on one scene is 2 to 4 s, inside the 30 s and 45 s budgets. Stream only after it passes. A later fail on segments 2+ repairs those segments or restarts with the whole fallback episode plus Salma's one line, never a splice. Use the model law scan on the sample. Change the pilot row to "checks 3, 5, 10 on scene 1 before scene 1 plays".
- **major** (2 (requirements table), 3.6 row "First open to first sound" (`POST /v1/onboarding/start` creates a `pre_account` learner and a LiveKit room), 4.2 schemas): Invite-only access and the waitlist are not implemented anywhere: no `waitlist` or `invites` table, no invite check on onboarding start, no mechanism to carry waitlist answers into A3. As written, anyone who reaches the URL gets an unlimited number of anonymous 3-minute Salma calls on LiveKit plus a pilot episode on on-demand pricing (about $0.30 to $0.50 each), with no limit like the one `sample_requests` enforces.
  - Evidence: PRD 1.4 Access: "Invite-only from the waitlist [NEW]"; 2.1.5 and 2.1.6 (waitlist form fields; invites in batches of about 50 a week from 2026-12-07; "Each invite carries the waitlist answers into onboarding, so Salma does not ask them again"); 19.3. The document's week 3 says "The waitlist answers collected since M0 flow into onboarding" with no store to flow from.
  - Fix: Add `waitlist` (id, name, contact, contact_hash, why_english, self_rating, city, sample_episode_id, created_at) and `invites` (token, waitlist_id, batch_no, sent_at, redeemed_at, learner_id). `POST /v1/onboarding/start` requires an unredeemed invite token and copies the waitlist answers into the `pre_account` learner's intake facts. Add an `/admin/invites` page that sends a batch of 50 (invite email as a transactional kind, see the 3.10 gap below). Add a global daily cap on onboarding starts as a spend guard.
- **major** (6 cost table, row "Infra per session": "$1.30 at 15 learners ... about $0.05 to $0.06 at 10,000 learners ... Below the PRD's $0.03 hosting line only at scale"): The sentence is arithmetically wrong ($0.05 to $0.06 is above $0.03, not below it), and the document never says how the beta's cost-per-session gate counts infrastructure. If fixed infra is included, the week-5 measured cost per session is about $0.66 AI plus $1.30 infra, above the $1.00 stop line on 2026-12-07; if excluded, the 19.2 exit criterion and the 16.1 gate need that rule stated. At scale, infra alone is 15 to 20% of the $0.30 to $0.35 target and no lever is named.
  - Evidence: PRD 14.5 (a session's cost includes "Proof share and hosting $0.03"; stop line $1.00; scale target $0.30 to $0.35); 16.1 (cost per session under $0.60, stop over $1.00); 19.2 exit criteria ("a measured cost per session; a costed path to $0.35 or less"). Arithmetic: $8,000 to $10,000 / (10,000 x 20 sessions) = $0.04 to $0.05; $400 to $600 / 300 sessions = $1.33 to $2.00.
  - Fix: Correct the sentence. Add a [DECISION] on the metric: the 16.1 number is AI plus variable infra (LiveKit minutes, S3 egress, Inngest steps) per session, with fixed infra reported separately and amortized at the planned 250-learner run-rate ($0.10), and hand that rule to the cost domain. Add a row showing scale infra against the $0.35 target and name the levers (dedicated `media` service, CloudFront for produced audio once the security domain allows a service key, self-hosted Inngest above $0.01 per session).
- **major** (7 Build plan, weeks 2 to 4, and 3.6 rows "First open to first sound" and "Call connect"): The document's own design makes A3 a LiveKit call served by `call-worker` with the Salma persona, but `call-worker` is built in week 4 while onboarding A1 to A10 ships in week 3, so week 3 cannot deliver the first sound under 60 s or the two English turns. The 50 scripted calls (14.4 #3) also move to week 4 although the PRD tags the vendor tests P0 "in the beta build's first two weeks"; a streaming-STT or call-TTS vendor change after the week-4 test lands in week 5, on top of the after-session pass and the production stand-up.
  - Evidence: PRD 19.2 week 3: "Onboarding (A1 to A10) and Salma; the player; the word sheet; the feed; drop notifications; payments"; week 4: "The call pipeline"; 14.4 "[P0, in the beta build's first two weeks]" item 3 "Call latency: 50 scripted test calls"; Part 15 first-sound budget; 2.2 A2 offers Talk or Type and the document gives Type no path at all.
  - Fix: In week 2 stand up a minimal LiveKit Agents worker (reference STT, LLM and TTS plugins, static persona, no brief fetch, no talkback) and run the 50 scripted calls against it, so the vendor pair is chosen by 2026-11-15 as the PRD asks; serve A3 on it in week 3 and ship the typed A3 path through `api` in the same week (it is also the A2 "Type" option the PRD requires); grow the worker into the full call pipeline in week 4. State the 14.4 timing deviation explicitly if it cannot be met.
- **major** (3.6 row "On-demand episode (swap, return)" and 4.3 event `LearnerReturnDetected` "sender: api (app open or feed fetch after 5+ days)"): Podcast apps poll the RSS feed automatically, typically hourly, whether or not the learner ever plays anything. Firing `learner.return_detected` on a feed fetch starts a return episode on standard pricing for a learner who is not there, and does so again on each poll unless something dedupes it; the inactive learner the allowance was designed to cost nothing becomes the most expensive learner.
  - Evidence: PRD 3.8.3 ("when the learner next opens the app or plays the feed"; plays, not fetches); 3.6.2 (no episode after an inactive day; only one episode ever waiting); 1.4 cost budget. Apple Podcasts and most apps refresh subscribed feeds on a schedule.
  - Fix: Trigger return detection only on app open or on the first enclosure (`.mp3`) Range request from the feed, idempotent on `learner_id + local_date` and only when no return episode exists for that date; the XML keeps the stale item until then. Log XML fetches separately (they are also needed for the 12.3 hours-heard rule, which counts feed episodes only once the learner later uses the call, chat or small door).
- **major** (3.6 row "Audio deleted within 15 min of the call when storage is off" and 4.3 `audio/expire` (trigger `call.ended {voice_storage: false}`) and `call/verbatim-turn` ("if voice storage off and this was the last turn: delete the turn audio")): The 15-minute path exists only for live-call audio. Small-door voice notes, say-it-once, shadow lines and the then-and-now recording are learner audio with no `call.ended`, so when voice storage is off they are covered only by the `ephemeral` flag, a daily scan and a 1-day S3 lifecycle, i.e. up to 24 h instead of 15 min. Within a call, only the last turn is deleted when its verbatim succeeds; earlier turns wait for the 14-minute sleep although the PRD deletes on verbatim success.
  - Evidence: PRD 12.5.2 ("audio is analysed and deleted within 15 minutes of the call... The 4-weekly recording follows the same rule unless the learner keeps that one recording explicitly"); 5.4.11 (deleted when the verbatim re-transcription succeeds, and in any case within 15 minutes); 10.2.1 lists call, round two, shadow, say-it-once and small-door voice notes as audio turns; Part 15.
  - Fix: Make `audio/expire` fire on every `media_objects` insert of a learner-voice kind with `ephemeral = true` (`expires_at = created_at + 15 min`), not only on `call.ended`; have `call/verbatim-turn` delete each turn's audio when that turn's verbatim succeeds; keep `retention/daily` as the audit only. Add a `kept_explicitly` flag on `media_objects` for the one then-and-now recording the learner may choose to keep.
- **major** (4.2 schema: `episodes.learner_id uuid not null references learners(id)`, `media_objects.learner_id uuid not null`, "SSE-C with a per-learner data key"; 3.6 sample row): The sample episode is made before any learner exists and must be the person's "to keep and forward", yet the schema requires an owning learner and a per-learner DEK for every episode and media object, and no share URL or expiry is defined. The P0 sample path (week 3) cannot be built on these tables as written.
  - Evidence: PRD 2.1.3 and 2.1.5 (sample before signup; "The sample is the learner's to keep and forward if they choose"); 1.4 (installable web app, no account before the first call); the document's own 3.6 row routes the sample through `episode/produce` and `media_objects`.
  - Fix: Either make `learner_id` nullable with a `sample_request_id` on `episodes` and `media_objects`, or create a `pre_account` row per sample. Store sample audio under a service key (not SSE-C) in `forone-media/samples/`, served at `GET /s/{share_token}.mp3` with a 30-day expiry and no login, and say so in 12.6 terms (it holds no learner data beyond a first name and a topic). Add the share token to `sample_requests`.
- **minor** (2 row "Nine beta roles as pipeline steps"): Wrong count; the PRD has thirteen roles with eleven in the beta (teacher, writer, fitter, editor, voice studio, host, producer, analyst, memory keeper, proof producer, recording rater). The vendors.yaml in 4.5 does list all eleven, so only the sentence is wrong, but a reader planning functions from section 2 will miss two.
  - Evidence: PRD 14.1 "Thirteen roles; eleven in the beta."
  - Fix: Change to eleven and list them; map the rater and the proof producer to their functions (`proof/make`, the finale step) in the 4.3 table.
- **minor** (2 row "Exactly three outbound message kinds: `api` has three senders and no other; nothing else holds email or push credentials" versus 4.3 `day/next` (`push.drop_notification` in `workflows`), `gap/note` ("send the note by email and push"), 3.5 "`workflows` -> `api`: none", 4.7 (`RESEND_API_KEY` to api and workflows; VAPID keys to api only)): The 3.10 guard is described as a service boundary that the document's own functions violate: `workflows` sends push and email but has no VAPID keys and no route to `api`.
  - Evidence: PRD 3.10 (exactly three kinds); document sections cited.
  - Fix: Put the three senders in `forone.core.messaging` with a closed enum of kinds as the 3.10 guard (a lint and a unit test, not a service boundary); give `workflows` the VAPID keys; rewrite the section 2 row accordingly.
- **minor** (4.3 event catalogue and function table; 9 item 5): The function table uses events absent from the catalogue (`gap.armed`, `chat.closed`, `recording.completed`, `verbatim.turn_completed`, `verbatim.completed`, `notes.ready`, `episode.fallback_shipped`, `ideas.ready`), `feed.publish` reads `feed_items.publish_at` from a table this document never defines or assigns (the pipeline document defines it as written by the pipeline and read by delivery), and `episode.streaming_started` carries `first_segment_media_object_id` here but `first_segment_url` in the pipeline document. The catalogue claims names are canonical.
  - Evidence: 02-generation-pipeline.md lines 83, 116, 125; this document 4.3 and 9.1.
  - Fix: Complete the catalogue with every event named in the table; add `feed_items` to 4.2 or state its owner and schema in 9.1; align the `episode.streaming_started` payload with the pipeline document (media object id, with the URL minted by `api`).
- **minor** (4.3 "Inngest sees pointers, never content" versus `SampleRequested {name, topic, job}`, `LearnerExportRequested {email}`, `EpisodeReady.artifacts: dict`): Three payloads carry PII or content into Inngest Cloud EU, contradicting the rule the section opens with and the processor description given to counsel in section 10 item 2 ("holds ids and timestamps only").
  - Evidence: Document 4.3 and 10.2; PRD 12.6.
  - Fix: Pass `sample_id` only (name, topic, job stay in `sample_requests`); look the email up at send time; type `artifacts` as a list of media object ids.
- **minor** (3.4 steps 1 and 2 and 4.3 `day/next` (`drop_at` = next local calendar day at the drop time)): Three untreated cases: (a) the onboarding day is active at A7 (a call started) but `learner_schedule` exists only after A9, so a `day.became_active` sent at A7 fails in `compute-drop`, and if none is sent, the A10 pick starts `episode/produce` with no `day/next` waiting, so day 2 gets no feed item and no push; (b) a learner active before today's drop time (plays yesterday's episode at 06:00, drop at 07:40) waits about 25 h for the next new episode under the "next calendar day" rule, which is a [DECISION] the document makes without tagging it; (c) the PWA replays events up to 24 h late (section 5), so `day.became_active` can arrive after `drop_at - 6h` or after `drop_at`, giving a negative timeout.
  - Evidence: PRD 2.2 A7, A9, A10 ("Tomorrow at 7:40"; the pilot counts as day 1's episode); 3.6.1; 3.2; Part 15.
  - Fix: `api` sends `day.became_active` at A9 once the schedule is saved. Tag the calendar-day rule as [DECISION] and state the before-drop case. In `day/next`, if `now > drop_at - 6h` on start, skip the pick wait and send `learner.cutoff_reached` at once; if `now > drop_at`, publish immediately on `episode.ready` without a push.
- **minor** (4.3 `day/next` config "cancelOn: learner.schedule_changed" and 4.2 `learner_schedule.version` ("so a running day/next can be cancelled and restarted")): Nothing restarts the run after cancellation, and a restarted run would wait again for `idea.chosen` even when the pick and the episode already exist.
  - Evidence: Document 4.2 and 4.3; PRD 3.9.4 re-anchoring and 12.4 (drop time changeable in Controls).
  - Fix: `api` re-sends `day.became_active` with the new `schedule_version` after the change; `day/next` first reads `episodes` for a daily episode with that `local_date` and skips the wait steps that are already satisfied; `episode/produce` recomputes its cut lines from the new `drop_at` on `learner.schedule_changed`.
- **minor** (4.3 `proof/fanout` and `retention/daily`; 4.2 `learners.status`): `proof_weekday` is null until the first week is known, so new learners are never selected for a proof although the PRD gives them one on day 7; and nothing stops `day.became_active`, `proof/fanout` or `gap/note` for `trial_ended` learners, for whom new episodes must stop.
  - Evidence: PRD 11.1.1 ("until then, day 7"; it never waits for a minimum number of sessions); 12.5.9 ("new episodes stop" after an unpaid trial).
  - Fix: `proof/fanout` falls back to onboarding weekday + 7 when `proof_weekday` is null; add a status guard (`trial`, `paid` only) in `api` before sending `day.became_active`, and in the two fan-outs.
- **minor** (4.5 vendors.yaml: `call_tts: same vendor as episode_tts so the host sounds the same`; `embeddings: {vendor: openai}` [ASSUMPTION]): If Gemini 3.8 Flash TTS wins the 14.4 blind test (it is one of the three candidates), there is no streaming low-latency voice from that vendor for the call, so "same vendor" cannot hold; the test protocol must rate the episode-plus-call pair, or the document must say what happens. OpenAI embeddings add a third text processor that receives every learner script and is not in the section 10 processor list for counsel.
  - Evidence: PRD 14.3 (call uses "the episode vendor's low-latency voice"; candidates Gemini 3.8 Flash TTS, Inworld TTS-2, ElevenLabs v4); AI-architecture lens table (call voices listed: Inworld Flash, ElevenLabs Flash, Cartesia); PRD 12.7.
  - Fix: State that the blind test rates each vendor's episode voice and call voice as a pair and that a vendor without a streaming voice is eligible for `episode_tts` only with a declared accent-matched `call_tts` from the secondary (announced on air per 7.1.3). Add OpenAI to the processor list or use Gemini embeddings (already a processor) or a local model in `workflows`.
- **minor** (4.5 `editor` fallback (`anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`) and 5 failure rows "Anthropic API down" and "Editor (Gemini) down"): Two independent fallbacks collide: when Anthropic is down the writer moves to Gemini Flash while the editor is already Gemini, so writer and editor share a vendor; when Gemini is down the editor moves to Haiku, same vendor as the writer. Both are degraded modes the PRD does not allow and they are tagged only as "logged".
  - Evidence: PRD 6.1.4 and 14.3 (editor from a different vendor than the writer).
  - Fix: Make the pair rule explicit in the adapter: `editor.vendor != writer.vendor` is checked at selection time; when the writer fails over to Gemini, the editor flips to the Anthropic fallback, and vice versa; tag both as [ASSUMPTION] degraded modes with the doubled human-review sample.
- **minor** (7 row "Cuts if there is one engineer" and 4.7 environments table): "fixed 2 call-worker tasks, enough for 50 concurrent calls" contradicts 4.4 ("one Fargate task ... about 8 concurrent calls", so 2 tasks is about 16); "Drop Apple sign-in" cuts an untagged (P0) account method and must be [OPEN for Muath]; Multi-AZ is the prod baseline in 4.7 and section 5 relies on its failover, but it is listed as P1 "during the beta" in section 7.
  - Evidence: Document 4.4, 4.7, 5, 7; PRD 1.4 Accounts (Apple, Google, or email code; untagged items are P0).
  - Fix: Correct the task count (6 or 7 tasks for 50 calls, or state 16); tag the Apple cut [OPEN for Muath]; decide Multi-AZ once (recommend baseline, about $70 a month more).
- **minor** (3.4 step 6 ("Its publish step ... closes the previous open session, creates the new `sessions` row") and 4.2 `one_open_session` index): Applied to every kind, publishing a weekly proof or finale episode (kind `proof`, `finale`) would close the learner's current day session or violate the unique index, although the proof "sits above today's episode".
  - Evidence: PRD 11.1.2; Part 4 rules 7 and 8.
  - Fix: State that the session-creating publish applies to `pilot`, `daily`, `swap`, `return` only; proof and finale episodes attach to the open session (or to none) and never close it.
- **minor** (4.3 `call/verbatim-turn` "concurrency 20 global (vendor limit)" and 4.10 scaling table): At 200 to 400 concurrent calls with about 25 learner turns each, a global concurrency of 20 cannot keep up during the two-hour morning peak, so the last turn's verbatim (and the 90-second notes that depend on it) queues behind hundreds of turns. The scaling table has no row for it.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.5.1 and Part 15 (notes under 90 s); document 4.10 call concurrency row.
  - Fix: Add a scaling row: verbatim concurrency contract negotiated with the vendor before public launch; per-call priority so the last turn of a finished call jumps the queue; fall back to the secondary verbatim vendor when queue age exceeds 30 s.
- **minor** (4.2 "Postgres row-level security ... policies filter on current_setting('forone.learner_id')" and 3.5 feed route): Cross-learner work (`proof/fanout`, `season/fanout`, `retention/daily`, `vendor/health`, admin pages, the outbox relay) needs a bypass role that is never named, and the feed XML is not said to carry `<itunes:block>yes</itunes:block>` and an equivalent for other directories, which is how feeds are excluded from podcast directories.
  - Evidence: PRD 12.6 ("feed URLs ... are excluded from podcast directories"); document 4.2, 4.3.
  - Fix: Name a `forone_batch` role with RLS bypass used only by `workflows` crons and admin, audited; add the directory-exclusion tags to the feed renderer requirement.
- **minor** (4.5 `Email.send` kinds): The 90-day deletion warning (12.5.9) and the invite (2.1.6) are not in the kinds list, and neither is in PRD 3.10's three kinds either; the document should surface this PRD gap rather than silently omit the emails.
  - Evidence: PRD 12.5.9 ("deleted after one email warning"); 2.1.6 (invites); 3.10.
  - Fix: Add `retention_warning` and `invite` as transactional kinds and flag in section 10 that 3.10 needs amending (escalation rule in PRD Part 18 closing paragraph).
- **minor** (7 week 1 ("The 30 M0 clips run through `vendor-lab`" against Anthropic, Google, ElevenLabs, Inworld, Soniox, Deepgram, AssemblyAI, ElevenLabs Scribe) and 10 item 11): The Milestone 0 consent names Claude, Gemini and ElevenLabs as the processors; running the clips through five more vendors in week 1 is outside that consent as written, and the document's open item 11 asks only about the week-5 end-to-end run.
  - Evidence: PRD 10.14 ("with consent"); /mnt/project-files/milestone-0/02-consent.md lines 5 to 14.
  - Fix: Extend open item 11 to cover the vendor tests, or add a one-line consent addendum to the M0 kit before 2026-11-02 naming the recognizer and voice vendors.

Missing:
- Waitlist and invite storage plus the invite gate on onboarding (PRD 1.4 Access, 2.1.5 and 2.1.6): no tables, no endpoint, no admin batch send (see the major issue).
- Billing state in the schema: trial start at A9, founding-member cap of 100, plan, renewal, the day-6 trial-ending email, the 48 h grace the failure table assumes, and reconciliation; no `subscriptions` or `payments` table although `api` owns webhooks (PRD 1.4 Price and Checkout, 3.10 transactional kinds).
- A `consents` table with timestamps and wording version for voice storage and data use (needed as PDPL evidence and to drive `audio/expire`, `learner/purge-audio`, the Langfuse rule); the document only has a `LearnerConsentChanged` event.
- `push_subscriptions` (VAPID endpoints per device) and the rule that the drop push goes to installed devices only (PRD 3.3.2, 4.6).
- Crisis-protocol storage: PRD 7.9.4 requires every activation logged for SB 243 reporting; the admin list names a "crisis log" page but there is no `crisis_events` table or event in the catalogue, and no statement that the call-worker's step-out is recorded through `/internal/events`.
- The then-and-now recording's shape: `calls.kind = then_and_now` exists but the three-part task (retell, opinion, 8 sentences, PRD 10.9.2) and the "keep this one recording" exception (12.5.2) have no media kinds or flags; the P0 first "then" in week 1 of the beta depends on it.
- The typed path for A2/A3 ("Talk / Type", PRD 2.2) and for the chat-only learner; every onboarding path in 3.6 assumes a LiveKit call.
- A global abuse cap on `POST /v1/sample` and `POST /v1/onboarding/start` beyond the per-device 24 h limit (a bot flood at $0.05 per sample or $0.30 per pilot is the cheapest way to hit the spend cap and halt production for real learners).
- The 12.3 hours-heard counting rule needs feed enclosure fetches logged per episode and later joined to call, chat or small-door use; the media route should emit a `feed_play` event with the token and episode id.
- The human reviewer (PRD 6.8) as a contractor who reads learner episodes: absent from the section 10 processor list and from `/admin/review` access controls (which learner fields the reviewer sees).
- A statement of how the editor's rule "different vendor from the writer" survives both failovers, and how the rater pin (`pinned_until: 2026-12-31`) aligns with the first 4-week window that starts 2026-12-07 (ends 2027-01-03).
- Deletion of learner data held by LiveKit Cloud (room metadata, logs), Resend (addresses, delivery logs) and Sentry within the 24 h step of `learner/delete` (PRD 12.5.8).

Strengths:
- One durable Inngest run per learner per active day (`day/next`) implements PRD 3.6 rule 2 and the cutoff, batch-to-on-demand switch and drop directly, with no cron scan and no state tables; the rejected-alternatives row explains why.
- Envelope DEKs with S3 SSE-C give per-learner crypto-shred (PRD 12.6) at $1 a month instead of $1 per learner, keep Range reads native for the player, and are cleanly separated from text, which survives key destruction.
- Every Part 15 budget has a named request path with a time breakdown, and the two that depend on vendors are tied to a measurement (50 scripted calls, first 20 real calls); segmented media and turn-level verbatim during the call are the right mechanisms for the 30 s and 90 s budgets.
- The adapter layer is concrete: typed Protocols per capability, `vendors.yaml` plus a `vendor_config` table for same-day failover without a deploy, a weekly canary so the secondary voice vendor is proven rather than promised, and one middleware emitting `vendor_calls` and spans (PRD 14.3 and 14.5).
- Honest team sizing: a single-engineer cut list, an explicit list of what is too much (Kubernetes, multi-region, self-hosting, a separate admin app), and a failure table whose rule "a learner never sees an error page for a generation problem" resolves every failure to one of three visible states.
- The Python decision is argued from real constraints (ERRANT-style classifier, lemmatizer, LiveKit reference plugins) and the conflict with the pipeline document is surfaced for the integrator instead of hidden.

## review:conformance:02-generation-pipeline

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (4.5 Step 3, 'Prior for unseen words': p = sigmoid((size_est - rank) / (0.35 * size_est)) clipped to [0.02, 0.98]): With this prior the coverage pass is arithmetically unreachable for any learner whose word bank is mostly unseen (every new learner for the first days, every sample and pilot). The sigmoid's maximum is sigmoid(1/0.35) = sigmoid(2.857) = 0.946 at rank 1, so even a script made entirely of the top 100 words averages about 0.94, below the 95% default, below the 96% 'one step easier' the PRD mandates for the first three episodes, and below the 93% floor once any rank-1000-plus words (p about 0.87) appear. Every script fails the fitter, burns two rewrite rounds, fails editor check 1, repairs, and falls back, three times in seven days pages a human.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 6.2.3 (pass at 95% audio / 98% text), 9.6 (first three episodes one step easier: 96% / 99%), 2.1 (sample uses a stub model), 2.2 (pilot). Calculation: rank 1 gives (3000-1)/(0.35*3000) = 2.856, sigmoid = 0.9456; rank 1000 gives 1.905, sigmoid = 0.870; rank 2000 gives 0.952, sigmoid = 0.72. Top-1000 words are roughly 85% of running words in a B1 script, so expected coverage is about 0.92 at best. The clip to 0.98 is never reached.
  - Fix: Replace the prior with one that saturates: function words and the top size_est/3 lemmas at 0.98, a logistic that is 0.95 at rank size_est/2 and 0.5 at rank 1.3*size_est (or a steeper width such as 0.12 with the center shifted to 1.2*size_est), and state the self-check: 'a script of top-1000 words must score at least 0.97 under the prior'. Add a unit test to the week-1 lexicon port that runs the 10 Milestone 0 scripts under the cold-start prior and asserts they pass at 96%. Keep the ASSUMPTION tag on the numbers, not on the shape.
- **blocker** (4.6 'Decision rule (code)' and 10.5 [OPEN for Muath] Soft editor fails: 'a remaining fail on 6 to 9 or 11 only ships with review_items flagged editor_soft_fail'): This re-decides a settled PRD rule and, as written, ships episodes that break a law. Check 9 (stands alone) is the mechanism of Law 4; check 8 includes the ban list and the similarity check, where the PRD says a script above the threshold 'is regenerated', not shipped; check 7 (not generic) is the one guard the personalization lens calls the main risk. The stated reason (a fallback 'scores worse on exactly those checks by construction') is not true of the PRD's fallback, which only has to pass 1 to 5 and 10, and the PRD's header says [NEW] rules stand unless vetoed, with 'a question ... escalates before it is built', not 'ship with a flag while waiting'.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 6.3 'On a second fail, ship the fallback episode'; 1.2 Law 4 'no episode requires an earlier one to be understood (editor check)' and 'A feature that breaks one does not ship'; 6.5.4 'above the threshold it is regenerated'; reading rule in the PRD's closing paragraph.
  - Fix: Make the PRD rule the default: a remaining fail on any check after one repair and recheck is `fallback`. If a soft-ship switch is kept at all, limit it to checks 7 and 11 (taste judgments), never 8 or 9, default it off (`editor.soft_fail_ships=false`), and keep the OPEN item as a request to relax, not a shipped default.
- **major** (4.2 step table, row `editor` 'On exhaustion: second vendor unavailable: code checks only, verdict.degraded=true, episode ships'; 5 Failure modes 'Editor second vendor down'): An editor outage ships episodes that have passed none of checks 3 (language correctness, 'no errors in the model sentences'), 5 (no unsourced world facts, a [NEW] P0 rule because there is no scout), 6, 7, 9, 11, or the semantic half of 10 (guilt, 'I miss you', a host claiming to be human, which are Laws 5 and 7). The PRD says the gate is every check, pass or fail, before anything ships; a vendor outage is exactly when a second line is needed, and the adapter pattern exists to swap vendors.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 6.3 'Every check is pass or fail'; 6.1 step 4 'a mid model from a different vendor than the writer'; 14.3 'Every vendor sits behind a thin adapter, so a swap is configuration'; 1.2 Laws 5, 7.
  - Fix: On second-vendor failure, run the same rubric on a third adapter target (another vendor behind `llm-adapter`, or, as a last resort, an Anthropic model of a different class from the writer, e.g. Opus as editor over the Sonnet writer) with `verdict.degraded='same_vendor'` and a review item; ship with code checks only never. Keep the 'page if the outage lasts past the switch for 3 learners' rule.
- **major** (9 Assumptions about other domains, item 6: 'the learner's name clip is retained for name verification regardless of voice-storage consent as a 30 s clip, or deleted on revoke'): This contradicts the data rules. The name clip is learner audio; with voice storage off all audio is deleted within 15 minutes, and on revoke all stored audio within 24 hours. Nothing in Part 12.5 carves out a name clip, and the trust contract (promise 5, 8) is shown to the learner in plain words.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.5.1 to 12.5.3; 5.4.11; Part 15 'Audio deleted when voice storage is off: within 15 min'; 1.3 promises 5 and 8.
  - Fix: Verify the name within the 15-minute window (the pipeline in 4.8 needs under a minute), store only derived data in `voice_casting.name` (respelling, IPA, the ASR form and Double Metaphone key of the learner's pronunciation), and delete the clip under the same rule as call audio. 'Say it differently' in A5 re-records a fresh clip under the same rule. Remove the ASSUMPTION and state the rule.
- **major** (6 'The one budget this design does not promise on 2026-12-07 is on-demand first sound under 30 s at p95; it promises under 45 s at p95' and 4.13 item 4): The 30-second streamed budget for swap and return is a P0 requirement stated three times with no P1 tag; the document declares it unmet for launch and makes the fix (streaming writer) P1. The alternative it rejected ('pre-writing all three ideas and voicing the likely pick') is not the only one: pre-writing the rank-2 script as text in the nightly batch costs about $0.04 (the doc's own writer batch figure), no voice, and makes a swap's first sound equal to one TTS render of scene 1 (5 to 8 s per 4.13). The return episode can be generated at the day-5 boundary, since the gap and the gated claims are known then, and held until the learner opens the app. The pilot's 45 s is also at risk: the name-verification chain (Haiku respellings, three TTS renders, ASR, Metaphone) sits serially before scene 1 can render the name, and the doc does not count it.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 3.6.3 'playable within 30 seconds, streamed'; 3.8.3 'playable within 30 seconds, streamed'; Part 15 'On-demand episode (swap, return) playable: Under 30 s, streamed'; 2.2 A6 'within 45 seconds of the end of A3'; 19.2 exit criteria 'every [P0] item works'. 14.5 removed unused pre-made picks (voiced), not a text pre-write.
  - Fix: Either make the streaming writer P0 for scene 1 (it is one streaming call, the schema is already per-scene) or add a `prewrite_rank2` step to the daily run (script only, batch, no editor model call until the swap is requested) and generate the return episode on `learner.gap_5d` from the scheduler rather than on app open. For the pilot, run name verification in parallel with the writer, and have scene 1 use the best candidate with `verified=false` if verification has not finished. State the measured budget per kind in the week-3 exit numbers.
- **major** (3.2 Data flow and 4.3: the teacher produces 'tomorrow-night's ideas' in the post-pick run; 3.4 events consumed): Two teacher triggers in the spec have no implementation: a dated follow-up coming due rewrites one idea (9.1.3), and a life update in today's session rewrites the affected idea 'before showing it' (3.1.4). In this design the three ideas shown at the end of day N's session were written the previous night (about 22:05 in the worked example) and are never refreshed, so the composition rule 'at least one idea from the learner's own life if a follow-up is due' is checked against a stale state, and an idea about a moved interview is shown anyway. `ideas.ready` is emitted once per plan.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 3.1.3, 3.1.4 'If today's session brought a life update that changes an idea (the interview moved), the teacher rewrites that one idea before showing it'; 9.1.3 'Follow-up due: a dated event coming due rewrites one idea'; 9.1 'Concurrent triggers coalesce into one run'.
  - Fix: Add an `ideas/refresh` step (Sonnet or Haiku, standard, one idea at most, under 5 s) triggered by `session.after_pass.completed` and by a daily `follow_up.due` event from the learner-model domain, that re-validates the three ideas against facts_usable and follow_ups_due, rewrites at most one, and re-emits `ideas.ready`. Also state how `working_on.changed` coalesces with a running `episode/produce` under the concurrency key (today it would queue, not coalesce).
- **major** (4.6 editor table and 4.4 writer prompt 'Hard rules (checked by code after you)'): The document promises code checks it never specifies. There is no plan validator (1 grammar target, 2 to 4 words, no target more than 4 consecutive sessions, 3 to 6 new words with 80% top-3,000, at most 2 facts, exactly one wildcard, at most one arc idea, one-step-per-session dial moves, first three episodes one step easier) and no package structure validator (cold open at most 30 s with the 3 s pause, 2 to 4 scenes with seams, the throw present and addressed by name, exactly the chosen cast, name used 2 or 3 times, recap ends with a question, two shadow lines carrying targets). Nor is there a post-mix gate on the measured duration against the 4:00 and 8:00 hard bounds; the only length control is a word band before voicing. 'Models propose, code decides' is D4's reason but the deciding code is absent for these rules.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 8.1 'Models propose, code decides'; 5.1 (length hard bounds, cold open, scenes, throw, cast); 9.2.3, 9.5, 9.6, 3.1.3; 2.2 'first three episodes are pitched one step easy'.
  - Fix: Add two code steps with their failure behavior: `validate_plan` after the teacher (fail: one re-prompt with the violations listed, then the rule-based brief) and `validate_package` before the fitter (fail: a rewrite round with the violations as SentencePatch constraints). Add `check_duration` after mix (4:00 to 8:00; outside: cut or extend by the writer's rewrite of the last scene, then re-render that scene). List each rule and the field it reads, as 4.6 does for checks 1 to 11.
- **major** (4.6 check 4 'Every carries-free proper noun and every sentence mentioning a person, place, event or date is matched against facts_allowed and bible entities; any entity not in either set fails'): As written the check fails every episode: the interest entities the learner supplied (Al Hilal, Riyadh, a player's name), the side characters the writer introduces in this very package (`bible_updates`), and place names in a story-time scene are in neither set. Conversely, the check does not re-verify the sensitivity window ('sensitive topics only if the learner raised them in that week') even though `sensitivity` is in the fact shape.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 6.3 check 4 (confirmed, unexpired, not 'don't bring up', quote in the event log, at most two); 5.1 'sensitive topics only if the learner raised them in that week'; 7.4 side characters created by the writer; 8.3 Interests store.
  - Fix: Define the allowed-entity set as facts_allowed plus bible entities plus this package's `bible_updates` plus the learner's interest entities (from TeacherInput.interests) plus a per-package 'fiction entities' list the writer declares; the model half of check 4/5 then judges whether any remaining real-world entity is stated as a fact about the learner. Add the week check: a fact with sensitivity set passes only if its session is within the current 7 days.
- **major** (2 Requirements table row '2.3' and 4.6 editor checks): The document claims to implement 'the editor checks it on day 2' but no check exists: `callback` is nullable in EpisodePlan and no code or model check requires a verbatim onboarding callback when season.day = 2.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 2.3 'The day-2 episode carries at least one verbatim callback sourced verbatim from something said in onboarding. The teacher puts it in the brief; the editor checks it on day 2.'
  - Fix: In `validate_plan`: if season.day == 2 then callback != null and callback.session_id is the onboarding session; in editor check 4 code: the callback quote is present verbatim in the script and `facts.verify_quote` passes. Fail path: one teacher re-prompt, then rule-based brief with the first usable onboarding fact as the callback.
- **major** (4.4 WriterBrief and 4.8 Casting: 'swap and accent are writes to voice_casting'; D9 'the next shipped episode carries one authored announcement line' (vendor failover only)): A learner-initiated voice swap or host rename after onboarding must be announced on air in the next episode, and the brief has no field to carry that. `voice.change_requested` is consumed but nothing in WriterBrief or the writer prompt tells the writer to say it; `SeriesBible.hosts.display_name` changes silently. The same gap covers the flip-back after failover.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 7.1.3 'Changes after onboarding (voice swap or rename, both through Salma or Controls) are announced on air in one line in the next episode. A host's voice, personality or memory never changes silently'.
  - Fix: Add `announcements: {kind: 'voice_swap'|'rename'|'vendor_change'|'vendor_restore', host, line_ar_en?: string}[]` to WriterBrief, filled from a `pending_announcements` table written on `voice.change_requested` and on failover state changes, cleared by `publisher` when the episode ships; add a writer rule and a code check that each pending announcement appears once in the cold open.
- **major** (4.4 CallBrief type and 4.1 `rehearsal` kind): The mid-season rehearsal is a P0 format (no tag in 7.6.5; 0.1 row 1 puts it in the beta) whose call 'is the scene itself, with the host playing the other person'. CallBrief has position, pushback, moves and recasts for a debate but no scene fields (the situation, the host's role as the other person, the learner's goal in the scene, how it ends), so the call domain cannot run it.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 7.6.5; 0.1 row 1; 2.4 item 3 (the season goal is a real scene).
  - Fix: Add `scene: {setting, host_role, learner_role, goal_can_do, opening_line, success_condition, exit_move} | null` to CallBrief, required when `script.format == 'rehearsal'`, and a writer prompt paragraph for the rehearsal pattern; keep the move contract fields since 5.4.6 still applies in the scene.
- **major** (4.13 item 5 (sample) and 4.1 `sample` row): The sample episode spec requires it to end with Salma, in Arabic, saying the full show is a new episode like this every day plus a call with the hosts. The sample kind renders no Salma line (the feed tag is a different text and is skipped for the sample), so the one conversion line of the acquisition funnel is missing. The sample's cost line ($0.035) also omits that render.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 2.1.4; 2.1.3 cost ceiling $0.05; Part 15 'Sample episode playable: under 30 s'.
  - Fix: Add a fixed Salma closing clip to the language package (`salma_strings.sample_close_ar`), rendered once per Salma voice and appended at mix for `episode_kind=sample`; count it in the sample cost (about $0.002) and in the sample's first-sound path (it is the last segment, so nothing waits on it).
- **minor** (4.13 item 2: 'The writer is called twice in parallel: write_script ... and write_rest (article, call brief, ...)'): The article must arm the reader against the script and add a counterargument, a missed fact or a reason to switch; written in parallel it has only the throw question, not the script. The on-demand swap and return episodes therefore ship articles that cannot meet the content contract.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.3.2 'The writer receives the script and is told to arm the reader against it'.
  - Fix: Start `write_rest` when the script finishes (it is off the first-sound path, which depends only on scene 1), or pass the finished script to it and accept the 10 to 15 s delay on the article, which the learner reads after the episode anyway.
- **minor** (4.2 `wait_after_pass` 'waitForEvent, 15 min'): On a cutoff trigger (`learner.cutoff_reached`, drop minus 6 h) the session ended hours earlier and `session.after_pass.completed` has already fired; a plain waitForEvent waits the full 15 minutes and then wrongly sets `plan.evidence_lag=true` on every cutoff episode.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 3.1.5 (cutoff), 9.1 (after-session pass within 10 minutes), 10.2.8.
  - Fix: Check `learner_model_version` from `briefs.compile` against the last session id before waiting; wait only when the pick arrived within 15 minutes of session end.
- **minor** (6 Cost table, 'Levers ... (3) batch TTS ... P1 because the batch turnaround is unverified'): The PRD's stack says episode voices are 'generated in batch' and 'batch for everything nobody waits on' is a day-one lever; deferring it to P1 leaves the voice line at $0.135 instead of about $0.07 and is the single biggest gap to the $0.35 costed path the exit criteria require. The unverified turnaround is a week-1 test, not a reason to defer.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 14.3 'generated in batch'; 14.5 levers; 19.2 exit criteria 'a costed path to $0.35 or less'.
  - Fix: Add 'batch TTS turnaround on 20 jobs, p95' to the week-1 vendor tests (14.4) for both finalists; if p95 is under 2 h, batch TTS joins the cut-line mechanism in week 2 with `t_voice_cut = drop - 2 h`.
- **minor** (7 Build plan, week 3 'name pronunciation from the intake clip' and 4.8 'transcribed by the streaming recognizer (Arabic-capable)'): The streaming recognizer is the call domain's week-4 deliverable; the name pipeline in week 3 depends on it. The batch verbatim ASR adapter already exists in week 1 and handles Arabic.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 19.2 week 3 (onboarding) versus week 4 (call pipeline); 14.3 streaming recognition row.
  - Fix: Transcribe the name clip with `asr-adapter.transcribe_batch` (10 s of audio, under 3 s) and drop the dependency on the streaming recognizer.
- **minor** (4.6 editor prompt [USER] 'FACTS_ALLOWED: {facts}' and '{name}, {age}, {job} in {city}'; 4.14 review page 'shows the script, article, audio'): The learner's name, age, city, job and quoted life facts go to a second vendor (Gemini) and to a paid human reviewer; age and city are not in TeacherInput or WriterBrief so their source is unspecified; and the PDPL review (0.2 item 3, 12.7) needs the list of processors that see personal data. Not a rule breach by itself, but the document does not flag it to the privacy domain.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.6 'no cross-learner data paths except ...'; 12.7; 1.3 promises 5 and 6; 6.8.
  - Fix: Add to section 9 item 6: the editor call and the review page are processors of personal data; pseudonymize the name and drop age/city in the editor prompt (the pitch test works from interests and job); show the review page with facts quoted but name replaced; list both for the DPA and the consent text.
- **minor** (4.4 WriterBrief `length.article_words {min,max}`): The mapping from reading estimate to article length is not stated; the Milestone 0 figure the team will copy (220 to 320) is below the PRD's B1 band.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.3.1 '150 to 300 words at A2, 250 to 450 at B1 and B2, 350 to 600 at C1 and above'.
  - Fix: State the table as config `writer.article_words_by_reading_estimate` with those three bands, chosen from `skills.reading.value`.
- **minor** (3.4 'Events consumed: idea.chosen {learner_id, idea_id, session_id}'): A remixed idea (long-press to change topic, format or cast) and a 'Tell Salma an idea' pick are not identifiable by `idea_id` alone; the brief's `learner_description` has no event source.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 3.1.2, 3.1.6.
  - Fix: Extend the event to `{idea_id | null, overrides?: {topic?, format?, cast?}, learner_text?: string, salma_confirmation?: string}` and derive the brief's idea from it.
- **minor** (D5 and 4.2 'Prompt caching applies inside batches, so the cached prefix saves there too'; 6 cost table counts 6k to 7k cached tokens per call): Batch requests are processed at unknown times and the ephemeral cache lasts minutes; one request per batch per learner spread across a night gives no guarantee the prefix is warm, so the cached-token lines in the cost table are optimistic by up to about $0.02 to $0.03 a session (the doc's own figure).
  - Evidence: The document's lever (5) names this exact risk; 14.5 'measured cost replaces them from the first learner'.
  - Fix: Use the 1-hour cache TTL on the prefix breakpoint, pool learners whose cut lines fall in the same 30-minute window into one batch, and show `cache_read_input_tokens` per step in the week-2 measured cost.
- **minor** (4.3 EpisodePlan, 4.11 SeriesBible): Small spec items with no field: side characters capped at three to five per world (7.4); the return episode's optional fresh-start offer near a landmark (3.8.4); the teacher's weekly mix of listening, speaking, focus and fluency (9.1.2, 14.1); the host brief's single callback versus `callbacks_allowed[]` (8.6); the prediction formula for idea ranking is computable in code (9.7) but left to the model.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 7.4, 3.8.4, 9.1.2, 14.1, 8.6, 9.7.
  - Fix: Add `season.landmark_offer: string|null`, `weekly_mix: {listen, speak, focus, fluency}` to EpisodePlan, a code cap on `bible_updates.side_characters` (fail when the bible would exceed 5 non-dormant), reduce `callbacks_allowed` to one for the host brief, and compute `predicted_choice` in `variety-service` from taste and format history rather than asking the teacher.

Missing:
- An idea-refresh step for PRD 3.1.4 and 9.1.3 (life update or follow-up due rewrites one idea before it is shown) and the coalescing rule for triggers that arrive while episode/produce is running (9.1).
- The day-2 verbatim onboarding callback check in the editor (2.3).
- Code validators for plan caps and package structure (9.2.3, 9.5, 9.6, 3.1.3, 5.1) and a measured-duration gate against the 4:00 to 8:00 hard bounds after mix (5.1).
- A data path for announcing learner-initiated voice swaps and renames, and the flip-back after failover, in the next episode (7.1.3).
- Rehearsal-format scene fields in the call brief (7.6.5) so the host can play the other person.
- Salma's Arabic closing line on the sample episode (2.1.4).
- A costed, P0-dated path to on-demand first sound under 30 s for swap and return (3.6.3, 3.8.3, Part 15), and the name-verification chain in the pilot's 45 s budget (2.2 A6).
- The reading-estimate to article-length table (5.3.1).
- Batch TTS turnaround in the week-1 vendor tests (14.3 'generated in batch', 19.2 exit criterion 'costed path to $0.35').
- A privacy note for the editor vendor and the human reviewer as processors of personal data (12.6, 12.7), and the fate of episode artifacts on account deletion and in the export (12.5.7, 12.5.8).
- The allowed-entity definition for editor check 4 (interest entities, this package's bible_updates, declared fiction) and the sensitive-fact week check (5.1, 6.3 check 4).
- The casting race: what happens to an in-flight pilot render when the learner swaps voice or accent during A5 (2.2 A5).
- The remix and 'Tell Salma an idea' payload in idea.chosen (3.1.2, 3.1.6).
- The follow-the-learner's-description-on-conflict rule for custom formats in the writer prompt (6.5 format library).

Strengths:
- One parameterized durable function for every episode kind with a per-step timeout, retry and exhaustion rule, and explicit degraded flags on every artifact so nothing fails silently: this is exactly the 'durable workflow step with a timeout and retries' PRD 6.1 asks for, and the failure-mode table is the most complete in the set.
- The code/model split of the eleven checks (D7) and the fitter as code plus one Haiku tagging call (D6) honor 'models propose, code decides' (8.1) and keep the coverage number exact and reproducible; the coverage algorithm, sentence-selection rule and 25% cap per round are precise enough to port straight from coverage-checker.html.
- The AudioManifest and WordIndex schemas isolate the player from the voice vendor (D10, D12): one timing shape regardless of vendor, the gloss index shipped with the episode, and the pause_no_capture window for the cold open, which is what makes the 300 ms word sheet and the 2 s drop start real rather than aspirational.
- Two TTS vendors with pre-paired understudy voices, automatic sticky failover and the on-air announcement rule (D9) meet 7.1.3 and Part 15 'second vendor within the same day's production' with nobody awake at 02:00.
- The cost table is itemized per step with standard and batch columns and reconciles to the PRD's $0.36 generation line; the arithmetic checks out against the lens prices (Opus batch $0.077, Gemini TTS 15,000 audio tokens at $9/M = $0.135, ElevenLabs 8.3k chars at $0.08/k = $0.66), and the document is honest about which budget it cannot meet on 2026-12-07 instead of hiding it.

## review:conformance:03-live-call-and-voice

Verdict: needs-revision

- **major** (4.15 Latency budget, row '@moves header parsed: 120 ms p50 / 250 ms p95 (about 40 tokens at streaming speed)' and 4.3 'The worker buffers tokens until the first newline (about 40 tokens, 100 to 200 ms)'): The header cost is under-counted by 3 to 5x, which puts the PRD 15 median gate (1.5 s) at risk. The example header `@moves {"recast":{"heard":"I go","form":"went"},"clarify":false,"nudge":false,"recall_gap":"rg2","retell":false,"praise":false,"close":false,"goodbye":false,"crisis":false,"ai_disclosure":false}` is about 60 to 70 tokens, and 40 tokens in 120 ms implies 330 tokens/s, far above a Sonnet-class streaming rate (roughly 70 to 120 tokens/s). First audio therefore waits 0.5 to 0.9 s for the header alone, so the honest p50 is about 1.3 to 1.7 s, not 0.95 s, before the vendor round trips that 01 §3.6 adds (0.4 to 0.55 s from me-central-1).
  - Evidence: PRD 15 'Host reply after end of learner turn under 1.5 s (median), under 2.5 s (p95)'; PRD 14.4 #3 [P0]; arithmetic on the header in 4.3 at 70 to 120 tokens/s; 01-system-topology.md §3.6 'Summed honestly the median is 1.1 to 1.6 s with little headroom' and its CALL_WORKER_REGION test, which this document neither adopts nor rejects.
  - Fix: Shrink the header to a positive-only, single-letter form of about 8 to 15 tokens (for example `@m r=went<I go g=rg2` with absent keys meaning false; `crisis` and `ai_disclosure` as bare flags), measure header tokens in the 50-call test, and rebuild the 4.15 table with a measured tokens/s figure plus the 01 §3.6 vendor round-trip row and the region decision; state the margin honestly rather than 'about 950 ms'.
- **major** (4.3 Phase machine 'any ─(crisis)─► CRISIS ─► ENDED(reason=crisis)' versus section 5 row 'Crisis: the session continues only if the learner chooses (the screen offers continue or stop)' and D15 'ends the argument'): The phase machine always ends the call on crisis, the failure table says the learner may continue, and D15 says the argument ends. PRD 7.9.2 requires that the character steps out, says it is an AI and cannot help, shows resources, and that the session continues only if the learner chooses, so continuation must exist as a path. The document also does not say what the host knows or does after a 'continue' (back in character, the crisis turn quarantined per 07 D9, no return to the topic).
  - Evidence: PRD 7.9.2 'The session continues only if the learner chooses'; 07-security-privacy-and-compliance.md D9 quarantine; internal contradiction between 4.3, 5 and D15.
  - Fix: Add `CRISIS ─(client crisis.continue)─► ARGUE_AFTER` with the host told via operator note to resume the argument without referring to the crisis turn, keyword/header re-triggers ending the call on a second activation; `CRISIS ─(client crisis.stop | 60 s no answer)─► ENDED(reason=crisis)`; add `crisis.continue`/`crisis.stop` to the client-to-worker protocol in 4.12 and make D15 and the section 5 row agree.
- **major** (4.4 'Learner "sorry?", "slower", "again" and the Arabic equivalents in language.repair_phrases are matched by code on the streaming final' and D15 'a package keyword list fires on a learner turn' together with D5/4.9 (the host stream is English-only; Arabic is kept out of the host's transcript)): The host's streaming recognizer is configured for English only, so Arabic repair phrases (PRD 5.4.4 rule 6 'in either language') and Arabic crisis keywords (PRD 7.9.1 'in either language') spoken on the open mic will never appear in the streaming final and cannot be matched. The two PRD rules are silently unmet for the Arabic case; only words said while holding S reach an Arabic-capable stream.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.4 rule 6; PRD 7.9.1 'Every live role ... watch for statements of self-harm intent or crisis, in either language'; D5 rationale 'keeps Arabic out of the host's transcript'; 4.9 'the recognizer is the talkback configuration (Arabic plus English) on the main stream' only for onboarding.
  - Fix: Decide explicitly: either run the host stream on Soniox with `[en, ar]` and code-switch on, strip Arabic spans from the text sent to the host and the English verbatim pass (so D5's isolation is preserved by code, not by vendor config), and match repair and crisis phrases on the raw stream; or declare in 4.4 and D15 that Arabic detection on the open mic is not possible in the beta, tag it [OPEN for Muath] because it narrows PRD 7.9.1, and give counsel the narrowed scope before 2026-12-07.
- **major** (Section 5 row '`api` unreachable: events and turn_audio_ready spooled to forone-voice/spool/{callId}.ndjson (encrypted) and ingested by retention/daily'): With `api` down, no `media_objects` row is written and no `call.turn_audio_ready` reaches Inngest, so neither `call/verbatim-turn` nor `audio/expire` runs; the spool is read by a daily job and the S3 lifecycle backstop is 1 day. For a storage-off learner the turn audio can therefore survive up to 24 hours, breaching the 15-minute rule in that failure mode. Also the `calls` row end state (`POST /internal/calls/{id}/end`) is lost in the same case and is not mentioned in the spool.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.11 'in any case within 15 minutes of the call ending'; PRD 12.5.2; PRD 15 'Audio deleted when voice storage is off: within 15 min of the call'; 01-system-topology.md §4.3 `audio/expire` is triggered by `media.object_created`, which `api` emits.
  - Fix: Give the worker a local deletion duty: when any `turn_audio_ready` post has failed and `voice_storage` is false, the worker (which holds the S3 credentials and the key) deletes the call's turn objects itself at call end + 14 min before exiting the job (or hands a delete list to a sidecar timer if the task is draining); spool into a `spool/` prefix polled every minute by a small Inngest cron (`spool/ingest`), not `retention/daily`; include the `calls` end record in the spool; add this case to 07's `privacy/verify` checks.
- **major** (4.3 Governor: `__init__(self, kind, budgets, limits, anxiety_prior)` and `ANXIETY_OVERRIDE`; CallMetrics `anxiety_mode: null|"prior"|"short_turns"|"two_silences"`; prompt layer A (no anxiety-mode instruction)): PRD 5.4.7 names three triggers; the code wires two in-call triggers plus a prior, but the bundle's `anxiety.last_call_abandoned` flag is never read by the governor (only `anxiety_prior` is), and the metrics enum has no value for it. Separately, 'more backchannel' is implemented (1.0 s threshold) but 'simpler questions' exists nowhere: the operator note carries `mode=anxiety` and the system prompt never says what anxiety mode means for the host, so the model has to guess. 'The correction moves to Salma's notes' is also not signalled to the assessment domain on the event.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.7 'or the learner's last call was abandoned ... no nudge, at most one recall gap, recasts kept, more backchannel, simpler questions. The correction moves to Salma's notes.'
  - Fix: Enter anxiety mode when `anxiety.prior or anxiety.last_call_abandoned`, add `"last_call_abandoned"` to the `anxiety_mode` enum and the `call_ended` metrics; add one paragraph to layer A defining anxiety mode (shorter, yes/no or either/or questions, one idea per turn, no nudge, keep recasts) and render `mode=anxiety` in the note as 'anxiety mode: simpler questions'; carry `anxiety_mode` and `why_anxiety` on `call_ended` so 05 can move the correction to the notes.
- **major** (1. Summary 'Code, not the model, enforces every budget in the host's move contract' and 4.3 `check()` / `allowances()`): The governor enforces only maxima. PRD 5.4.6 also has minima: recall gaps 2 to 4 per call, retell once per call, position 'pushes back at least once'. A host that never uses a recall gap, never asks for the retell and never pushes back passes the governor; nothing in the operator note or the phase clock pushes the host toward the unmet minima before the close, and the retell window (2:00 to 4:30, gated on one recall gap) is an untagged assumption that can silently prevent the retell altogether.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.6 rows 'Position', 'Recall gaps 2 to 4 per call', 'Retell once per call'; PRD 6.7 gates 'nudge budget, recall-gap count'.
  - Fix: Add a 'due' side to `Allowances` (for example `must: ["recall_gap"]` when `t >= 150 s and recall_gaps == 0`, `must: ["retell"]` at the window's end, `pushback` tracked by a header flag and nudged after minute 2); render it in the operator note ('do this turn: a recall gap'); log `minima_unmet` on `call_ended`; tag the retell window [ASSUMPTION] with the M0 timing evidence that sets it; make `violations` and `minima_unmet` both 50-call gates.
- **major** (4.13 'The 50-call latency test (PRD 14.4 #3, [P0], staging, build week 4, rerun in week 5 ...)' versus section 7 week 2 '50-call run #1 (latency only)' and 2. Requirements table row 14.4): PRD 14.4 places the vendor tests, including the 50 scripted calls, in the first two weeks of the build (so the vendor and region choices can be frozen by 2026-11-15); 4.13 schedules the first run in week 4. The document contradicts itself and the PRD on a dated P0 item. 01 §7 plans a minimal worker in week 2 for exactly this reason.
  - Evidence: PRD 14.4 'Vendor tests [P0, in the beta build's first two weeks] ... 3. Call latency: 50 scripted test calls'; PRD 18 #7 'Beta build weeks 1 to 2'; 01-system-topology.md 'minimal call-worker in week 2 so the 50 scripted calls run inside PRD 14.4's two weeks'.
  - Fix: Rewrite 4.13 to match section 7: run #1 by 2026-11-15 on the week-2 skeleton (latency, connect, TTS first-byte gate, both worker regions), run #2 in week 4 with the full move-contract gates, run #3 in week 5; state which gates apply to which run.
- **major** (4.14 `talkback_stt: {vendor: soniox, secondary: deepgram, languages: [ar, en], code_switch: true}` and 4.9 (onboarding uses the same configuration on the main stream)): The secondary cannot do the job: the voice lens states Deepgram's code-switching covers 10 languages and not Arabic. A Soniox outage therefore takes down talkback and the whole A3 onboarding conversation, contrary to PRD 15 'voice vendor outage covered the same day' and the standing rule of two wired vendors per role; the 30-clip test's code-switch metric would also show this only after the choice is written as a default.
  - Evidence: voice-speech.md 'Deepgram's covers 10 languages and not Arabic'; PRD 14.3 'Soniox by default (covers Arabic and code-switching, for talkback)'; PRD 15 'Voice vendor outage: second vendor within the same day's production'.
  - Fix: Name an Arabic-capable secondary for `talkback_stt` and the onboarding stream (AssemblyAI Universal realtime or Gladia, both claimed code-switching in the lens), include it in the 30-clip code-switch test, and keep Deepgram as the secondary only for the English host stream; add 'both Arabic-capable recognizers down' to the failure table (talkback degrades to the typed ask over the data channel).
- **major** (4.2 `crypto: { dek: string /* base64, 32 bytes, memory only */ }`, 4.10 'uploads it with SSE-C under the learner's DEK', 3.3 and 9.1 (deviations list)): The topology hands the worker a per-call key `HKDF(DEK, "call:" + call_id)` and records `media_objects.key_derivation`, explicitly 'never the learner DEK'; 07 §4.9 says the worker 'receives the bytes' from `api`. This document uses the raw DEK, which widens the blast radius of a worker compromise and makes the object rows unreadable by `api`'s re-derivation path, and section 9 does not record it as a deviation.
  - Evidence: 01-system-topology.md 'per-call key (32 bytes, in memory only, zeroized at call end; never the learner DEK)' and `media_objects.key_derivation = 'call:{call_id}'`; this document's section 9 claims to list every deviation from 01.
  - Fix: Replace `crypto.dek` with `crypto.call_key` derived as in 01, write `key_derivation` on every `media_objects` row the worker causes, keep the spool file under the environment CMK as 07 states, and either adopt 01's `call_turn_verbatim` append (04 D2) in 4.10 and 4.12 or list the update-in-place as a deviation.
- **major** (4.7 talkback flow 'host STT frames muted' and 4.10 'The recorder keeps the learner's decoded PCM ... per turn'; `CallTurnAudioReady.language: Literal["en", "ar"]`): The document never says whether the audio spoken while S is held is part of the learner turn's PCM that is uploaded and sent to the English verbatim pass. If it is, Arabic speech lands inside the English verbatim transcript and the error profile; if it is not, the `language: "ar"` value has no producer and the retention status of talkback audio (learner voice under PRD 12.5) is undefined. 05 §4.1 assumes talkback clips 'never come here'.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.5 rule 2 'That audio goes to the Arabic-capable recognizer, not to the host'; PRD 12.5 rules 1 and 2 apply to all learner voice; 05-assessment-and-after-session.md 4.1.
  - Fix: State that the recorder drops frames between `talkback.down` (minus the 400 ms pre-roll) and `talkback.up` from the turn PCM and from the full mix when storage is off, that talkback audio is streamed only (no object, no retention beyond the vendor's in-transit processing), and remove `"ar"` from `CallTurnAudioReady.language` or give it a producer; add `talkback_ms_excluded` to the `call_turn` payload so fluency measures are not distorted.
- **major** (4.4 host prompt and 4.12 event list (no wrong-memory path)): When the learner tells the host a callback is wrong, PRD 8.4 rule 7 requires the character to say so plainly, the fact to be fixed and a `wrong_memory_flag` event to be logged; PRD 16.1 gates on 'wrong memories under 1 in 100 callbacks'. The host prompt, the header and the event list have no way to detect, acknowledge or log this, so the metric cannot be computed from calls and the host may argue back from the brief.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.4.7; PRD 1.3 promise 7 'A wrong memory is a bug ... counted as a quality metric (Part 16.2)'; PRD 8.2 lists `wrong_memory_flag`; PRD 16.1.
  - Fix: Add `wrong_memory: {fact_id|null, quote}` to the header keys with the instruction 'if the listener says something you were told about them is wrong, say "My mistake, noted", declare wrong_memory and do not use that fact again'; the worker emits `wrong_memory_flag {fact_id, learner_quote, turn_n, door: "call"}` and adds 'do not mention: <fact>' to the next operator notes; the memory keeper handles the fix after the call.
- **minor** (4.3 '...ends the turn regardless' at `p + max_extend` (up to 4.0 s) and D3): PRD 5.4.4 rule 3 bounds end-of-turn silence to 1.0 to 2.5 s; the design waits up to 4.0 s (and 5.0 s in the retell) when the turn model says the sentence is unfinished. This is defensible for learners but it is an untagged departure from a stated PRD number.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.4.3 'adapts per learner, within 1.0 to 2.5 seconds'.
  - Fix: Tag the extension as [DECISION] with the voice-lens pause evidence, or cap `p + max_extend` at 2.5 s and rely on hold-to-talk for longer thinking; either way say it in 4.5 and in the requirements table row for 5.4.4.
- **minor** (4.3 Retell: 'the retell ends when the learner's turn ends or at 90 s, when the host simply replies' and Timers 'at hard_end_s (480) the worker ends after the current TTS sentence, even mid-reply'): Two places where the learner can be cut off are unspecified: the host 'replying' at 90 s while the learner is still mid-retell (PRD 5.4.6 'never cuts the learner off'), and the hard end at 8:00 arriving during a learner turn (law 7 'nobody interrupts a learner mid-sentence').
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.6 Retell row; PRD 1.2 law 7; PRD 5.4.2.
  - Fix: State that at 90 s the ring simply disappears and the host waits for the learner's end-of-turn; at `hard_end_s` the worker waits for the current learner turn to end (bounded by `p + max_extend`), then plays the goodbye line; record both as rules in the turn machine.
- **minor** (4.14 / 4.4 fallback order 'then claude-haiku-5-5 with the same prompt'): The same request body includes `thinking: {type: "between_tools"}`, which only Claude Sonnet 5.5 accepts; sent to Haiku 5.5 it returns a 400, so the first fallback step fails on every attempt. The Gemini step also needs a stated rendering for the mid-conversation operator notes, which that API does not take as system turns.
  - Evidence: claude-api skill: 'only Claude Sonnet 5.5 accepts [between_tools]'; Haiku 5.5 runs adaptive thinking by default with effort default medium.
  - Fix: For the Haiku step omit `thinking` and set `output_config.effort: "low"`; for Gemini render each operator note as a bracketed line prepended to the next user turn; add both to the adapter contract and to the weekly canary.
- **minor** (6. Cost 'holds at ... $0.14 to $0.16 with Inworld or Gemini'): The table sums to about $0.12 for the live main call with Inworld (0.014+0.001+0.034+0.015+0.002+0.029+0.001+0.005+0.015+0.004) plus about $0.05 for round two, so the Inworld case is about $0.17, slightly over the $0.16 line, not under it; the prose understates it by a few cents, which matters because the session total already sits at $0.66 against a $0.60 budget.
  - Evidence: PRD 14.5 'Call (main and round two) $0.16'; arithmetic on the section 6 table.
  - Fix: Restate the total as about $0.17 (Inworld or Gemini) to $0.22 (ElevenLabs), say that the line is met only with the shorter-host-turn lever or the Haiku host, and carry that into the cost domain's day-one levers.
- **minor** (4.9 onboarding 'Type mode sends text.turn over the data channel' and 'creates the pre-account learner and the room before the mic prompt'): The topology serves typed onboarding (A2 'Type', or mic denied) through `POST /v1/onboarding/{id}/messages` without a LiveKit room; this document requires a room and a data channel even for a learner who never grants the mic, and section 9 does not record the difference. One of the two paths has to be built.
  - Evidence: PRD 2.2 A2 'Talk (asks for the microphone only now) / Type'; 01-system-topology.md §3.6 first-sound row.
  - Fix: Pick one: keep the room for both modes (the client joins without publishing a mic; Salma's audio still plays, which also keeps 'first sound under 60 s' for typed learners) and list it in section 9 as the deviation, or accept 01's typed route and drop `text.turn` to the per-message switch only.
- **minor** (4.9 'The name clip is the learner's turn audio for the name field, uploaded like any turn' and 4.10 deletion rules): Pre-account learners have `voice_storage = false`, so `call/verbatim-turn` deletes the name turn the moment its verbatim succeeds, possibly seconds after it was spoken, while 02 §4.9 reads the clip at `onboarding.intake_completed` for name pronunciation. The race is unstated.
  - Evidence: PRD 6.1 step 5 'name pronunciation from the learner's own recording of their name'; PRD 12.5.2; 02-generation-pipeline.md name-pronunciation step.
  - Fix: Mark the name turn `hold_until_consumed: true` so the per-turn delete skips it and only `audio/expire` at +15 min removes it; require 02's name step to finish inside that window or to work from the Arabic streaming output the worker already has (`name_clip_text` on `onboarding.intake_completed`).
- **minor** (4.5 Pause profile 'p = clamp(P90 of within-turn pauses over the last 3 calls, 1.0, 2.5)' and 3.4 'learner model domain writes it; this domain specifies the formula'): 04 §4.3.4 specifies a different formula (`clamp(1000, 2500, median(last 30 end-of-turn silences) + 300)`). Two owners, two formulas; the learner-model domain is the one that writes it.
  - Evidence: 04-learner-model-and-data.md `pause_profile` and its update rule.
  - Fix: Keep one formula in 04 and reference it here; if this domain believes P90 of within-turn pauses is the right signal, say so in section 9 as an ask of 04 and emit `pauses_ms[]` on `call_turn` (already in the payload) so 04 can compute it.
- **minor** (4.11 Background row 'calls abandoned on the move is computed from this reason plus motion hints'): 'Motion hints' means reading device motion sensors, which on iOS needs an explicit permission prompt and is not in the client domain's spec; it is also sensing about the person that PRD 1.3 promise 6 ('told, not inferred') does not cover, and the PRD measure only needs the abandonment reason.
  - Evidence: PRD 16.2 'Calls abandoned on the move'; PRD 1.3.6; 06-client-and-delivery.md has no DeviceMotion permission.
  - Fix: Drop motion hints; compute the measure from `end_reason: abandoned` plus `started_by: feed` and the M0 day-7 self-report, or make it an explicit [OPEN for Muath] with the permission prompt cost named.
- **minor** (4.4 Recast handling and 4.8 captions ('marks the recast span for the caption')): PRD 5.4.6 requires the corrected word to be 'stressed in the voice, declarative intonation'; the design bolds it in the caption but sends plain text to the TTS, so the audible stress the law depends on is left to chance per vendor.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.6 Recast row; PRD 10.12 'audible recasts'.
  - Fix: Have the TTS adapter wrap the `form` span in the vendor's emphasis markup where it exists (ElevenLabs v3/v4 audio tags, Inworld markup; Gemini via style instruction) and verify audibly in week 1; log `recast_emphasis_supported` per vendor on `vendor_calls`.
- **minor** (4.13 pass lines (error survival 0.85/0.70, filler retention 0.80, hallucination 0.1 per clip, code-switch 0.8, streaming p95 500 ms)): The PRD only names the metrics; every threshold here is a choice that will decide the vendors and the confidence threshold, and none is tagged.
  - Evidence: PRD 10.14 lists the counts to make, no pass lines; PRD 18 #10 says the confidence threshold comes from data.
  - Fix: Tag each pass line [ASSUMPTION] with its rationale, and say what happens if no vendor passes a line (ship the best and raise the confidence threshold so fewer edits feed claims).
- **minor** (4.3 Timers 'Round two is offered on Today the next day'): PRD 5.4.9 makes round two available any time after the main call until the session closes and only suggests it on Today the next day; the wording here reads as next-day only.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.9; PRD 4 rule 1 'Round two opens after the main call is done'.
  - Fix: Say 'available from `calls` kind main done until the session closes; the client suggests it on Today the next day'.
- **minor** (4.11 Echo row 'the client additionally mutes the mic track for 150 ms after each host sentence starts when no earphones are detected'): Earphones are 'not reliably detectable on iOS' (same table), so on iOS this mute fires on every host sentence and can clip the first 150 ms of a learner's barge-in or answer; it is also a client behaviour the client domain does not list.
  - Evidence: PRD 1.2 law 7 (never interrupt a learner); 06-client-and-delivery.md lists no such mute.
  - Fix: Remove the mute and rely on hardware AEC plus the 0.5 s / 2-word interruption threshold; if echo proves a problem in week 3, apply the mute only on Chrome where `enumerateDevices` confirms the speaker route.
- **minor** (4.3 `allowances()`: `clarify=... and (meaning_lost or self.mode == "normal")` and the `first` kind 'clarify only if meaning is lost'): `meaning_lost` is a function input that nothing in the document computes; the governor cannot know meaning was lost, so in practice the clarify gate is only the cap of 3, and the `first` kind rule has no mechanism.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.6 Clarification request 'When an error blurs or blocks meaning'; PRD 2.2 A7.
  - Fix: Drop `meaning_lost` from the code or define it (for example the recognizer's mean word confidence for the turn under the package threshold, or the host declaring `clarify` with a `heard` string that the governor logs); keep the cap as the only hard gate and make 'clarify on a clean turn' a logged quality metric.

Missing:
- PRD 12.1 sound design on the call: 'the learner's turn is signaled by the host's breath and a soft rising tone, never a beep'. Neither this document nor 06 implements it; it belongs here as a pre-rendered clip per host voice played at HOST_SPEAKING → LISTENING, logged as not host talk time, like the backchannels.
- PRD 5.4.6 Nudge 'followed by a pause for self-repair': no rule extends the turn controller's patience after a declared nudge (for example `p + 1.0 s`, no backchannel on the first pause), so the self-repair window is the ordinary endpoint.
- PRD 5.4.6 Recast 'only on the brief's targets or on errors that blur meaning': the governor could check `recast.form` against `recast_list` and `targets.words` and log `recast_off_target`; nothing observes this half of the rule.
- PRD 4 rule 6 'the main call happens once' versus `resumed_after_drop`: the door rule in `api` must treat an abandoned, dropped or drained main call as not done, else `POST /v1/calls` refuses the fresh call; the condition (for example `end_reason in (abandoned, fallback, pipeline_error, drained) and learner_speech_ms < 120 s`) is unstated.
- PRD 7.9.1 for Salma: the talkback resolver's Arabic transcript is the only Arabic the worker ever sees; the crisis keyword check on that stream (07 lists 'Salma talkback' as a trigger) is not described in 4.7, nor is Salma's honest answer to 'are you real?' asked in Arabic during talkback (PRD 7.8.1, kind `other` in the resolver schema).
- PRD 5.4.3 host share at most 40%: no live lever. A single operator-note line when the running host share exceeds 40% after minute 2 ('shorter: one sentence, one question') costs nothing and makes the 50-call gate reachable without prompt surgery.
- Per-turn operator notes accumulate in the transcript for the whole call (about 25 system entries); the `clear_at: "next_user_message"` beta exists for exactly this and would keep the history clean without invalidating the cache; say whether it is used or why not.
- Hard end at 8:00 during a learner turn, and the 90 s retell timer during a learner turn (see the issue above): both need an explicit rule in the turn machine.
