- Onboarding `first_sound_ms` under 60 s is reported but the budget's components (page load, `POST /v1/onboarding/start`, room join, worker accept, clip start) are not laid out as 4.15 does for the main call; the A3 path is a PRD 15 budget on its own.
- The DeviceMotion permission, the `crisis.continue/stop` client messages, the `hold_until_consumed` name-clip flag and the Arabic-capable talkback secondary are all asks of other domains that section 9 should carry once decided.

Strengths:
- Per-turn upload and verbatim transcription during the call (D9) is the one design that makes both the 90-second notes budget and the 15-minute deletion rule achievable with margin; the whole-call alternative is correctly rejected.
- The declare-then-speak header with a code governor that can abort and re-request once is the right shape for law 7: it keeps first audio streaming, keeps the budgets out of the model's hands, and makes `violations` a measurable regression gate.
- Talkback on a second Arabic-capable stream opened only while S is held, with word-or-hint decided by code from the produced-lemma set, implements PRD 0.1 rows 4 and 7 exactly and keeps the host's transcript clean.
- Compiling the CallBundle at `POST /v1/calls` so a notebook veto an hour before the call is honoured (law 3) is a better reading of the PRD than compiling only at episode time, and it is cheap.
- Pre-rendered opening line, gap variant, backchannels and crisis clips remove model and TTS round trips from the moments where latency and safety matter most.
- The failure table (section 5), the single `tools/call-bench` harness for both PRD tests, the explicit client audio contract (4.11) and the honest 'too much for one to two engineers' cut list are the kind of specificity the build weeks need; keep them.

## review:conformance:04-learner-model-and-data

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (3.3 flow D 'A rebuild' and 9.4 ('its stages are pure functions ... so learner/rebuild can re-run them without vendor calls')): The rebuild design contradicts itself and the PRD. PRD 8.5 triggers a rebuild when the analyst, edit classifier or measures change version; the analyst and memory keeper are models (PRD 10.2 steps 2 and 7, 14.1), so a rebuild after an analyst change must re-call a model over every stored session. The document says 'no new vendor calls' while 9.4 says 'the analyst and classifier are re-run'. Worse, the model's proposals (AfterPassResult) are never appended to the log: the taxonomy has no event for analyst or memory-keeper output, so a code-only rebuild (classifier, measures, scheduler, updater) cannot replay without re-calling the model either. PRD 8.1 says the log is the source of truth and models only propose; the proposals are therefore part of the record.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.1, 8.5, 10.2, 14.1 ('Analyst: small model with a fixed schema'). Cost: analyst plus memory keeper is $0.04 per session (PRD 14.5); 100 learners x 20 sessions x 1 month = $80 per rebuild in December, about $8,000 per rebuild at 10,000 learners with a year of history; the stated '2 s per session' ignores model latency entirely.
  - Fix: Add an `after_pass_result` (+) event type that stores the validated AfterPassResult with analyst, classifier and memory_keeper versions. Define two rebuild kinds: (a) code-version rebuild replays stored results through the new code (no vendor calls, 2 s per session); (b) model-version rebuild re-calls the analyst and memory keeper, is costed per learner, runs in batch pricing, and needs the [OPEN for Muath] sign-off already proposed in section 10 #8. State both costs in section 6.
- **major** (4.1.2 DDL and 4.1.3 Idempotency ('Uniqueness is (learner_id, event_id); the primary key includes occurred_at only because Postgres requires the partition key')): The database does not enforce (learner_id, event_id) uniqueness: on a table partitioned by occurred_at, the only unique constraint possible is the PK that includes occurred_at. Combined with 4.1.4, which replaces occurred_at with recorded_at on skew, the same event can be inserted twice: first attempt arrives late and gets server time; the offline replay with the X-Forone-Replay header keeps the original timestamp; different partition key, no conflict, two rows. Play completions, taps and call turns then double-count, which breaks the active-day rule (PRD 3.6) and the hours count (PRD 12.3).
  - Evidence: PostgreSQL partitioned tables require unique indexes to include all partition key columns; 4.1.4's skew rule mutates the partition key; topology 4.2 requires `unique (learner_id, client_event_id)`.
  - Fix: Never mutate occurred_at; store the server's corrected time in a separate `effective_at` column (or in payload) and order by (coalesce(effective_at, occurred_at), seq). Enforce uniqueness with a small unpartitioned `event_ids (learner_id, event_id) primary key` table written in the same statement (`insert ... on conflict do nothing returning` drives which rows go into `events`). Also align the column names with the topology contract (`client_event_id`, `received_at`) or note the rename in section 9.
- **major** (4.5.3 `due_list` and `retrievability`): `retrievability` returns 0.0 for any card in state New, and `due_list` selects every structure with `state <> 'not_seen'` and every word with `status <> 'unseen'`, then sorts ascending by R and keeps the first 60. Every merely heard structure (fsrs state new, never reviewed) and every heard-only word with no review therefore sits at R = 0 at the top of the list and crowds out the genuinely due items; and kept words, which the text says 'enter the due list regardless of R', are sliced away by `[:60]` when their R is high. The teacher brief's due list (PRD 8.6 'the full due list', 9.4.2) is wrong on both counts.
  - Evidence: PRD 9.4.2 'Due means predicted recall below 0.85 by the next drop time'; PRD 9.5.4 'Kept words are scheduled like targets'; the code as written in 4.5.3.
  - Fix: Only cards with reps >= 1 are candidates for 'due'; heard-but-never-reviewed structures reach the teacher through the structure_map state and the avoidance flag, not the due list. Return kept words and active targets in their own lists outside the 60-cap. Use the library's own retrievability (`Scheduler.get_card_retrievability`) instead of a hand-written FSRS-4.5 curve that will diverge from the pinned py-fsrs version, and note that `next_interval` is not a public py-fsrs API.
- **major** (4.7.3 Sensitivity and 4.7.5 `facts_usable` (sensitive facts usable until end of week, exposed to the writer via `facts_allowed`)): PRD 8.4.3 says a sensitive fact 'is used only in the session where the learner raised it, stored only if the learner confirms, and never brought up first'. The document makes a confirmed sensitive fact usable by the teacher and writer for episodes for the rest of the week, with a carve-out when the idea came from 'tell Salma'. That is PRD 5.1's rule for sensitive *topics* in episodes stretched to cover stored *facts*, and it waters down 8.4.3 and trust promise 6.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.4.3 vs 5.1; PRD 1.3 #6.
  - Fix: Default to the stricter reading: `facts_usable` excludes rows with `sensitivity is not null` for every role except `salma_notes` and `chat_host` within the same session_id; the writer never receives a sensitive fact. Record the 5.1-versus-8.4.3 tension as [OPEN for Muath] with the week rule as the alternative, instead of deciding it silently here.
- **major** (4.11 Object storage layout (`forone-voice/{learner_id}/ephemeral/calls/...`; 'one lifecycle rule is the backstop') and D15): S3 lifecycle rules filter by key prefix (or object tag), not by a path segment in the middle of the key. With `{learner_id}/ephemeral/...` there is no single prefix that matches all learners' ephemeral objects, so the 'one lifecycle rule' backstop for the 15-minute deletion promise does not exist. The layout also covers only calls and recordings with an ephemeral variant: say-it-once, shadow, voice notes and claim clips have no ephemeral path at all, although PRD 12.5.2 and 15 apply the 15-minute rule to every learner-voice object and the topology lists all seven kinds. Finally the key layout disagrees with the topology (`forone-voice/learners/{lid}/calls/{callId}/{ephemeral/}turn-{n}.ogg`), which the call-worker already builds on.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.11, 12.5.2, Part 15 'Audio deleted when voice storage is off: within 15 min'; topology 3.2 row 'Audio deleted within 15 min' (seven kinds) and section 4.6 step 4 (key layout); AWS S3 lifecycle filter semantics (prefix or tags).
  - Fix: Either put `ephemeral/` first (`forone-voice/ephemeral/{learner_id}/...`, one prefix rule, per-learner listing still works with a second prefix) or tag every ephemeral object `ephemeral=true` and filter the lifecycle rule by tag. Give every learner-voice kind an ephemeral variant. Reconcile the layout with the topology in section 9 and name which document wins.
- **major** (4.12.2 Deletion step 5 (the hand-listed purge order)): The purge is a hand-written table list and it already misses tables that hold learner text or identity: `model_io` (prompt and completion text per learner, topology 4.2, 90-day partitions), `recordings` (assessment domain), `subscriptions`/`payment_events`, `crisis_log`, `audit_log`, `push_subscriptions`, `accounts`/`otp_codes`, chat threads, `waitlist`/`invites`, `working_on_additions` is listed but `skill_snapshots` of other domains are not. Learner text surviving in `model_io` after the 'account deleted' email breaks PRD 12.5.8 and trust promise 5. `crisis_log` is the opposite case: PRD 7.9.4 says every activation is logged for annual SB 243 reporting, so deleting it may be wrong, and the document does not rule on it.
  - Evidence: PRD 12.5.8 'purged from live systems within 24 hours'; PRD 7.9.4; topology 4.2 `model_io(learner_id, prompt, completion)`.
  - Fix: Drive the purge from `information_schema` (every table with a `learner_id` column, in FK-dependency order computed at run time) with an explicit allow-list of tables that keep a de-identified row (`deletion_ledger`, `crisis_log` pseudonymized, cost aggregates), and a CI test that fails when a new learner_id table is not classified. Mark the crisis-log retention as [OPEN for Muath] with counsel.
- **major** (4.7.4 Time ('dated plans expire the day after dated_event_on') together with follow-ups ('due_on = dated_event_on + 1 day') and 4.7.5 `facts_usable`): A dated fact becomes `dormant` the day after the event, and `facts_usable` excludes dormant facts; the follow-up for that fact becomes due on the same day. The writer therefore cannot use the fact for the follow-up episode, and the editor's check 4 ('every life fact used is confirmed, unexpired') fails it. The day-after-the-interview callback, which PRD 3.1.3 and 8.4.5 require ('a dated event triggers one follow-up after its date, then moves to history'), can never ship.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.4.5, 3.1.3, 6.3 check 4; the two rules in 4.7.4 read together.
  - Fix: A dated fact stays `active` until its follow-up is `used` or `expired` (21 days), then moves to `dormant`; `facts_usable` exposes a `follow_up_due` flag so the teacher can place it. Add the example to the test plan in week 2.
- **major** (4.10 and 4.14 (name clip 'kept regardless of voice-storage consent'), section 10 #4): The document's default keeps the learner's own name recording after voice-storage consent is revoked. PRD 12.5.3 says 'all stored audio is deleted within 24 hours' and Part 15 repeats it. The exception may be reasonable, but the default must comply with the PRD and the exception be the OPEN item, not the reverse.
  - Evidence: PRD 12.5.3, Part 15 'All audio deleted after revoking consent: within 24 h'.
  - Fix: Default: the name clip is a learner-voice kind, ephemeral when consent is off, purged on revoke; hosts use the respelled text. Keep section 10 #4 as the request to carve out an exception with one sentence of consent copy.
- **major** (7. Build plan, week 5 and the 'one engineer' cut): Week 5 (2026-11-30 to 12-06) carries `apply_after_pass` complete, all five learner-edit types, claims, proof and return briefs, export, delete, retention jobs, the research set, a minimal rebuild, the PITR drill and the PII test, for a one-to-two-engineer team that is also integrating with the assessment domain that week. The P0 data rules (PRD 12.5.7 export within 1 h, 12.5.8 deletion) are mechanical and independent of the after-pass; stacking them in the last week is where they slip. The cut for one engineer moves the rebuild runner and `research_transcripts` to P1 without an [OPEN for Muath] tag although PRD 8.5 (untagged, so P0) requires rebuilds and the exit criterion is 'every P0 item works'.
  - Evidence: PRD 19.2 week 5 scope; PRD header tagging rule; PRD 12.5; the ground rule to mark scope cuts [OPEN for Muath].
  - Fix: Move `learner/export`, `learner/delete`, `deletion_ledger` and the retention queries to week 3 (they need only `events`, `media_objects` and the roles, all week-1 work); keep week 5 for after-pass integration, claims and the end-to-end run. Tag the rebuild-to-P1 and research-set-to-P1 cuts [OPEN for Muath].
- **major** (4.3.2 `structure_acc_14d` view (`where observed_at > now() - interval '14 days'`) used by the 4.6 lifecycle rules, and 3.3 flow D): The 14-day window is anchored to wall-clock `now()`, but D14 and 4.6 say the map is recomputed by replaying observations in time order on a rebuild. During a replay the view returns the window as of today, not as of the replayed session, so `taking → stable` transitions, `stable_at`, `recheck_due_at` and the resulting claims come out different from the live run, which is exactly the non-determinism the rebuild design promises to avoid.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.5 (rebuild must reproduce the stores); PRD 10.6 (stable over 14 days or more); D8 'windows must be recomputable after a rebuild'.
  - Fix: Replace the view with a SQL function `structure_acc(learner_id, structure_id, as_of timestamptz)` and pass the replay time from `structures.step(..., now)`; keep the view only as a convenience over the function with `as_of = now()`.
- **minor** (4.7.2 ladder table (intake card → `usable`) versus the example row in 4.3.6 (`confidence: ground_truth, confirmed_by: intake_card`) and the week-3 plan ('intake facts as ground_truth via intake_card')): The document contradicts itself on what an intake card left standing yields. PRD 8.4.2 gives ground truth only to facts the learner edited; a card left standing is 'left unedited on a notebook card the learner viewed' which is `usable`.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.4.2.
  - Fix: Make the example row and the week-3 line say `usable`; an intake card the learner edits with ✎ is `ground_truth`.
- **minor** (4.7.3 ('the Sunday ending the ISO week of said_at (Saudi weeks start Sunday ...)')): An ISO week ends on Sunday and starts Monday; a Saudi work week starts Sunday and ends Saturday. The sentence names both and they disagree, so 'that week' (PRD 5.1) is undefined by one day in each direction.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.1 'sensitive topics only if the learner raised them in that week'; ISO 8601 week definition.
  - Fix: Define the week in the learner's local calendar as Sunday 00:00 to Saturday 23:59 and store `sensitive_usable_until` from that; drop the word ISO.
- **minor** (4.9 `briefs` cache keyed by (learner_id, role, learner_model_version, scope_id)): Several volatile parts of a brief depend on the date, not on the version: `facts_usable` (expiry, `sensitive_usable_until`), `follow_ups_due`, the due list (next drop time). A host or Salma brief compiled yesterday at version n is served today at version n with an expired fact or a sensitive fact past its week, which the editor's check 4 would have caught for episodes but no gate catches for the live host.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.4.3, 8.4.4; D12 'a new version invalidates by construction' is only true for version-dependent content.
  - Fix: Include the local date (or the next drop time) in `scope_id` for roles with date-dependent tails (teacher, writer, host, chat_host), or recompile the volatile tail on every read and cache only the stable prefix.
- **minor** (4.1.2 partitions (`events_2026_11` onward) and 7 week 1 ('Import the 15 Milestone 0 learner files and Scribe transcripts as source=import events') plus 4.1.3 ('reject ids more than 7 days old')): Milestone 0 runs 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-01, so imported events carry October timestamps: there is no `events_2026_10` partition and the 7-day age rule rejects them. The week-1 test plan cannot run as written.
  - Evidence: PRD 19.1 dates; the DDL in 4.1.2; rule in 4.1.3.
  - Fix: Create `events_2026_10` in the baseline migration and exempt `source = 'import'` from the age check (UUIDv7 ids minted at import time, `occurred_at` from the kit).
- **minor** (4.6 `attempting → taking` rule ('two observations correct and not prompted and clean in two different sessions')): PRD 10.6 requires the correct, unprompted use to be 'in an obligatory context'; the rule does not say `obligatory = true`, so a correct non-obligatory use (the form appeared where it was optional) would count.
  - Evidence: PRD 10.6 'Taking: Correct and unprompted in an obligatory context in 2 different sessions'.
  - Fix: Add `obligatory` to the predicate, and to the `examples` selection.
- **minor** (4.3.9 notebook_counts ('if a rebuild would lower a count ... the UI keeps the higher value and the difference is logged')): Showing a number the system knows is too high contradicts trust promise 1 ('Never lie'). PRD 12.3 says the count is cumulative and never reset, which is about not punishing the learner, not about preserving a wrong figure.
  - Evidence: PRD 1.3 #1; PRD 12.3 Hours.
  - Fix: Treat a lowered count like an invalidated claim: correct it and have Salma say so once in the next proof (the same `claim_invalidations` mechanism), rather than freezing the wrong value.
- **minor** (4.14 PII table ('Learner speech and writing: allowed in Langfuse prompts (processor approval ...)') and section 10 #3): The topology sends prompt and completion text to Langfuse only when `consents.data_use` is true and after the name scrub; the document states the text is allowed without the consent condition, which reads as a watering-down of trust promise 8 and disagrees with the sibling document it builds on. Separately, section 10 #3 names OpenAI as the embedding vendor by the topology's default while the topology's processor register excludes OpenAI.
  - Evidence: PRD 1.3 #8; topology 3.2 Langfuse row and section 9 item 6 ('excludes OpenAI').
  - Fix: State the consent gate in the PII table; make the `conversation_chunks` embedding vendor an [OPEN for Muath] with the approved-processor list, and note that `model_io` holds the text in-region regardless.
- **minor** (4.7.1 `QUOTE_SOURCES` includes `onboarding_answer` while 'a quote from a streaming call_turn is never accepted'): Onboarding A3 is a live Salma conversation in the call-worker; a voice onboarding answer is streaming recognizer text, which PRD 10.2 and the assessment lens say smooths learner errors. Accepting it as a verbatim quote source while rejecting streaming call turns is inconsistent, and PRD 8.4.1 names only the verbatim transcript and chat.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.4.1, 10.2.1; assessment-progress lens 'speech-to-text quietly hides the errors'.
  - Fix: Require `call_turn_verbatim` for voice onboarding turns (the worker already uploads A3 audio), or accept the card text the learner confirmed on screen as the quote with `confirmed_by = 'intake_card'`, and say which.
- **minor** (4.12.1 Export step 6 ('AES-256 zip with a 20-character passphrase')): Python's standard `zipfile` cannot write encrypted archives at all; AES zip needs `pyzipper` (or 7z). The step as written will not build in week 5 without a dependency decision, and AES-zip support in the Files app on iOS is limited, which matters for a mobile-first learner opening the export on a phone.
  - Evidence: CPython `zipfile` documentation: encryption is not supported for writing.
  - Fix: Name the library (`pyzipper`, WinZip AES) and the test that the archive opens on iOS Files and Android; or pick the alternative in section 10 #7 (24-hour presigned link, no passphrase) and say so.
- **minor** (4.1.5 ('Months older than 24 months may be moved to cheaper storage later') versus topology ('cold partitions older than 90 days compacted to Parquet in S3 (data domain)')): The two documents assign this domain different retention mechanics for the same table, and neither says how export (`events.ndjson`) and rebuilds read partitions that have left Postgres.
  - Evidence: Topology section 6 capacity table row 'Events per day'; PRD 12.5.4 and 12.5.7.
  - Fix: Rule on one number here, and state that export and rebuild read from the detached location (or that nothing is detached in the beta) in section 9.
- **minor** (4.1.1 payload rules and 4.13 migrations): Event payloads have no `schema_version`; 'fields beyond the schema are kept' handles additions, but a renamed or re-typed field means a rebuild replaying two years of events needs versioned readers, and nothing says where those live.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.1.1 (whole model rebuildable from the log); PRD 8.5.
  - Fix: Add `schema_version smallint` to every payload (or to the `events` row) written by the registry, and a rule that readers in `forone.learner.events` keep an upgrader per type and version.
- **minor** (4.12.4 research set ('pseudonym = hmac_sha256(server_secret, learner_id)'; 4.14 'sensitive classes ... never in the research set')): HMAC with a server secret is pseudonymization, not anonymization (PRD 12.5.5 says 'anonymized'); the rows stay re-linkable, which D18 relies on. And the transcripts in `turns` carry the learner's sensitive utterances verbatim, so the PII table's claim that sensitive classes are never in the research set is true only of `life_facts` rows, not of the text.
  - Evidence: PRD 12.5.5; PRD 1.3 #8.
  - Fix: Call it pseudonymized, send the definition to domain 07 to confirm the consent copy, and either strip turns containing confirmed sensitive facts' quotes from the research rows or drop the claim from the PII table.
- **minor** (3.3 flow A ('computes whether the day just became active ... play (≥ 50% of duration)')): `play` carries only `position_ms`; 'half an episode played' needs the union of play intervals from `play`/`pause`/`seek`, which can arrive across several batches and out of order from the offline queue. The rule for computing it (per batch? on every append? what counts after a seek?) is not written, and it drives both the allowance (PRD 3.6) and the hours count (PRD 12.3).
  - Evidence: PRD 3.6.1, 12.3 Hours.
  - Fix: Specify a `playback_intervals(session_id)` function over the deduplicated rows (merge intervals, cap at duration) and run the active-day check after each append for the session; store `active_day_marked_at` only once.
- **minor** (4.5.3 and 4.15 ('weights = FSRS-4.5 defaults', `scheduler: fsrs-4.5/w-default`)): Current py-fsrs releases implement FSRS-5/6 (19 to 21 parameters) with a different decay; the document pins 'FSRS-4.5' defaults while calling the current `Scheduler(desired_retention=...)` API. One of the two is wrong, and `code_versions.scheduler` will record a label that does not match the installed library.
  - Evidence: PRD 9.4.1 'FSRS-style scheduler (code)'; py-fsrs release history.
  - Fix: Pin the py-fsrs version in `code_versions.scheduler` (for example `py-fsrs==X.Y/default-params`) and let the library compute retrievability and intervals.

Missing:
- An event (or table) that records the analyst's and memory keeper's validated proposals per session (the AfterPassResult), without which PRD 8.5 rebuilds cannot replay anything model-produced and PRD 8.1's 'log is the source of truth' is only half true.
- Any treatment of pronunciation scores (PRD 10.3: say-it-once and shadow lines are scored internally, never shown): no store, no AfterPassResult field, no event payload carries a score.
- A rule that export and delete remain available while `learners.status = 'trial_ended'` for the 90 days (PRD 12.5.9); 4.12.2 step 1 returns 410 for `deleting` only, but nothing says the 410 is not applied to `trial_ended`.
- The per-learner 'delete all recordings' control (PRD 12.4) as a flow distinct from revoking consent: which `media_objects` kinds it covers, whether `kept_explicitly` recordings go too, and what the notebook's 'Words you own' clips show afterwards.
- A statement of what the learner sees for 'See all my data' (PRD 12.4, trust promise 5) beyond the notebook: whether the export is the only 'see', or an in-app view of the nine stores exists.
- Cross-domain conflicts the document should list but does not: the event column names (`client_event_id`/`received_at` in the topology vs `event_id`/`recorded_at` here), the 24 h skew guard in the topology vs 300 s here, and the voice key layout (`learners/{lid}/.../{ephemeral/}turn-n.ogg` in the topology vs `{learner_id}/ephemeral/calls/...` here).
- How the rule (c) 'viewed unedited' promotion fires when no after-pass runs for days: 'apply_learner_edit handles the promotion when the visit's last event is older than 10 minutes, or the next after-pass does' names no trigger.
- The retention and ownership of `conversation_chunks` text under voice-storage-off: chunks must be built from verbatim text (callbacks are checked by verify_quote against first_event_id..last_event_id), which is not stated.
- A PRD 10.11 check that `rater_pin` in `code_versions` matches `vendors.yaml pinned_until` for the window a claim was made in, so a rebuild during a window cannot mix rater versions.
- A rule for events arriving after a learner's deletion started (worker spool, PWA offline queue replay up to 24 h): the document discards the worker spool but says nothing about `POST /v1/events` from a revoked session beyond 410, nor about rows that land between the purge and the ledger.

Strengths:
- One append-only `events` table with a trigger guard and revoked UPDATE/DELETE, plus `call_turn_verbatim` as a second event instead of an update, is the right reading of PRD 8.1 and keeps the verbatim re-transcription requirement honest; the topology has already accepted D2 and D5.
- A per-learner integer `learner_model_version` recorded with the high-water seq and the code versions, carried on briefs, CallMeta, model-produced events and claims, implements PRD 14.3's tracing requirement precisely and makes a bad episode traceable to its model state.
- Life facts as rows with `quote_event_id`, a database quote check (`verify_quote`), the three-rung ladder, sensitivity with in-session confirmation, shelf life and supersession implement PRD 8.4 and the personalization lens's evidence (LongMemEval, HaluMem) directly, and the editor's check 4 becoming one SQL predicate is exactly the kind of code-not-prompt enforcement the trust contract needs.
- `structure_observations` as per-turn evidence rows with a computed accuracy window, and `claims` plus `claim_invalidations` so a rebuild never silently retracts a shown claim, give the claim gate's 'openable' rule (PRD 10.10 #5) and PRD 8.5 a concrete home.
- Beta(a,b) per unit and surface with a frequency prior seeded by the word check gives the fitter p(understood) and every number a range for free, which is what PRD 8.3 ('every estimate carries a range') and the assessment lens asked for.
- The rejected-alternatives table is specific and mostly right for a two-person team: no Kafka, no document database, no HNSW across learners, no backup scrubbing.

## review:conformance:05-assessment-and-after-session

Verdict: needs-revision

- **major** (3.2 Diagram B and 4.5.6 `skill_answer()` ("'where do I stand' reads `skill_calibration`, not claims") versus D10 ("claims ... is the only input of ... 'where do I stand'")): The document contradicts itself and waters down law 12: the band-range answer to "where do I stand" bypasses the `claims` table and the five rules entirely, so it has no `claim_evidence` row and nothing to open under "how we know". It also adds a requirement the PRD does not have (external-test r >= 0.5 on >= 20 learners), which cannot exist before week 8 of the beta (2027-02), so a P0 feature (10.8 is untagged) is silently removed from the beta.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.10: "Every sentence about the learner's progress, in the proof, the finale, notes, a host line or 'where do I stand', must pass all five rules"; 10.8.3 conditions the answer only on the 10.11 bar (QWK about 0.7); 1.2 law 12; Part 17 excludes only "skills not yet calibrated".
  - Fix: Make the skill answer a claim kind (`skill_range`) written to `claims` by the gate with rules 4 and 5 applied (clean evidence: the sessions behind the estimate; openable: words owned, the tape, the two most recent comparable segments) and rules 1 to 3 marked n/a by kind. Keep the calibration precondition as the PRD states it (rater QWK >= 0.7 per skill); if the external-test correlation is wanted as an additional bar, tag it [OPEN for Muath] because it removes the feature from the beta.
- **major** (4.7.1 `select_cards`, step 1 ("a did_it / first_ever candidate that passes the gate's clean+openable rules") on `analyst.fast` output): In stage 1 the edit classifier and the obligatory-context detector (stage 2 code) have not run, so a structure `first_ever` claim ("first clean correct unprompted context") shown in the notes within 90 s rests on the Haiku analyst's `needed[].realized` judgment alone. That is a model deciding a claim, against the document's own D3/D4 and PRD 8.1.2, and the claim is marked `shown` before code ever verifies it.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 8.1 rule 2 "Models propose, code decides"; 10.2 step 3 (the classifier computes obligatory-context use); 10.10 rule 4; the document's 4.3 says "a proposed context survives only if the rule has a detector and one of its cues is in the span", which stage 1 never runs.
  - Fix: In stage 1 restrict "New for you" to `did_it` (can-do with an exact quote) and lemma-level word `first_ever` (a code check against `produced_before`), or run the structure's `detector` and `cue_present` on the single quoted span in stage 1 (it is spaCy, under 100 ms) before a structure `first_ever` can be a card. State which.
- **major** (4.8.1 `did_it` row ("Minimum history: none") and 4.8.2 (`did_it` returns `passed` after rules 4 and 5)): `did_it` has no "not done before" check, yet it is exempt from rule 3 (Repeated) as if it were a first-ever event. The same can-do ("you disagreed with Karim and gave a reason") can be claimed in the notes and the proof every week, which breaks the notes contract ("one real thing the learner did that they had not done before") and stretches the PRD's narrow exemption for first-ever events to any single moment.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.5.2.1 "one real thing the learner did that they had not done before"; 10.10 rule 3 "at least two data points point the same way, except first-ever events"; 2.2 A8 "one real thing he did that is not easy".
  - Fix: Require `no passed or shown claim with the same can_do_id for this learner` (make `did_it` a first-ever event by construction), or merge it into `first_ever` with `subject={can_do_id}`. Add `claims.did_it.once_per_can_do = true` to 4.8.4.
- **major** (4.7.1 polish card (`pool = [e for e in a.edits if e.clean and not e.self_corrected and e.structure_id]`; `on_target*4` is only a score)): The polish card may be an error on any structure when no on-target error exists, including a structure the learner vetoed in "working on". The PRD restricts the polish card to errors on active targets, and vetoed items leave targeting unless they break meaning. The margin-note rule in 4.7.5 gets this right; the notes do not.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.5.2.2 "Chosen from errors on active targets, preferring confident, repeated errors"; 9.2.5 veto "leaves targeting unless it breaks meaning"; 9.3 last row; 10.12 "Right after the call ... polish" as a correction layer on targets.
  - Fix: Restrict `pool` to edits whose `structure_id` is in `active_target_ids` or `working_on_items` (source teacher or learner, not vetoed), plus patterns with `blurred_share > 0.5`; if the pool is empty, no polish card (the PRD says "each only when real evidence exists"). Keep `polish_score` for ranking inside that pool.
- **major** (3.3 events consumed (`chat.closed`, `chat.learner_message`, `voicenote.received`) and 3.4 (`analyst.full` runs at 0:46 on "all learner turns plus chat so far")): The chat opens after the call ("within 30 seconds of the call ending") and runs for 3 to 5 host messages; round two [P1] and the small-door voice note can come hours later. The pass runs once at `call.ended`, so chat messages, round-two turns and small-door notes are never fed to the analyst, classifier, updater or memory keeper. `chat.closed` and `voicenote.received` are listed as consumed but nothing in the document runs on them. Chat is the PRD's cleanest grammar evidence and the only source of the writing estimate; round two is the designated like-for-like fluency sample.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.2 step 2 "for each learner turn and chat message"; 5.6.2 chat timing; 10.10 rule 4 "grammar claims from chat text or high-confidence speech"; 5.4.9 round two "a like-for-like retell sample for fluency"; 4 rule 7 "Its partial evidence still feeds assessment"; the document's own 4.5.6 writing observation needs >= 60 chat words.
  - Fix: Define `session/after-pass` as runnable per evidence event: a full run on `call.ended` (main or round two, `kind` already carries it) and a text-only run on `chat.closed` and `voicenote.received` (analyst on the new messages only, classifier, updater, memory keeper, claims candidates; no notes stage). Give each run its own cost line (about $0.003) and say that `learner_model_version` bumps on each; the teacher's nightly run picks up the chat evidence.
- **major** (4.8.2 gate (`cal = metric_calibration.get(c.metric, c.after.task_type)`) and 4.8.1 kinds `accuracy_change`, `accuracy_fell_while_trying`, `talkback_falling`): Standard errors come only from test-retest pairs of comparable segments (retell, round two, recording tasks). Obligatory-context accuracy and errors per 100 words are measured on all clean call turns and chat (4.4), and talkback asks are counts per call; none has a `task_type` with test-retest pairs, so under the code as written these three kinds fail with `no_standard_error` forever. The P1 "full gate on fluency and accuracy claims" (11.1.8) and the PRD's example of the honest worse-week sentence cannot ship.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.10 rule 2 and the paragraph after it; 11.1.8 "The full gate on fluency and accuracy claims follows once standard errors exist [P1]"; 10.3 accuracy "always read next to what was attempted"; 5.4.5.5 falling talkback use is progress evidence.
  - Fix: Decide and tag: (a) accuracy and talkback claims use a count-based standard error (binomial SE on correct/contexts, Poisson on asks per call) with the same 2x rule, recorded in `metric_calibration` with `source='analytic'`, or (b) accuracy claims are made only on recording and retell segments where test-retest pairs exist. Add `task_type='call_turns'` and `'chat'` rows and the formula to 4.8.2 and the thresholds table.
- **major** (Section 5 last paragraph (drop order: margin note, avoidance, external-test page, rebuild automation) and Section 7 Beta row ("avoidance weekly job ... P1")): Avoidance (10.7, 9.3 "Avoided" treatment) and the margin note (5.6.6) carry no priority tag in the PRD and are therefore P0; the document moves avoidance to the beta period as P1 and lists the margin note as the first cut, with neither marked [OPEN for Muath]. A scope cut of a P0 item is the owner's call.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 Part 0 tags: "A feature in Parts 2 to 14 with no priority tag is [P0]"; 10.7; 9.3; 5.6.6; 10.12 lists the margin note as a correction layer.
  - Fix: Either keep avoidance in build week 5 (it is 20 lines of code once the structure map exists) and the margin note in week 4, or tag both cuts [OPEN for Muath] with the cost of keeping them (about half a day each). The drop order should start with items the PRD already tags P1 (external test page, rebuild automation, forms C and D).
- **major** (3.4 stage-1 table ("88 worst") and the 60 s step timeout): The "worst allowed" column sums to 3 + 20 + 25 + 1 + 25 + 13 + 10 + 1 = 98 s, not 88, so the design's own worst case misses the 90 s budget; the three model calls alone can take 25 + 25 + 12 = 62 s against a 60 s step timeout, so the retry path can never complete. The 90 s clock also starts at `call.ended` arrival rather than at the call's end (upload of the last turn, `call-worker` to `api` to Inngest), which eats several seconds the table does not show.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.5.1 "Ready within 90 seconds of the call ending"; Part 15 "Salma's notes after a call: under 90 s"; the document's own figures.
  - Fix: Budget by wall clock from the `call.ended` timestamp in the payload: skip the `notes.write` retry when t > 50 s, skip the polish-line TTS when t > 70 s (render it after `notes.ready` and attach by update), and cap `analyst.fast` at 20 s. Restate the worst case with the corrected sum and keep the degraded path as the guarantee.
- **major** (4.9 step 1 ("Audio stays only where voice storage consent exists") and `calibration_samples.audio_ref` in 4.16; Section 9 assumption 6): Voice-storage consent is "Keep my recordings so I can hear my progress (only for you, delete any time)"; the data-use switch covers anonymized transcripts, no audio. Putting a learner's audio into a calibration set read by a paid tagger is covered by neither consent. The rater and the analyst are designed on transcripts, so the audio is not needed.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.5.5 "anonymized transcripts (no audio) may be used to check and improve the teacher"; 1.3 promise 8; 2.2 A9 consent wording; 12.6 "no cross-learner data paths except ... anonymized, opt-in transcripts".
  - Fix: Drop `audio_ref` from `calibration_samples`; calibration is text plus `clean_spans` and word timings only. If an audio calibration set is wanted for the recognizer (10.14), it comes from Milestone 0 clips with their own consent, stored separately and tagged [OPEN for Muath].
- **major** (4.6 rule 2 (ladder: new fact → `said_once`, never spoken) and the input line "the onboarding fact cards (session 1 only)"): Facts the learner saw as cards in A3 (with ✎ and ✕) and left untouched enter at `said_once`, which is never spoken. The day-2 episode must carry a verbatim callback from onboarding and Today's host line must always be a confirmed memory, so with this ladder the day-2 callback and the first host line are impossible, and the week-1 episodes have no usable facts until a second session repeats one.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 2.3 "The day-2 episode carries at least one callback sourced verbatim from something said in onboarding"; 3.4 "One line from a host above it, always a real, confirmed memory"; 8.4.2 "left unedited on a notebook card the learner viewed: usable"; 2.2 A3 cards with ✎ and ✕.
  - Fix: State that an A3 fact card shown to the learner and not removed before A9 counts as "viewed and left unedited" and enters at `usable` at account creation (the card is the notebook view); a card edited with ✎ enters at `ground_truth`. Sensitive-class cards still need an explicit confirmation.
- **major** (4.5.4 `update_patterns` (`if not e.clean or e.self_corrected or e.structure_id is None: record_slip`) and 4.3 fallback (`OTHER: word_choice`, `structure_id=None`)): Word-choice, collocation and preposition-with-verb errors mostly match no structure rule and fall back to `structure_id=None`, so they are recorded as slips forever and can never reach 3-across-2, never become a pattern, never be targeted, and never become a `mistake_gone` claim. The PRD's item-based treatment (recast, then the item returns on schedule) therefore has no input.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 9.3 "Attempting, item-based (word choice, collocation)" treatment; 9.2.6 pattern rule; 10.5; 10.4.2 word choice is one of the twelve categories used in the proof.
  - Fix: Key item-based patterns on `(category, lemma_or_chunk, error_type)` when `structure_id is None` and the edit's head lemma is identifiable (errant `R:OTHER`, `R:PREP` with a governing verb, `R:VERB` word choice); hand them to the teacher as `item_patterns` beside `error_profile`, with the same 3-across-2 rule. Keep true unclassifiables as slips.
- **major** (Section 7 week 1 row (structure_rules.yaml v1 with about 120 structures, errant plus spaCy in the worker image, classifier with unit tests, M0 import, measures, the 10.14 test, the claims module, every migration in 4.16) against PRD 19.2 week 1): PRD week 1 already assigns vendor tests, the event log and learner-model schema, the lexicon and scheduler, the package skeleton and cost tracing to the same one or two engineers across nine domains; this domain's week 1 alone is two to three engineer-weeks (a rule table of 120 hand-written structure matchers on ERRANT output is the long pole). The document never states its own P0 minimum for the team as it is; its "too much for the team" paragraph cuts P1 items only after week 5 slips.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 19.2 week table; the task's team-reality rule; 10.4.1 "a few hundred items" is the inventory, but claims only need the claim-feeding set (10.11.2).
  - Fix: Define the beta minimum now: `structure_rules.yaml` v1 with the claim-feeding set only (the about 40 B1 core structures in `invited_structures`), everything else classified to a category by the errant fallback; `analyst` and the gate in week 2; measures and `metric_calibration` in week 3; the rater, `recording/rate` and the proof in week 5. Move the few-hundred-row inventory to the beta period and say so in the exit criteria.
- **major** (4.8.3, 4.11 and 4.16 (`text_ar text not null -- rendered from the kind's template by code; the only sentence any surface may speak`)): The Arabic claim templates per kind are the only progress sentences the learner ever hears and the object of the monthly false-claim audit and the exit criterion ("zero progress sentences anywhere that do not resolve to a claims row"), yet none is given. Without them the law scan (no percentages, no levels) cannot be checked against `accuracy_change` ("errors per 100 words" reads as a rate) or `fluency_change`, and the proof producer's "paste verbatim" instruction has nothing concrete to test.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 1.2 law 1 (no percentages); 11.1.3.1 and 11.2.2 "measured differences in plain words"; 10.3 accuracy "always read next to what was attempted"; 16.1 false-claim audit.
  - Fix: Add a table of the ten `text_ar` templates with their slots (the Milestone 0 prompt F sentence "في التسجيل الأول وقفت ٦ مرات وسط الجملة، هالمرة مرتين" is the model), one per kind and direction, and state that the law scan runs on the rendered template set in CI.
- **minor** (4.10 (forms A to D rotation: "season 1 'then' = A or B ..., 'now' = the other; season 2 uses C and D") and `recordings.unique(learner_id, season_number, kind)`): The schema and rotation imply two recordings per season (a 'then' and a 'now'), while the PRD has one recording in week 1 and then one per finale, each finale's recording serving as the next comparison's 'then'. Two per season doubles what the learner is asked to record and makes the 11.3 longer windows (1 month vs 3 months) ambiguous.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.9.2.3 "The first 'then' is offered after session 2 ... Afterwards: every season finale"; glossary "the same 3-minute speaking task every 4 weeks"; 11.3.
  - Fix: Model `recordings` as one row per sitting (`sitting_n`, `season_number`, `form`) with forms cycling A, B, C, D, A so consecutive sittings never share a form; `generate_then_and_now()` compares sitting n with n-1 (and later n-3 for the 3-month window).
- **minor** (4.8.2 rule 3 comment ("for then_and_now, where 'repeated' is met by rule: two recordings are two points")): Two recordings are the before and the after, not two points pointing the same way; this is a reinterpretation of rule 3 rather than an application of it, and it is not tagged as a [DECISION].
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.10 rule 3; Milestone 0 kit 06-then-and-now.md gate rule 2 (both raters agree and the transcript measure moves the same way).
  - Fix: Satisfy Repeated for `then_and_now` the way the pilot kit does: the code metric and the pinned rater's band (or the retell and the opinion task) must move the same way; tag it [DECISION, PRD-v2 10.10 rule 3].
- **minor** (4.7.3 law scan ("any digit outside the header and the quote") reused in 4.11.1 ("same scanner as notes")): The proof legitimately contains numbers in claim sentences ("40 seconds", "eleven times") and in the cumulative counts, so the notes scanner as described would reject every proof script; the whitelist is implied by the prompt but not by the scanner.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 1.2 law 1 allows counts that only grow; 11.1.4 cumulative counts; the document's 4.11.3 prompt.
  - Fix: State the proof scanner's whitelist: digits inside pasted claim sentences (matched by string equality with `claims.text_ar`) and inside the three cumulative-count slots; any other digit fails.
- **minor** (4.7.5 and `margin_notes ... unique (chat_id)`): The PRD's chat is one long thread per host across days; if `chat_id` is that thread, the unique constraint allows one margin note per host for the learner's lifetime. The document assumes a per-session `chat.closed` but does not define `chat_id`.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.6.1 "One long thread per host, across days"; 5.6.6 "at most one per chat".
  - Fix: Define `chat_id` as the per-session exchange (opener to closer) inside the host thread, or change the constraint to `unique (session_id, host)`.
- **minor** (3.3 (`learner.consent_changed` consumed) with no handler; 4.7.4 `notes.audio_refs`; `claim_evidence.media_object_id`): Revoking voice consent deletes all stored audio within 24 h, but the document does not say what happens to shown claims whose evidence audio disappears, to notes' audio refs, or to `test_retest_pairs`/`calibration_samples` rows for a learner who turns data use off or deletes the account.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.5.3, 12.5.5, 12.5.8; 10.10 rule 5 (openable).
  - Fix: Add a `consent/apply` step in this domain: on voice revocation, `claim_evidence` rows keep quote and timings with `media_object_id=null` and "how we know" renders text; on data-use off or deletion, `calibration_samples` and `test_retest_pairs` rows for that `learner_ref` are removed or kept only if already anonymized under the security domain's rule (state which, tag [ASSUMPTION]).
- **minor** (4.5.6 ("A recording re-anchors speaking with R = 64 using the pinned rater's band") and 4.9 (calibration cannot reach 150 to 200 samples before 2026-12-07)): In the beta the week-1 'then' recordings are rated by a rater whose QWK bar has not been measured (Milestone 0 yields at most about 60 rated samples), yet its band re-anchors the speaking estimate with the lowest observation noise in the system. This is a planning signal, so it is allowed to be noisy, but the document presents the re-anchor as the authoritative correction.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.11.2 and 10.11.3; 10.1 (planning signals may be noisy, claims may not); 10.8.1.
  - Fix: Use `R = 64` only while `calibration_runs.passed = true` for the rater; otherwise re-anchor with the code measures alone at `R = 100` and record `band` as advisory.
- **minor** (4.6 rule 3 (sensitive facts "never usable for a brief outside the session week it was raised") and "stored only if the learner confirms (ladder step 2 by chat or notebook)"): The PRD says a sensitive fact is used only in the session where it was raised and stored only if confirmed; the document widens to the week (following 5.1's "that week") and stores it as `said_once` pending notebook confirmation, which means it exists in the notebook before confirmation. Both readings are defensible but the choice is untagged.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 8.4.3; 5.1 "sensitive topics only if the learner raised them in that week"; 1.3 promise 6.
  - Fix: Tag the week reading [DECISION, PRD-v2 5.1 over 8.4.3] and state that a sensitive candidate is held in a `pending_confirmation` state that is not shown as "I think..." and expires in 7 days if unconfirmed.
- **minor** (4.4 comparable segments (`measurable` is size-based only) and 4.8.2 rule 4 (clean check applies to `GRAMMAR_KINDS` only)): Fluency and complexity claims can be built on segments with a low clean share, where the recognizer hallucinated near long pauses or dropped words, which moves speech rate and MLR directly.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.14 (hallucinations near long pauses); 10.10 rule 4 spirit; 5.4.11 low-confidence evidence produces no claims.
  - Fix: Add `measurable = size rule and clean_share >= asr.segment_clean_min (placeholder 0.8)` and record the failing reason.
- **minor** (4.15 (`rater.pinned_until` 2026-12-31) versus Open item 7 ("this document assumes" 2027-01-31)): The configuration default contradicts the assumption the design relies on; as configured, the first finales on 2027-01-03 cross a window boundary and get no then-and-now sentence.
  - Evidence: The document's own 4.10 and 10.7; PRD-v2 11.2.1 (finale on or after day 28).
  - Fix: Set the default to 2027-01-31 and keep the open item for Muath to confirm cohort-aligned windows.
- **minor** (4.7.2 notes prompt ("قبل ما تنزل، كيف تصلّحها؟")): The try-first line is a learning-science lens recommendation that the PRD did not adopt into 5.5.2.2 (own sentence, then natural version, then say it once); it is added untagged. The PRD says lens items not adopted are left out, not quietly added.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 0.1 last paragraph; 5.5.2.2; learning-science lens section 3.
  - Fix: Tag it [DECISION] with the lens citation and make it a package string the owner can remove, or drop it.
- **minor** (4.1 rules ("`mode = voice_notes` turns are marked `low_confidence_source=true`") and gate rule 4): The blanket rule treats every voice note as weak-link audio, but the small-door voice note (3.7) and say-it-once are deliberate recordings on a normal link; their evidence is discarded for claims without reason.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 3.7; 5.4.10 (the weak-link fallback is the only voice-note mode the PRD calls low confidence); 10.2.1 lists small-door notes as verbatim inputs.
  - Fix: Set `low_confidence_source` only for turns produced under the 5.4.10 fallback (03's `mode = voice_notes` on a failed live call); small-door and say-it-once uploads follow the normal confidence filter.
- **minor** (4.4 comprehension measures and 4.16 `session_summaries`): The "how much did you follow?" answer (one tap after some episodes) is a PRD comprehension measure and a 16.2 health metric but appears nowhere in the measures table or the session summary.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.3 Comprehension row; 16.2 "'How much did you follow?' ... against predicted coverage".
  - Fix: Add `followed_answer smallint` to `session_summaries` from the client event and list it under comprehension.
- **minor** (3.3 APIs provided): The live talkback role needs "the learner's produced-word history (for word-or-hint)" from the word bank, and the Salma brief needs active targets with evidence; this domain owns both but exposes no API for them, so 03 will read the store directly or guess.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.4.5.4; 8.6 Salma row.
  - Fix: Add `produced_before(learner_id) -> set[str]` and `active_targets_with_evidence(learner_id)` to `forone/assessment/api.py`, or state that the data domain's brief compiler serves them.
- **minor** (4.10 ("forms C and D written in build week 2 to the same template") and Open item 8): The Milestone 0 template's opinion task is about 2 minutes; the PRD changed it to 60 to 90 seconds. Forms C and D built "to the same template" would inherit the longer task, and forms A and B need the shorter limit too.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.9.2.1 "[NEW: 90-second opinion rather than 2 minutes]"; milestone-0/06-then-and-now.md.
  - Fix: State that the package's task bank uses the PRD lengths (retell 60 to 90 s, opinion 60 to 90 s, 8 sentences) and that the Milestone 0 week-0/week-3 pairs feed `test_retest_pairs` with `source=m0` and a flag for the longer opinion task.
- **minor** (4.11.4 season finale and 4.11.2 honest sentences): The finale's honest fallback when both recordings exist but no difference passes ("too early to tell"; the pilot's "this is your starting line") is not specified; only the skipped-recording case is. The finale's "the moment Salma liked most" (11.2.2) is also unaddressed, and it is a sentence about the learner that should be drawn from passed `did_it` claims.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.10 "When nothing passes: the honest thing is said instead"; 11.2.2; milestone-0/06-then-and-now.md prompt F.
  - Fix: Add a `finale_differences()` result flag `nothing_passed=true` with the package sentence, and define "the moment Salma liked most" as the highest-ranked passed `did_it` or `first_ever` claim of the season.

Missing:
- The ten Arabic claim templates (`text_ar` per kind and direction) that are the only progress sentences any surface may speak; without them the law scan, the audit and the exit criterion cannot be tested.
- A per-event run of the pass for chat (`chat.closed`), round two and the small-door voice note; today only `call.ended` triggers analysis, so chat and round-two evidence is lost.
- A standard-error source for accuracy and talkback claim kinds (analytic SE or restriction to recording segments); as written they fail with `no_standard_error` forever.
- Handling of `learner.consent_changed` (voice revocation, data-use off) and account deletion for this domain's tables: `claim_evidence` media refs, `notes.audio_refs`, `calibration_samples`, `test_retest_pairs`.
- A stated beta minimum for a one-to-two-engineer team: which structures are in `structure_rules.yaml` v1, and which P0 items (avoidance, margin note) stay versus go, tagged [OPEN for Muath].
- The onboarding path into the memory ladder (A3 cards shown and left → usable at A9) that PRD 2.3 and 3.4 require for the day-2 callback and Today's host line.
- The 2.2 starting estimate: the two English moments in A3 (comprehension line, production line) are not used for the first listening and speaking observations; only the word check and the first call are.
- Where the memory keeper's `out_in_the_world` rows are stored and how the proof and finale read them (PRD 11.2.2, 12.3 'Out in the world').
- Shelf lives for `relationship`, `money`, `religion`, `politics` and `other` fact types (8.4.4 lists only six).
- Who computes each learner's proof day (the weekday used most in week 1, else day 7) and how `proof.requested` carries it (11.1.1); `proof.default_day` alone does not cover it.
- Consent for importing a learner's DET results into `external_tests` (trust promise 6: only what the learner told the show).

Strengths:
- The claim gate as one pure module with a single `claims` table that every progress surface reads, pre-rendered claim sentences pasted by code into the proof producer's plan, and the `no_standard_error` failure as the honest default: this is the correct mechanical reading of law 12 and 10.10 and should be kept exactly.
- Per-turn verbatim transcription during the call (D1) is the only design that makes the 90 s notes budget reachable; the analyst validation step (exact-substring quote checks, 4-token edit cap, closed id lists, `analyst_validation_drop` metric) turns 'models propose, code decides' into testable code.
- ERRANT alignment plus a package rule table with `claim_eligible` flags gives per-tag precision that calibration can measure and pin, and the `assessment_version` stamp on every row with shadow-table rebuilds and `notice_pending` retractions implements 8.5 and 10.11.3 faithfully.
- `memory/correct` is a real propagation design (fact fixed at once, `facts.invalidated` fan-out, disputed quotes poisoning evidence, the 1-in-100 counter), and the decision not to re-run the memory keeper in rebuilds protects learner-governed facts.
- The failure-mode table and the cost section are concrete and the arithmetic checks against the cost domain's prices; the domain lands at about $0.07 against a $0.14 allowance with the STT line correctly identified as the only real risk.

## review:conformance:06-client-and-delivery

Verdict: needs-revision

- **major** (3.4 Data flows, flow A ("finds episode.m4a already in forone-media-v1 from the previous evening's today.changed") and 4.6 rule 1 ("the page (not the SW) runs cache.add()")): The under-2-second dropped-episode path is premised on a cache warm that the design can only perform while the page is in the foreground, but tomorrow's episode is normally produced overnight in batch after the pick, so on most mornings the episode became ready while the app was closed and the warm never ran. The common path is therefore the cold network stream the document files under failure modes, and the budget is sized on the exception.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 15 (dropped episode start under 2 s); PRD-v2 3.2.1 to 3.2.3 (the pipeline runs on batch pricing after the pick and must be ready before the drop time, so readiness typically lands while the learner sleeps); the document's own 4.8 says the SSE stream exists only for the foreground app and 4.6 rule 6 says Safari has no Background Sync.
  - Fix: Warm in the service worker's push handler: on a `kind: drop` payload carrying `episode_id` and `manifest_version`, `event.waitUntil(cache.add(...warm_assets))` before `showNotification` (both Chrome and iOS run the handler with the app closed; respect `saveData`). Also warm on `session_closed` the evening before and on the last foreground `today.changed`. Then state plainly that the cold path is the sized path and give its p95 on 4G from the week-3 device matrix, with the `play_start_ms {context: daily, source: network|blob}` split logged so the two paths are measured separately.
- **major** (4.10.1 A1 to A10 and 4.3 ControlsDoc / 4.2 `/controls` row): The eight promises of the trust contract are never shown. Onboarding A1 to A10 as specified here carries only the voice-storage sentence at A9, and the Controls sheet has no item for the promises; the document treats the contract as rules it obeys, not as content it must render.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 1.3: "Eight promises, shown in plain words at onboarding and in Controls." The brief lists the trust contract as a hard constraint.
  - Fix: Add a `promises` block to `OnboardingDoc` (eight lines from the language package, `text_shown_version`) rendered once between A8 and A9 as a single scroll (no checkbox, no gate), and a Controls row "What we promised" that opens the same eight lines; log `consent_change {kind: promises_shown, text_shown_version}` so the security domain can prove display.
- **major** (4.2 Screens and routes; 4.3 API contract (only `/chat/{host}` for `karim|mona`)): There is no surface for talking to Salma outside the onboarding call and the live-call talkback. The PRD routes most of the product through a sentence to Salma (settings by conversation, "what do you know about me?", "why do we say it like that?", "where do I stand", life updates, fact corrections, re-anchoring, "Not quite right? Tell Salma" under Why this episode). The document even assumes one exists (`controls.changed`: "a change made through Salma by voice") but defines no route, document or endpoint for it.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.4 ("Everything else is a sentence to Salma"); 7.3.2 (Salma's duties, including settings changes by conversation and "what do you know about me?"); 10.8.3 and 12.3 ("Where do I stand: not a page: asked of Salma"); 12.3 Why this episode ("Not quite right? Tell Salma"); 3.9.4 re-anchoring; 8.4.7 corrections.
  - Fix: Add `/chat/salma` as a third thread on the same `ChatDoc` shape (text and hold-to-talk through the typed Salma turn the topology already runs in `api`), reachable from the Notebook header, every "Tell Salma" affordance and Controls; `POST /chat/salma/messages {text|media, context: {episode_id?, fact_id?, surface}}`; replies arrive as `chat.message {host: salma}` on the stream; Salma-initiated changes land as `controls.changed` and `consent_change`. Mark as P0 and place it in week 4 next to the host chat since it is the same component.
- **major** (4.3 `ChatDoc` and `NotesDoc`; 4.2 `/chat/[host]` and `/notes/[sessionId]` rows): The week-1 asks are not representable in the client. The rhythm question (3 to 6 days plus 2 spare) at the end of the session-2 chat, the season goal proposed in the learner's words in session 2 or 3, and the gap note to the future self (text plus a 7/10/14-day gap) in session 3 or the first proof all need a Salma message inside a host thread with a typed answer, plus storage fields; none exists. `PUT /me` has no rhythm, `PUT /controls` has a bare `gap_note?` with no text or gap length, and no sheet composes the note.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 2.4 items 1 to 4; 3.9.2 (rhythm range set in session 2); 7.7.2 (season goal confirmed or reworded); 3.8.1 (gap note text, gap length 7/10/14 default 10, editable in Controls); 12.4 ("Gap note: edit, change the gap, or turn off"). All untagged, hence P0.
  - Fix: Add a `salma_ask` message kind to `ChatDoc` with `{ask_id, kind: rhythm|season_goal|gap_note, prompt_ar, options?}` and `POST /chat/{host}/messages {reply_to: ask_id, choice?|text}`; add `rhythm: {days_per_week: 3..6, spare: 2}` and `season_goal_text` to `PUT /me`; add a `/gapnote` sheet (text, gap chips 7/10/14, on/off) reached from the ask and from Controls, backed by `PUT /controls {gap_note: {enabled, text, gap_days}}`.
- **major** (4.10.1 A6 ("opens the player with the transcript open") versus 4.4.4 case 3 ("While streaming ... the transcript is folded regardless of the learner's default") and A6's "the pilot streams (4.4.4 case 3)"): The document contradicts itself on the pilot, and one branch contradicts the PRD: the pilot is a streaming kind, the streaming rule folds the transcript, but A6 must show the transcript with the Huh? and tap-a-word hint on first contact.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 2.2 A6: "The transcript is shown on the pilot (first contact); one gentle hint points at 'Huh?' and at tapping a word."
  - Fix: Change 4.4.4 case 3 so streaming degrades only the highlight granularity (sentence-level `data-active`, word taps still resolve through the index), not the fold; the fold stays governed by `player_defaults.transcript_open` with an explicit override `transcript_open: true` for `kind: pilot` in the manifest or `EpisodeDoc`. Say what 🔊 on a word does under `timing_quality: sentence` (play the sentence, not the word).
- **major** (4.3 API contract, `PUT /controls` row and the `/controls` route in 4.2): "Delete all recordings" is missing. Controls can flip `voice_storage` (which deletes within 24 h by 12.5.3) but the PRD lists a separate action that deletes stored recordings while keeping storage on, and no endpoint or event carries it.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.4: "Keep my recordings: on or off, and 'delete all recordings'"; 12.5.1; Part 12 untagged, hence P0.
  - Fix: Add `DELETE /v1/me/recordings` → 202, two-tap confirm in the sheet, appending `consent_change {kind: recordings_deleted}`; it clears `media_objects` of learner-voice kinds through the data domain's existing deletion step and leaves `consents.voice_storage` unchanged.
- **major** (7. Build plan, week 5 row (2026-11-30 to 12-06)): Week 5 asks one to two engineers for the whole Notebook, the then-and-now recording screen, the proof screen with "how we know", export and delete flows, trial-ended and day-7 states, the crisis card, the sample landing page, share page, waitlist and invites, an accessibility pass, Lighthouse and bundle work, and the end-to-end run with Milestone 0 learners, in the same week the other domains are also landing their week-5 items. That is not shippable by the stated team, and the document does not say so or give the cut; the topology already lists several of these as at risk.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 19.2 week 5 and the 2026-12-07 exit criterion ("every [P0] item works"); the brief's team reality (one to two engineers plus Muath); topology 01 §7 "P0 items at risk of slipping into the first beta week".
  - Fix: Pull the Notebook sections and the proof screen into week 4 (they are read-only document renderers and the NotesDoc fixtures exist from week 4), move the share page and the accessibility pass to beta week 1 as explicitly accepted slips, and ship the sample landing page as a static form plus the minimal player in week 3 (the waitlist must be collecting before invites start on 2026-12-07). State the resulting week-5 scope as: recording screen, export and delete, trial and payment states, crisis card, end-to-end run, and nothing else.
- **minor** (3.4 flow A ("Tap → notificationclick → ... play() starts from local bytes in under 2 s")): Read literally, the notification tap starts playback, which is autoplay on open; the Today screen in the ready state shows the cover and ▶ Play, and the 2 s budget is from the Play tap.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 3.4 ("Episode ready, not started: The cover and ▶ Play"); law 11 (no autoplay); 15.
  - Fix: Rewrite the flow so the tap opens Today with the hero ready and the learner presses Play; define `play_start_ms` as Play tap to first audible sample.
- **minor** (4.3 `TodayDoc` (`show.season.day`, `learner.trial_day`)): The home document carries two day counters that nothing in the PRD renders and that 3.9.3 and law 1 forbid showing; nothing says they are never rendered, and `trial_day` only exists to drive `payment.ask_now`, which the server already computes.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 1.2 law 1; 3.9.3 ("never shown as a counter, calendar, flame or record of missed days").
  - Fix: Drop `season.day`; replace `trial_day` with `payment.ask_now` and `learner.status` alone; add both field names to the law-1 lint rule's banned list so a future screen cannot bind them.
- **minor** (4.11 Bidirectional text (`<html lang="ar" dir="rtl"> once`) and the `_ar` fields throughout 4.3 (`message_ar`, `title_ar`, `text_ar`, `signature_ar`)): Language and direction are hard-coded in the client and in field names, while the PRD requires UI strings and text direction to come from the language package, built through the package mechanism for the first pair as well.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 13.1 and 13.2 ("the UI strings and text direction" are package content; "the first pair is built through the same mechanism to keep this honest").
  - Fix: Serve `lang`, `dir` and the UI string bundle from a `GET /v1/package/ui` document cached in the shell; rename `_ar` fields to `_native` (and `_target` where an English counterpart exists). One pair still ships; the mechanism is honest.
- **minor** (4.1 `middleware.ts` ("except `/call/[id]?k=` and `/today?h=`") and 4.7 feed item notes (`/show/{id}?k=...&door=read`)): The article link the feed carries goes to `/show/{id}?k=`, which the middleware does not exempt, so a learner tapping "read the article" from the podcast app is redirected to onboarding. The exchange also never strips `k` from the URL or history (only `h` is stripped), and the behaviour of an expired feed link (30-day TTL inside a 60-item feed of a replayable archive) is unspecified.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.1 ("The feed item's notes carry the call link and the article link"); 5.4.1 (entry from the feed link); 12.6.
  - Fix: Exempt `/show/[id]?k=` in the middleware; `history.replaceState` after both exchanges; on an expired or revoked `k`, show the sign-in screen and then route to the requested episode rather than a dead end; consider minting the feed link with the feed token's lifetime instead of 30 days since it is revoked with the token anyway.
- **minor** (4.3 `DoorState` type): `DoorState` cannot express a door that is not yet openable (Chat before any of Listen, Read or Call; Notes before a call; Round two before the main call), so the client has no honest way to render rule 1 and would either hide the door or let a tap fail.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 4 rule 1; 3.4 in-progress state shows the four doors.
  - Fix: Add `available: boolean` (and an optional `reason_ar`) to `DoorState`, set by `api`; the client renders an unavailable door as a word without a tick and no handler.
- **minor** (4.2 `/chat/[host]` row and 4.3 `GET /chat/{host}`): Nothing tells the server that the chat door opened, so the 30-second clock for the host's opener has no defined start from the client.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.6.2 ("the host's first message arrives within 30 seconds of the chat door opening"); 15 (chat opener under 30 s).
  - Fix: State that mounting `/chat/[host]` posts `POST /sessions/{id}/doors/chat {state: in_progress}` (idempotent) and that this call triggers the opener; log `client_perf {kind: chat_opener_ms}`.
- **minor** (4.2 Emits column (ideas on `/today` and the chat closer emit only `idea_chosen`)): `idea_shown` is a PRD event type the client must emit when the three ideas render, and 16.2's "tomorrow chosen" share needs it as the denominator.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 8.2 (`idea_shown` in the type list); 16.2 ("Tomorrow chosen: share of sessions ending with a pick").
  - Fix: Emit `idea_shown {plan_id, idea_ids[], surface: chat_closer|today}` once per render, like `claim_shown`.
- **minor** (4.2 Design tokens ("hosts are simple portrait marks ... No images of people, no mascot")): The small "AI" mark on host portraits is missing from the design tokens and the lint checklist.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 7.8.1 ("host portraits carry a small 'AI' mark"); law 5.
  - Fix: Add the mark to the host portrait component as a non-optional element and to the law lint fixtures.
- **minor** (4.3 `POST /onboarding/start` body (`{invite_code?, tz, mode}`)): The A1 18+ attestation is enforced only as a disabled button; it is never recorded server-side, which leaves no evidence of the attestation and diverges from the topology's contract.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 1.4 Audience ("A checkbox on the first screen"); topology 01 §9.5 expects `{invite_code, tz, age_attested, mode}`.
  - Fix: Send `age_attested: true` with `text_shown_version`; refuse the call without it.
- **minor** (4.4.7 Media Session (`artwork: [{ src: coverPngBlobUrl ... }]`)): Safari has a history of not displaying Media Session artwork given as a `blob:` URL; a blank lock-screen cover is exactly the commute surface the document is designed around, and nothing in the week-3 matrix calls it out.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.1 Lock screen row; 3.4 (typographic cover is the show's identity).
  - Fix: Serve the cover from a same-origin route on `app.forone.app` (`/covers/{episodeId}.png`, cached by the SW) and add artwork display to the week-3 device matrix.
- **minor** (4.6 Serwist configuration (`skipWaiting: true, clientsClaim: true`) and rule 4 ("a new SW deletes caches with other versions on activate")): Immediate activation plus purge of the old precache can leave an already-open page unable to lazy-load a route chunk between a deploy and its next navigation (the classic Next.js post-deploy chunk 404), which conflicts with the document's own promise never to disrupt mid-playback or mid-call.
  - Evidence: 4.1 ("the page reloads on the next navigation, never mid-playback or mid-call"); PRD-v2 15 (no session lost).
  - Fix: Register with `skipWaiting` deferred: the page messages the waiting SW to activate only when the player is idle and no call or recording is active, then reloads; keep the previous precache until that activation.
- **minor** (4.13 share page (`forone.app/s/{sampleId}`) and 4.7 feed XML): Two sibling rulings are not reflected: the topology ruled that the share page uses an unguessable `share_token` separate from the poll handle (the sample id is also the `GET /v1/sample/{id}` handle, so forwarding the share URL leaks the poll endpoint), and the topology's feed carries `<googleplay:block>yes</googleplay:block>` and a one-line note that feed-only listening does not start a new episode, while this document says item notes carry "exactly the two links".
  - Evidence: Topology 01 §9.5 (`share_token` wins); 01 §3.3 and §3.6 (directory tags; feed notes say so in one line); PRD-v2 12.6; 3.6.2 consequence for feed-only listeners.
  - Fix: Adopt `share_token` for `/s/` and `/s/{token}.mp3`; add `<googleplay:block>` next to `<itunes:block>`; add the one fixed package sentence to the item description and amend the pipeline assumption in section 9.2 accordingly.
- **minor** (4.9 rule 7 (dynamic manifest, `start_url=/today?h=`)): The per-learner manifest must not be cached by the browser or Vercel's edge, and the first-launch handling when the PWA jar already holds a cookie (every launch after the first carries the consumed `h`) is only implied.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 2.2 A10 install card; the document's own D10 rationale.
  - Fix: Set `Cache-Control: no-store, private` and `Vary: Cookie` on the manifest route; in `/today`, if a full `forone_s` cookie is present, strip `h` without calling the exchange; only call `handoff/exchange` when no cookie exists.

Missing:
- A rendering of the eight trust-contract promises at onboarding and in Controls (PRD-v2 1.3).
- A Salma conversation surface outside calls: settings by conversation, 'what do you know about me?', 'why do we say it like that?', 'where do I stand', life updates, fact corrections, 'Not quite right? Tell Salma' (PRD-v2 12.4, 7.3.2, 10.8.3, 12.3, 8.4.7, 3.9.4).
- The week-1 asks as client interactions: rhythm (3 to 6 days plus 2 spare), season goal confirm or reword, the future-self gap note with its 7/10/14-day gap, and the Controls editing of the note (PRD-v2 2.4, 3.9.2, 7.7.2, 3.8.1, 12.4).
- 'Delete all recordings' as a Controls action and endpoint (PRD-v2 12.4, 12.5.1).
- A cache-warm path that works when the episode becomes ready while the app is closed (SW push handler warm), and a stated p95 for the cold path (PRD-v2 15, 3.2).
- `idea_shown` event emission (PRD-v2 8.2, 16.2).
- `DoorState.available` for the door-ordering rule (PRD-v2 4 rule 1).
- The client-side trigger that starts the 30-second chat-opener clock (PRD-v2 5.6.2, 15).
- `age_attested` in the onboarding start payload (PRD-v2 1.4; topology contract).
- The 'AI' mark on host portraits (PRD-v2 7.8.1).
- Package-driven UI strings and direction instead of hard-coded `lang="ar"` and `_ar` field names (PRD-v2 13.1, 13.2).
- The 'hide what I'm working on' option and the 'don't bring this up' flag in the notebook endpoints' payloads (PRD-v2 12.3).
- Word-sheet 🔊 behaviour when the manifest has only sentence-level timing (streaming kinds), and the topology's HLS fallback decision for streaming kinds if the week-3 seam test fails (topology 01 §3.7).
- `article_scroll` payload that supports 'time per section' (PRD-v2 5.3.7).
- Middleware exemption for `/show/{id}?k=` and the expired-feed-link behaviour (PRD-v2 5.1 feed notes, 5.4.1).
- Handling of the consumed install-handoff token on later launches and `no-store` on the manifest route (PRD-v2 2.2 A10).

Strengths:
- D6 and D7 are the right calls, argued from law 1 and the 12.3 counting rule, aligned with the topology, and honestly surfaced as [OPEN for Muath] item 6 rather than silently re-deciding 3.8.3.
- The three message kinds are enforced at both ends (exactly three sender functions in `api`; the service worker refuses any other `kind`), which is the strongest possible implementation of 3.10 and trust promise 2.
- The law-1 lint rule, the explicit list of the only numbers the client renders, the no-`next` player state machine and the Media Session play no-op in `thrown` turn laws 1, 8 and 11 into testable code rather than review guidance.
- The Blob-URL-from-Cache-Storage playback path, the never-cache-206 rule and the immutable `(episode_id, manifest_version)` media keys are a boring, deterministic answer to the iOS service-worker media problem; the fallback ladder in 4.4.4 is clear.
- The feed-link limited session scope, the install `start_url` handoff and the Apple relay-email domain registration show real knowledge of the iOS cookie-jar and podcast-app realities the beta's 19.3 switch-to-native signal depends on.
- The Milestone 0 to code mapping table, the configuration-key table, the failure-mode table and the ordered single-engineer cut list are exactly the artefacts a two-person team needs and should be kept as they are.

## review:skeptic:01-system-topology

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (Section 7 'Build plan for 2026-11-02 to 2026-12-06', week 1 row and week 3 row): The five-week plan is not feasible for two engineers as written, and the document gives cuts only for the one-engineer case. Week 1 asks for monorepo, CI, compose, full Terraform (VPC, RDS, S3, KMS, ECR, ECS, ALB, Secrets Manager), core settings/DB/migrations, all 4.2 tables, the adapter interfaces, tracing middleware, EIGHT vendor adapters (Anthropic standard and batch, Google, ElevenLabs, Inworld, Soniox, Deepgram, AssemblyAI, ElevenLabs Scribe), the vendor-lab CLI, Inngest wiring, the api skeleton, Langfuse and Sentry. Week 3 asks one engineer (E2) for the whole PWA, RTL, three auth providers, A1 to A10, Today/Show/Notebook, the segmented player, word sheet, feed, push, sample, payments and admin, while A3 and A7 are LiveKit calls whose worker is built in week 4.
  - Evidence: Section 7 week 1: 'adapters for Anthropic (standard and batch), Google, ElevenLabs, Inworld, Soniox, Deepgram, AssemblyAI, ElevenLabs Scribe; vendor-lab CLI; Inngest wired...'. Week 3: 'onboarding screens A1 to A10 wired to api' while week 4 is 'call-worker on LiveKit Agents'. PRD 2.2 A3 and A7 are live Salma and host calls. The document's own goal (intro): 'a team of one to two engineers plus Muath can ship every P0 item'. The sibling 03 schedules a first 50-call latency run in week 2, which also needs the worker earlier than 01's week 4.
  - Fix: Rewrite section 7 with a two-engineer cut list and an honest critical path: week 1 runs the 14.4 and 10.14 tests with throwaway scripts on vendor SDKs (not production adapters), Terraform limited to RDS, one S3 bucket, ECS and Secrets Manager (or the console setup the single-engineer row already allows); formal adapters land in week 2 only for the winners; move a minimal call-worker (Salma onboarding persona, no governor) to week 2-3 so A3/A7 can be wired in week 3 and the main call hardened in week 4; name the P0 items that slip to the first beta week (Show tab, admin pages, Apple sign-in, offline event queue) and mark them [OPEN for Muath].
- **blocker** (Section 4.3, the `day/next` shell: 'drop_at = ... next local calendar day at the learner's drop time' and 'timeout=cutoff - now()'): The schedule math has no minimum lead time and no handling of a pick made after the cutoff. For a learner whose session ends within 6 h of the next drop (drop 06:00, chat finished 00:30; or drop 00:15, listened at 23:50) `cutoff - now()` is negative: the Python SDK rejects or zero-times the wait, `learner.cutoff_reached` fires immediately, and the pipeline starts on the top-ranked idea before the learner has picked; the real `idea.chosen` minutes later starts a second `episode/produce` for the same (learner_id, local_date, kind='daily') and hits the unique constraint. A second race: Inngest `wait_for_event` matches only events received after the wait registers, so an `idea.chosen` that lands before step 'wait-pick' is reached (outbox relay order, retries, fast onboarding A10) is never seen and the run falls through to the cutoff path.
