  - Evidence: PRD 3.1 rule 5 only says the top idea is used 'if nothing is chosen by 6 hours before the drop time'; it does not say a late pick is ignored. Section 4.3 function table: `episode/produce` 'idempotency episode_id' while `IdeaChosen` and `LearnerCutoffReached` carry no episode_id and no step creates the `episodes` row before the trigger. 4.2: `unique (learner_id, local_date, kind)`. 4.3 day_next code: `timeout=cutoff - now()` with no clamp.
  - Fix: Define `next_drop_at` as the first occurrence of the drop time at least N hours (say 7 h = cutoff plus one) after the activation; if the next occurrence is closer than that, either skip to the following day (PRD 3.6 tolerates one waiting episode) or run on-demand pricing from the start and say so in a [DECISION]. Clamp the wait timeout at zero and, before waiting, `step.run` a DB check for an existing pick for that local_date. Make `episode/produce` idempotent on `learner_id + local_date + kind` (api inserts the `episodes` row in state `requested` and puts `episode_id` on `idea.chosen` and `learner.cutoff_reached`), and state the rule for a pick that arrives after the cutoff (ignore, or swap-style replace if nothing has reached `voicing`).
- **major** (Section 4.8 'deploy-staging.yml ... ecs update-service api, workflows, call-worker (rolling, min healthy 100%)' and 4.4 item 6 'autoscaling target 60% CPU'): Every rolling deploy and every autoscaling scale-in of `call-worker` kills calls in progress. ECS Fargate stops a task after `stopTimeout`, whose maximum is 120 s, and a call lasts up to 8 min (PRD 5.4.2). The document says nothing about draining or scale-in protection, so a deploy at 07:50 Riyadh time cuts the morning calls mid-sentence.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.2: 'ends by 8:00'; PRD 4 rule 4: 'A call cannot resume mid-call'. ECS Fargate `stopTimeout` upper bound is 120 seconds. The failure table in section 5 lists `api` down, Postgres down, LiveKit down, but not 'deploy during a call'.
  - Fix: Add a deploy rule for `call-worker`: new task set starts, old tasks are put into LiveKit Agents drain mode (stop accepting jobs) and set ECS task scale-in protection while `active_jobs > 0`, old tasks are stopped by a small script only when idle or after 10 min; scale-in uses the same protection API. Pin `call-worker` deploys to a window outside 06:00 to 10:00 and 12:00 to 14:00 Riyadh time in the runbook. Add the row to the failure table.
- **major** (Section 3.6, row 'Host reply median under 1.5 s, p95 under 2.5 s': 'STT endpoint finalization 200 to 400 ms; LLM first token 300 to 600 ms with prompt cache; TTS first byte 100 to 300 ms; network 100 to 200 ms'): The latency arithmetic omits two terms and never says where the clock starts. (1) The adaptive end-of-turn silence is 1.0 to 2.5 s (PRD 5.4.4); if the PRD's 'after end of learner turn' is measured from the last learner speech frame, the endpointing wait alone consumes the whole median budget. (2) `call-worker` is placed in me-central-1 while Soniox, Anthropic and the TTS vendor terminate in the US or EU: three sequential vendor hops at 120 to 180 ms RTT each are 400 to 550 ms of network, not '100 to 200 ms'. Summed honestly the median is about 1.3 to 1.6 s with no headroom, and p95 will exceed 2.5 s.
  - Evidence: PRD 15: 'Host reply after end of learner turn: Under 1.5 s (median), under 2.5 s (95th percentile)'. PRD 5.4.4: 'End-of-turn silence starts at 1.5 seconds ... within 1.0 to 2.5 seconds'. 03 §4.15 defines two metrics (`reply_latency_ms` from the end-of-turn decision and `silence_to_sound_ms` from the last speech frame); 01 picks neither. 01 3.6 itself says 'worker in the same region as RDS is irrelevant here (no DB on the turn path)' but does not draw the conclusion about vendor RTT.
  - Fix: State the measurement point (adopt 03's `reply_latency_ms` = end-of-turn decision to first host audio frame as the PRD gate, and report `silence_to_sound_ms` next to it). Add the vendor RTT term and decide [DECISION] that `call-worker` may run in the region closest to the chosen STT/LLM/TTS vendors (eu-central-1 or us-east-1), since it holds no data at rest and uploads turn audio to S3 cross-region; make the 50-call test in week 4 compare both placements. Confirm LiveKit Cloud's nearest edge to Riyadh and add it to the budget.
- **major** (Section 3.6, row 'Sample episode playable under 30 s': 'About 25 to 40 s to first audio (pipeline domain's measured target)'): The document declares every budget 'met by design' (section 6) while its own number for the sample is 25 to 40 s against a 30 s budget, and the pilot row adds editor checks and Inngest step overhead (each step is an HTTPS round trip from Inngest EU to me-central-1, plus scheduling) that are not in the 'Writer 8 to 15 s ... TTS 3 to 5 s' sum. The sample also depends on polling every 1 s rather than a push, adding up to 1 s.
  - Evidence: PRD 15: 'Sample episode playable: Under 30 s'; PRD 2.1 #3 'Playable within 30 seconds (streamed)'. 02 §717 admits 'under 45 s at p95' for on-demand first sound. Section 6 of 01: 'every budget in section 3.6 is met by design in the common path'.
  - Fix: Either state plainly that the sample and on-demand budgets are 30 s median, 45 s p95 for 2026-12-07 and tag it [OPEN for Muath] as a PRD deviation, or change the sample path: skip the editor for the sample entirely (the law scan only), use a 60-second script, and start TTS on sentence 1 rather than scene 1. Add the Inngest step overhead (about 0.3 to 1 s per step from EU to me-central-1) as a line in every on-demand budget.
- **major** (Section 4.3 event catalogue: `SampleRequested(... name: str; topic: str; job: str ...)` and `LearnerExportRequested(... email: str)` under the rule 'Inngest sees pointers, never content'): Two events carry personal data into Inngest Cloud EU in direct contradiction of the document's own rule and of the residency argument made to counsel in section 10 item 2 ('holds ids and timestamps only'). A first name, job and free-text topic of an anonymous visitor, and a learner's email, are content, not pointers.
  - Evidence: Section 4.3: 'Rule that binds every function: Inngest sees pointers, never content.' Section 10 #2: 'Inngest Cloud (EU; holds ids and timestamps only)'.
  - Fix: Store the sample answers in `sample_requests` (add `name`, `topic`, `job`, `answers_audio_ref` columns, or a JSON `intake` column) and send `sample.requested {sample_id, device_key}` only; drop `email` from `learner.export_requested` and read it from `learners` inside the step. Add a CI test that fails if any event model has a field named `name`, `email`, `text`, `topic` or longer than 200 characters.
- **major** (Section 3.6, row 'On-demand episode (swap, return) under 30 s': 'learner.return_detected {learner_id, days_away} (on app open or feed fetch after 5+ days away)'): Podcast apps poll feeds and auto-download enclosures without the learner doing anything; Apple Podcasts refreshes subscribed shows on its own schedule. Triggering a return episode (standard pricing, about $0.50) on a feed fetch generates episodes for learners who merely have the feed subscribed, every 5+ days, which also contradicts PRD 3.6 rule 2. The client document already rejected this trigger.
  - Evidence: PRD 3.8 #3: 'when the learner next opens the app or plays the feed' (plays, not fetches). PRD 3.6 #2: 'No episode is made after an inactive day'. 06 §711: 'learner.return_detected is sent on app open or feed-link exchange only, never on a feed fetch (its 3.6; D6)'.
  - Fix: Change 3.6 and the `LearnerReturnDetected` sender note to 'app open or feed-link (call/article) exchange only'; add one sentence stating the consequence that a feed-only listener never becomes active (PRD 12.3 counting rule) and so never receives a new episode until they open the app, and that the feed item's notes say so.
- **major** (Section 3.2 trust boundaries ('Only api writes events') versus section 4.3 `call/verbatim-turn` ('call_turn event update with verbatim text, timings, confidences')): Two contradictions in the hottest write path. `workflows` must write to `events` (verbatim fields, `note_shown`, `claim_shown`, analyst outputs) although the document says only `api` does; and an 'update' to a `call_turn` event contradicts the append-only log. The data domain is left to choose between an in-place update and a second event, which means the verbatim path cannot be coded until that ruling exists.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.1: 'Every transcript, tap, replay ... is appended, never changed.' 01 3.2: 'Only api writes events; workflows writes stores'. 03 §711 explicitly asks the data domain to choose 'an append-only log with one allowed late-binding update per turn event, or a second event call_turn_verbatim'.
  - Fix: Decide here [DECISION]: `events` is append-only; the verbatim result is a second event `call_turn_verbatim {turn_event_id, ...}` written by `workflows` through `forone.core.events.append()`; both `api` and `workflows` may append, `call-worker` never writes directly. Update the trust-boundary paragraph and the 'Must not do' column accordingly.
- **major** (Section 3.2 'call-worker holds vendor keys and a per-call DEK handed to it at dispatch' versus 4.4 item 2 'the learner's wrapped DEK unwrapped by api (plain 32 bytes, in memory only)'; 3.5 'mTLS not needed inside the VPC; service token in Authorization'): The key handed to the worker is the learner's permanent data key, not a per-call key, so the most exposed service (holds every vendor secret, runs third-party plugins, autoscaled to 40 tasks) receives the raw key that protects every recording the learner ever made. The text is also inconsistent (per-call vs per-learner). The `/internal/*` routes are on the same ALB as the public `api`; the document never says they are blocked at the edge, so a leaked static `INTERNAL_SERVICE_TOKEN` exposes DEK unwraps from the internet.
  - Evidence: 4.4 item 2 quoted above; 07 §106 confirms 'never call-worker (it receives the bytes)'. 4.7 lists `INTERNAL_SERVICE_TOKEN` as one shared secret for `api` and `call-worker`. 3.3: `api` is one service 'FastAPI, Fargate, 2 tasks min' behind 'one ALB' (decision 9).
  - Fix: Pick one and write it: (a) derive a per-call key `HKDF(DEK, call_id)` in `api`, hand only that to the worker, and record the derivation in `media_objects` so reads can re-derive; or (b) keep the learner DEK but bind it to the task role with an ALB listener rule that routes `/internal/*` only from the VPC CIDR (or a second internal ALB) and a short-lived per-call JWT instead of the static token. Fix the wording in 3.2 to match 4.4.
- **major** (Section 3.7 'Media layout: segments, not files' and 4.6 'Player: <audio> per segment with a two-element gapless handoff' ('it is the only cross-domain media contract')): The document fixes a client contract that the client domain has since rejected and that is unreliable on the target platform: gapless handoff between two `<audio>` elements on iOS Safari (especially in a standalone PWA with the screen off) is not dependable, and per-segment elements break lock-screen position and Media Session. 06 D3 uses one element on the stitched file and segments only while an on-demand episode is still streaming. The boring, documented way to 'start on segment 1 while the rest renders' on Safari is an HLS playlist, which Safari plays natively in `<audio>`.
  - Evidence: 06 D3: 'per-segment elements everywhere (topology 4.6; gapless handoff is unreliable on iOS and complicates seeking)' listed as rejected. PRD 5.1 'Lock screen and headphones: Through the Media Session API'. PRD 1.4: 'works in mobile Safari and Chrome'.
  - Fix: Replace 4.6's player bullet with 06 D3 (one element on `episode.m4a`; two-element handoff only for streaming kinds) and keep 3.7's segment storage as a pipeline/storage contract only. Add HLS (event playlist from `api`, AAC segments, hls.js on Chrome) as the [DECISION] for streaming kinds if the week-3 iOS test shows seam gaps over 300 ms.
- **major** (Section 5 failure table 'Postgres down ... Multi-AZ failover in prod (60 to 120 s)' versus section 7 P1 row 'Multi-AZ' and section 6 'RDS Postgres $70/month (t4g.medium, 100 GB) → $140 (t4g.large)'): The failure table assumes Multi-AZ in prod; the build plan defers Multi-AZ to P1 (during the beta) and the cost line prices a single-AZ instance. So on 2026-12-07 Postgres is a single-AZ single point of failure for everything stateful, and a host failure means a restart or snapshot restore measured in tens of minutes, during which calls still run but nothing is recorded and no drop fires.
  - Evidence: Section 4.7 prod column: 'RDS db.t4g.large (beta) → db.r6g.xlarge at 10k, Multi-AZ, 30-day backups, PITR' is ambiguous; section 7 'P1, during the beta ... Multi-AZ'; section 6 RDS line prices single-AZ (Multi-AZ t4g.large in me-central-1 is about $240 to $280/month).
  - Fix: Decide one way and make the three sections agree. Recommended for the beta: Multi-AZ from day one at about +$120/month (cheaper than one lost morning of drops for 50 paying learners), or state explicitly that prod is single-AZ until 2027-01-10 with a documented restore drill and a 30 min RTO, and tag the choice [OPEN for Muath] with both prices.
- **major** (Section 6 cost table, 'ECS Fargate about $120/month (api 2 tasks, workflows 1, call-worker 1 at 1 vCPU/2 GB each, always on)' and 'Fixed infra total about $400 to $600/month'): The beta infrastructure number omits lines that exist in the design and undercounts Fargate. Fargate in me-central-1 is about $0.0496/vCPU-h and $0.0054/GB-h, so four 1 vCPU/2 GB tasks are about $180/month, not $120. A NAT gateway (required for Fargate tasks in private subnets to reach vendors) is about $36/month plus $0.045/GB; the ALB is about $25/month plus LCUs; CloudWatch logs for three services are $10 to $30; and staging ('one small copy of prod') adds a second RDS, three more tasks, a second NAT and ALB, about $250/month. The honest beta figure is $700 to $900/month, and this number goes to Muath as open item 4.
  - Evidence: Section 10 #4: 'Fixed infrastructure budget: about $400 to $600 a month ... Approve or name the cap.' Section 4.7 defines staging as a full copy. Section 4.1 Terraform module list includes VPC and ALB but no NAT line appears in section 6.
  - Fix: Add NAT gateway, ALB, CloudWatch and the staging environment as rows; correct the Fargate arithmetic with the region's prices; give the total as a range with staging on and off (staging can be stopped outside the 50-call tests to save about $200/month). Carry the corrected number into open item 4.
- **major** (Section 10 item 1: 'GCP me-central2 (Dammam) ... that swaps RDS, S3, KMS and Fargate for their GCP equivalents; two to three days of Terraform work, best done before week 3' with the decision due 2026-11-16): The region ruling is due at the end of week 2, after the infrastructure is built in week 1, and the swap cost is understated by roughly a week: Cloud SQL, GCS with customer-supplied keys, Cloud Run (which has request timeouts that conflict with long Inngest steps and the LiveKit worker), Cloud KMS, Secret Manager, Workload Identity for GitHub, a different IAM model, and every boto3 call in `forone.core.media` and `keys`. A GCP ruling in week 3 would consume the week that builds the web app.
  - Evidence: PRD 18 #3: legal review 'Before 2026-12-07'; 01 section 1 decision 8 'moving it is a one-day job before 2026-12-07' (only true between AWS regions). Section 7 week 1 builds 'Terraform for staging in me-central-1'.
  - Fix: Ask counsel for the one binary answer the architecture needs ('may Saudi residents' voice recordings rest in the UAE or EU on AWS: yes/no') by 2026-11-01, before week 1, and mark it the first line of section 10. State the GCP swap honestly as 5 to 8 engineer-days plus retest and name what it would displace. Note that AWS regions differ only by `HOME_REGION`, so `eu-central-1` versus `me-central-1` can wait until 11-16.
- **major** (Section 3.4 step 6: 'Its publish step writes episodes.state = ready, closes the previous open session, creates the new sessions row in state ready' and step 7 'If the episode is not ready at drop_at ... sends pipeline.human_page and the Today screen keeps yesterday's episode'): Two state-machine errors. (1) The pipeline closes the learner's current session when tomorrow's episode becomes `ready`, which is typically hours before the drop and may be while the learner is mid-chat at 23:00; PRD 4 closes a session when the next episode is published, and rule 8 says starting the next episode is what closes the old one. (2) When the episode becomes ready after `drop_at` (the common late case: batch escalation finishing 10 minutes late), `day/next` has already returned, so the feed item is never published and no push ever fires; the episode shows in the app only if the client reads `episodes.state` directly.
  - Evidence: PRD 4 rule 7: 'A session closes when the learner's next episode is published'; rule 8: 'Starting the next episode while the current one has unused doors needs no confirmation: the old one simply closes'. PRD 3.3 #1: 'The drop time controls the notification and the feed, not access.' 01 day_next code returns after `pipeline.human_page` with no later publish.
  - Fix: Move session close to `api` at the moment the learner first opens the next episode (and to the drop as the latest point, in `day/next`'s publish step). In `day/next`, after a missed drop, keep waiting for `episode.ready` until the next cutoff (or 12 h) and then publish the feed item late with no push; document that a late episode appears in the app the moment it is ready.
- **major** (Section 3.6 'First open to first sound' ('creates a pre_account learner and a LiveKit room') and 4.2 `learners.status 'pre_account'`): A3 records the visitor's voice (and keeps a name clip for pronunciation) before any voice-storage consent exists (A9), and the document defines no lifecycle for `pre_account` rows: no TTL, no default for `voice_storage`, no rule for abandoned onboardings (the majority of visitors), no `audio/expire` trigger since no `call.ended {voice_storage}` semantics are stated for an onboarding call. 06 had to invent 'the pre-account 24 h deletion' itself.
  - Evidence: PRD 2.2 A9: 'Voice-storage consent, right after he heard himself'; PRD 5.4.11 and 12.5 define deletion only relative to the consent flag. 06 §711: 'retention/daily gains the pre-account 24 h deletion'. 01 4.3 `retention/daily` lists no pre-account step.
  - Fix: Add to 4.2/4.3: `pre_account` learners are treated as `voice_storage=false` (every A3/A7 object `ephemeral`, deleted by `audio/expire`), the name clip is the one exception kept until A9 decides, and `retention/daily` deletes `pre_account` rows plus their keys 24 h after `created_at`. Say this in the consent text handed to the security domain.
- **major** (Section 3.6 'Sample episode ... limits check in sample_requests' and 4.2 `sample_requests.device_hash -- sha256 of a first-party device cookie`): The only abuse control on a public endpoint that spends about $0.05 per hit (and more on the pipeline's standard pricing) is a cookie the attacker controls; clearing it or scripting without it gives unlimited samples. The global `SPEND_CAP_USD_PER_DAY` then trips and stops real samples and, as written, every vendor call except live calls, which is a denial-of-service on the product by one script.
  - Evidence: PRD 2.1 #3: 'Limit: one per device and per phone number or email per 24 hours'; the PRD does not say the device signal is a cookie. Section 5 'Spend runaway ... Middleware refuses new vendor calls over the cap'. WAF rate rules appear only in 07, not here.
  - Fix: Add an IP-hash fixed-window limit (for example 3 per /24 per hour), a Cloudflare Turnstile or hCaptcha challenge on the sample form, a separate per-kind spend cap (`sample` capped at, say, $20/day independently of the global cap) so an attack cannot starve learners, and the WAF rate rule on the ALB. Reference 07's `forone.core.ratelimit`.
- **minor** (Section 4.10 'workflows autoscales 1 → 8 tasks on Inngest queue depth'): ECS autoscaling needs a CloudWatch metric; Inngest Cloud does not publish queue depth to CloudWatch, so this is a custom poller and metric nobody has budgeted. Inngest invokes steps over HTTP, so the simplest signal is the ALB target's request count or CPU.
  - Evidence: 4.10 row 'Generation window'. Section 4.8 CI/CD and 4.1 infra list contain no metrics exporter.
  - Fix: Scale `workflows` on ALB `RequestCountPerTarget` or CPU, with a fixed 2 tasks during the 22:00 to 02:00 window from 250 learners on. Note that Inngest's own function concurrency limits (150 global in beta) are the real throttle and must fit the paid tier's concurrency cap, to be verified in week 1.
- **minor** (Section 4.3 function table, `vendor/health`: 'if failures ≥ 5 and success rate < 50% in 10 min, set vendor_config to the secondary and send vendor.degraded' applied to `episode_tts` and `call_tts`): At 15 to 250 learners, five failures in ten minutes can be one bad call or one bad batch of segments; an automatic voice failover changes how the hosts sound and forces the PRD 7.1 on-air announcement on a transient. Automatic failover is sensible for STT and the editor, not for the voice the learner knows.
  - Evidence: PRD 7.1 #3: 'a forced vendor change is announced the same way'. Section 5 recovery column already says 'Manual switch back from /admin/vendors after a canary passes', so the switch-back is manual but the switch-over is automatic.
  - Fix: Make voice failover manual in the beta (page the on-call with the one-click `/admin/vendors` switch) and keep per-render understudy fallback inside the pipeline for individual segments; leave automatic failover for `streaming_stt`, `verbatim_stt`, `editor`.
- **minor** (Section 4.5 vendors.yaml: `embeddings: {vendor: openai, model: text-embedding-3-small}`): A fifth AI vendor (and a new PDPL processor that receives full learner scripts) is added for one cosine-similarity check over 20 scripts per learner. It is not in the processor list of section 10 item 2, nor are Anthropic, Google, ElevenLabs/Inworld, Soniox/Deepgram, AssemblyAI or Speechace, although all of them receive learner text or voice.
  - Evidence: Section 10 #2 lists only Inngest, Langfuse, LiveKit, Resend, Sentry. PRD 12.7: 'cross-border transfer' is part of the legal review.
  - Fix: Use Gemini's embedding endpoint (vendor already contracted for the editor) or a small local model in the worker image for similarity, and extend section 10 item 2 into a complete processor register (or point to 07's) so counsel sees every vendor that touches learner data.
- **minor** (Section 4.2 schemas: `episodes.learner_id uuid not null`; `feed_items.publish_at` referenced in 3.4 and the day_next code but never defined; `gap.armed`, `chat.closed`, `recording.completed`, `verbatim.turn_completed`, `notes.ready` used in the function table but absent from the event catalogue): Several names a developer needs on day one of week 2 are referenced but not specified here or clearly delegated. A sample episode has no learner, so `episodes.learner_id not null` cannot hold for `kind='sample'`; `feed_items` is written by the pipeline and read by `day/next` without a schema or owner statement in this document; five events in the function table have no Pydantic model.
  - Evidence: Section 4.2 `episodes` DDL and 2.1 'Sample episode ... in episode/produce'. Section 4.3 'Event catalogue (names are canonical ...)'. 02 §116 defines `feed_items` itself.
  - Fix: Make `episodes.learner_id` nullable with a check `(kind='sample') = (learner_id is null)`; add `feed_items` to 4.2 (or state 'owned by 02 §116, read by day/next') and add the five missing event models to the catalogue with sender and payload.
- **minor** (Section 3.3 `api` 'event ingestion (POST /events)' and section 5 'The PWA queues events in IndexedDB for up to 24 h and replays them'): The hottest endpoint in the system has no contract here: no client-generated event id for idempotent replay after the 24 h offline queue, no batch shape, no size limit, no ordering rule for `occurred_at` versus `received_at`. Without a client id, every replay after an `api` outage double-counts plays and 'active day' triggers.
  - Evidence: PRD 8.2 lists event types but not transport. 01 3.5 gives `POST /events` only the words 'appends to events'.
  - Fix: Specify `POST /v1/events` as a batch of up to 100 `{client_event_id (uuidv7), type, door, occurred_at, payload}` with `unique (learner_id, client_event_id)` on `events`, 207-style per-item results, and a 64 KB body cap; state that `day.became_active` is derived server-side from the deduplicated rows.
- **minor** (Section 4.2 'Postgres row-level security is enabled with a forone_app role whose policies filter on current_setting('forone.learner_id')'): RLS with a per-request GUC and pooled connections (SQLAlchemy async pool now, RDS Proxy later) is a classic leak if `SET` instead of `SET LOCAL` is used or a connection is returned mid-transaction; crons and fan-outs (`proof/fanout`, `retention/daily`, admin) need a bypass role; and nothing in the five weeks budgets the policy writing and tests. For two engineers this is more rope than protection.
  - Evidence: Section 4.2 quoted; 4.10 'RDS connections through PgBouncer (RDS Proxy) from 250 learners on'.
  - Fix: Either defer RLS to P1 and satisfy PRD 12.6 'row-level isolation' in the beta with a repository layer that requires `learner_id` on every query (a lint rule), or specify: `SET LOCAL` inside every transaction via a SQLAlchemy `begin` hook, a `forone_batch` role with `BYPASSRLS` used only by `workflows` crons, and one integration test per table.
- **minor** (Section 8 'Alternatives rejected', Temporal row and 4.10 'the same functions run on self-hosted Inngest or Temporal (both keep the step model)'): Overstated portability: Temporal's model (workers polling task queues, deterministic workflow code, signals instead of `wait_for_event` expressions, no HTTP-invoked steps) is not a drop-in for Inngest function shells; the function table's `cancelOn` expressions and priority expressions have no direct Temporal equivalent. The real lock-in is accepted, which is fine, but the document should not claim a free exit.
  - Evidence: Section 4.10 Inngest row; PRD 14.3 says 'Temporal is equivalent' as a choice, not as a migration.
  - Fix: Replace 'both keep the step model' with 'self-hosted Inngest keeps the code unchanged; Temporal would be a rewrite of the shells (about two weeks) with the step bodies reused', and keep the self-hosted Inngest path as the named exit.
- **minor** (Section 3.3 Admin: 'sqladmin, basic auth plus IP allowlist'): Muath and the on-call will reach `/admin` from phones and mobile networks in Riyadh with changing IPs; an IP allowlist either blocks them at 05:00 when an episode fails or gets widened to a carrier range. Basic auth also has no MFA and shares one secret.
  - Evidence: Section 10 #6 names on-call 'at 05:00 Riyadh time on some days'.
  - Fix: Put `/admin` behind Google sign-in restricted to two or three allow-listed accounts (the OAuth client already exists for learner sign-in) with the IP allowlist as an optional second factor, not the only one.
- **minor** (Section 3.5 and 4.2: `feed_tokens` plus `GET /f/{feedToken}/{episodeId}.mp3` streamed by `api` 'with Range passthrough'): Because every learner-scoped object is SSE-C, `api` is the media origin for every podcast-app download and every app play, forever, and the design has no way to use presigned URLs or a CDN. At 15 to 250 learners this is fine; the document should say explicitly that this is a deliberate beta constraint with the migration (service key for produced audio, 07 D3) already agreed, and should size `api` for Apple Podcasts' habit of HEAD plus full download of every new enclosure for every subscriber at the drop minute.
  - Evidence: Section 4.10 'Move the media route to a dedicated media service ... at about 1,000 learners'; 07 D3 accepts the later move. 06 §397: 'HEAD is supported (Apple Podcasts sends it)'.
  - Fix: Add one line in 3.7 or 4.10: 'In the beta, api is the only media origin by design; produced show audio moves under a service key and CloudFront after the security domain's post-beta review (07 D3); learner voice never does.' Add `api` CPU for the drop-minute burst to the week-4 load test.

Missing:
- A two-engineer cut list and a per-service definition of what 'P0 works' means on 2026-12-07; the document only gives single-engineer cuts and assumes the full PRD week table is achievable.
- A deploy and scale-in strategy for `call-worker` (drain mode, ECS task scale-in protection, deploy windows); the failure table has no 'deploy during a call' row.
- A complete definition of `next_drop_at` (minimum lead time, same-day-before-drop case, onboarding day, late pick after cutoff) and the idempotency key for `episode/produce` when triggers carry no `episode_id`.
- The `pre_account` lifecycle: TTL, consent default for A3/A7 audio, the name clip exception, cleanup in `retention/daily`.
- The event-ingestion contract (`POST /v1/events`): client event ids, batching, dedupe, offline replay semantics.
- Cost rows for NAT gateway, ALB, CloudWatch and the staging environment; corrected Fargate arithmetic with me-central-1 prices; the infra per-session figure in the exit criterion (does 'measured cost per session' include fixed infra or AI only?).
- A complete processor register for counsel (all AI, voice and recognition vendors plus OpenAI if kept), not only the five infrastructure processors in section 10 item 2; the option of Claude via Bedrock in an AWS region for residency should at least be mentioned and ruled in or out.
- Vendor concurrency and rate limits requested in week 1 (Soniox and TTS concurrent streams, Anthropic batch tier, AssemblyAI concurrency for 20 parallel verbatim jobs); 03 asks for 50 streams, 01 is silent.
- A backup restore drill, RPO/RTO numbers and the single-AZ versus Multi-AZ decision for the beta stated once and consistently.
- Where the host-reply clock starts (end-of-turn decision versus last speech frame) and the vendor RTT term from me-central-1; a statement of LiveKit Cloud's nearest edge to Riyadh.
- The statement that feed-only listeners never become 'active' (PRD 12.3 counting rule) and therefore never receive a new episode without opening the app, and how the feed notes tell them.
- Abuse controls on public, cost-bearing endpoints (`/v1/sample`, OTP) beyond a device cookie: IP rate limits, a challenge, a per-kind spend cap separate from the global cap.
- Image build details that bite in week 4: the LiveKit turn-detector and Silero models baked into the image at build time, ffmpeg and spaCy models in the shared image, and the resulting image size and cold-start time for autoscaled `call-worker` tasks.

Strengths:
- One durable Inngest run per learner per active day (`day/next`) is the right primitive: it implements PRD 3.6 rule 2 literally, inactive days cost nothing, and the idempotency, cancel-on and concurrency keys are thought through rather than hand-waved.
- The live-versus-durable service boundary (four deployables, one Python image with three entrypoints) is the smallest shape that fits LiveKit Agents and Inngest, and the rejected alternatives (monolith, microservices per role, Vercel-only, EKS) are argued honestly.
- 'Inngest sees pointers, never content' plus the write-behind `outbox` is a clean, cheap answer to both residency and Inngest outages; keep the rule and enforce it in CI.
- The adapter layer with `vendors.yaml` plus a `vendor_config` runtime override, a dated `prices.yaml` for the 2027-01-01 Gemini change, and a weekly canary episode on the secondary voice vendor is exactly the 'a swap is configuration' PRD 14.3 asked for and is sized for two engineers.
- Envelope DEKs under one KMS CMK with S3 SSE-C is a sound, boring way to get crypto-shred per learner at $1/month total, and the security domain has accepted it; the `media_objects` table makes deletion, export and the 15-minute rule table scans instead of bucket listings.
- The failure-mode table and the degraded-mode principle ('a learner never sees an error page for a generation problem') are concrete and map to PRD laws 11 and 7.1; the 'DEK destroyed by mistake' and 'spend runaway' rows show real operational thinking.
- Honest cost framing: the document says plainly that fixed infrastructure is $1.30 a session at 15 learners and is research cost, instead of pretending the PRD's $0.03 hosting line holds in the beta.

## review:skeptic:02-generation-pipeline

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (4.5 Step 3: the fitter, "Prior for unseen words ... p = sigmoid((size_est - rank) / (0.35 * size_est)) clipped to [0.02, 0.98]"): The prior is scale-invariant and caps every word's probability at about 0.946, so expected coverage can never reach the 95% audio target for a learner whose word bank is still mostly unseen (every learner in weeks 1 to 2). The fitter will fail every script, burn both rewrite rounds, fail editor check 1, trigger repair, then fallback, then page a human after three fallbacks in seven days, for every new learner.
  - Evidence: For rank 1: (size_est - 1) / (0.35 * size_est) = 2.856 for any size_est, sigmoid(2.856) = 0.946 < 0.95. A script made entirely of the 100 most common words scores about 0.94. The document also promises in section 7 week 1 that the port gives "the same numbers on the same scripts" as coverage-checker.html, but that tool is binary (`ok = r <= N || known.has(w)`), so a 97% script there scores about 93% here.
  - Fix: Replace the prior with one that saturates: p = clip(sigmoid((size_est - rank) / (0.12 * size_est)), 0.02, 0.99) with function words and the top 500 lemmas fixed at 0.99, or a piecewise rule (rank <= 0.6 * size_est -> 0.98; linear fall to 0.5 at rank = size_est; 0.1 beyond 1.5 * size_est). Add a week-1 unit test: the 10 Milestone 0 scripts must land within 1 point of the checker's binary score at the same N. Keep the width as config but ship a value that passes the test.
- **blocker** (4.1 and 4.13, `return` row: "teacher batch (standard run with `return` rules)" with deadline "first sound under 30 s"): The return episode is generated on the learner's next open (PRD 3.8.3) and the document runs a full Opus 5.5 teacher call first, which its own table (6) puts at 60 to 120 s, before the writer (10 to 20 s) and scene-1 TTS (5 to 8 s). First sound is 80 to 150 s, three to five times the PRD 15 budget the same row claims to meet.
  - Evidence: Section 6 latency column: Teacher (Opus 5.5) 60 to 120 s; 4.13 point 4: first sound 25 to 40 s assumes the teacher is skipped. PRD 15: "On-demand episode (swap, return) playable: under 30 s, streamed". PRD 9.1 trigger 4 lets the plan be made when the gap reaches 5 days, not at open.
  - Fix: Split the return path: the scheduler (01's `day/next` or a `gap/return-plan` function) emits `learner.return_due` at day 5 and the teacher runs then on batch pricing and stores a `return` plan and brief (claims_passed included). On `learner.return_detected` the pipeline starts at the writer with the stored brief, exactly like `swap`. If no stored plan exists (edge case), use the rule-based brief and mark `plan.fallback_rules=true`; never run Opus in the on-demand path.
- **major** (3.1 Components, "a TypeScript monolith deployed as one container" and 9.1 "One backend service (TypeScript)"; D19 "spaCy `en_core_web_sm` run in the fitter service"): The topology document (01, decision 1 and 3) chose Python 3.12 (FastAPI, Inngest Python SDK) and four services (`web`, `api`, `workflows`, `call-worker`), precisely because spaCy and wordfreq are Python. This document's TypeScript schemas, its single-service assumption and a spaCy lemmatizer inside a TypeScript process cannot all be true; a two-person team cannot run a Python sidecar for the fitter plus a TypeScript pipeline.
  - Evidence: 01 D1: "Python 3.12 ... The assessment domain needs spaCy, ERRANT-style rules and wordfreq"; 01 D3: "One monolith (Inngest steps and LiveKit workers cannot share a process shape)" rejected. This document 9.1: "One backend service (TypeScript) deploys this domain's modules".
  - Fix: State that the pipeline lives in 01's `workflows` service in Python; present the schemas as language-neutral (JSON Schema or Pydantic) and note that the TypeScript types are the client's view only. Delete the sidecar implication in D19.
- **major** (5 Failure modes table and 4.2; no row for a text-model vendor outage; `writer (standard)` on exhaustion: "restart as `fallback`; if already fallback, `episode.failed` and page"): Teacher, writer, rewrites, fitter tagging and glosses all run on one vendor (Anthropic). An outage or hard rate limit between about 21:00 and 06:00 Riyadh produces no episode for every learner that night; the only response is a page to a person who is asleep. The adapter has Google wired but nothing says the writer may use it.
  - Evidence: 4.2 table: writer exhaustion ends in `episode.failed`; section 5 lists only editor (Gemini) and TTS vendor outages. 08 section 4.7 already allows a same-vendor writer and editor with a `review_items(kind='same_vendor_edit')` tag, which this document does not use.
  - Fix: Add `llm-adapter` fallback rows: teacher -> rule-based brief (already there); writer and rewrite -> Gemini 3.8 Pro-class on the second vendor with `package.degraded_vendor=true`, the editor then runs on Claude Haiku/Sonnet with `verdict.same_vendor=true` and a review item (08's rule). Add the failure-table row and a drill in week 4 alongside the TTS failover drill.
- **major** (6 Cost and performance, "Generation total ... $0.33 to $0.38 (batch)" and "on the estimate"): The headline understates the generation half by about $0.05 to $0.10 and omits the 2027-01-01 case from the totals. Writer output is budgeted at 6k tokens including thinking, but the package JSON alone (900-word script with per-line metadata, 400-word article, call brief, recap, opener, bible updates) is 5 to 7k tokens and Sonnet 5.5 defaults to effort `high`, so 9 to 12k output is likely ($0.05 to $0.06 batch, not $0.04). TeacherInput with the full due list, a few-hundred-row structure map, error examples and 14 session summaries is 15 to 30k tokens, not 14k. Alignment ($0.04) is always paid if Gemini TTS wins (the lens notes it has no timestamps) yet the $0.33 low end drops it. Swaps, batch escalations and similarity regenerations are outside the table.
  - Evidence: Section 6 rows; lens table: Gemini 3.8 Flash TTS "no timestamps (add alignment)"; 08 section 1: "the recommended beta stack lands at $0.57 to $0.67 a session" with post-doubling prices as the base case. Recomputed batch generation: 0.09 + 0.055 + 0.02 + 0.003 + 0.015 + 0.001 + 0.135 + 0.04 + 0.04 + 0.01 = about $0.41, about $0.55 after the Gemini TTS doubling.
  - Fix: Rebuild the table with measured-shaped inputs: writer at effort `medium` with max_tokens 24000 streamed, output 8 to 10k; teacher input capped per section with `count_tokens` (target 16k); alignment always on for Gemini; a column for 2027-01-01 prices; a line for swaps and escalations. State the generation half as about $0.41 to $0.46 batch and $0.50 to $0.55 standard, and reconcile with 08's session total.
- **major** (D5 and 4.2 "Prompt caching applies inside batches, so the cached prefix saves there too"; 6 lever (5) alert if hit rate below 90%): The cache has a 5-minute TTL and cache writes cost 1.25x input. In the beta each learner's teacher and writer call happens once per evening at a time set by that learner's session, and a one-request batch is processed at a time the vendor chooses; at 50 to 100 learners the prefix is often cold, so the hit rate will sit well under 90% and the alert will fire every night. The cost table books 6k and 7k tokens as cache reads on every call.
  - Evidence: API reference: ephemeral cache TTL 5 minutes, write at 1.25x input, batch caching best-effort. 08 prices.yaml line 171: "Cache write = 1.25x input (5-minute TTL)". At 100 learners spread over about 5 evening hours that is one teacher call every 3 minutes, borderline, and batch processing times are uncorrelated.
  - Fix: Either price the table at uncached input (cost impact about $0.02 to $0.04 a session, which the document should show) or decide the 1-hour TTL for the two cached prefixes and price its 2x write. Set the alert threshold from a measured week-2 baseline, not 90%.
- **major** (4.5 coverage algorithm "lemma not in plan.new_words" and 4.3 `EpisodePlan.targets.words` (2 to 4) versus `new_word_budget {min 3, max 6}`): The planned-new-word list that coverage must exclude has no home: `EpisodePlan` has no `new_words` field, `targets.words` holds 2 to 4 items of mixed kind, `Script.new_words` is chosen by the writer after the fact, and the fitter "proposes planned-new status for at most one such lemma". A developer cannot tell who picks the 3 to 6 new lemmas, where the 80% top-3,000 rule is enforced before writing, or what the fitter excludes on round 0.
  - Evidence: PRD 9.2 caps targets at 2 to 4 words; PRD 9.5 requires 3 to 6 planted new words at 80% from the top 3,000; the two lists are different objects in the PRD and conflated in the schema.
  - Fix: Add `plan.new_words: {lemma, sense_hint, rank}[]` (3 to 6, chosen by the teacher, validated by code against the frequency list before the writer runs) separate from `targets.words`; the writer must use exactly those; coverage excludes `plan.new_words` plus `targets.words` of kind `new`; the fitter's "promote one unplanned lemma" rule is dropped or made explicit as a swap for a planned one.
- **major** (4.6 check 4 Memory (code): "every sentence mentioning a person, place, event or date is matched against `facts_allowed` and `bible` entities; any entity not in either set fails"): As written this is either unimplementable or always-failing: finding "a person, place, event or date" needs NER (not in the stated lexicon interface), fiction the writer legitimately invents in this very episode (new side characters, places in `bible_updates`, "last Sunday", "the derby") is not yet in the bible, and the rule would reject it. The memory check that matters (no fact about the learner outside `facts_allowed`) is not what the rule computes.
  - Evidence: 4.11: side characters are "applied from `bible_updates` only at publish", so they are absent from `bible` during editing. PRD 6.3 check 4 is about life facts used, their confirmation, expiry and quote existence, not about all named entities.
  - Fix: Make check 4 code do what code can: (a) count facts used by `fact_id` references the writer must emit per line (`carries` gains `{kind:'fact', id}`), at most 2, each verified by `facts.verify_quote`; (b) `never_mention` lemma match; (c) proper nouns in the script must be in `facts_allowed` entities, `bible`, `bible_updates` or `script.names`. Move "is any personal detail unsourced?" to the Gemini rubric with the facts list, as check 5 already does for world facts.
- **major** (4.8 Name pronunciation: "code picks the candidate whose recognizer output is phonetically closest (Double Metaphone distance) to the recognizer output of the learner's clip"): The learner's clip is transcribed by an Arabic-capable recognizer (Arabic script, or Latin if it guesses); the three TTS renderings are transcribed by an English recognizer. Double Metaphone is an English-orthography algorithm; comparing it across scripts and recognizers is not a measurement. The loop also costs three TTS renders plus four ASR calls per learner in the 45 s pilot window and adds an A5 control the PRD did not ask for.
  - Evidence: 9.2 assumes "the streaming recognizer used for the learner's name clip at A3 is Arabic-capable"; 4.8 compares its output with the batch English recognizer's output. Milestone 0 (04-production.md) solved this by hand: "spell them phonetically in the script (for example 'Fy-sal')".
  - Fix: For P0: Haiku produces one respelling from the learner's typed name plus the package's transliteration table (`name_rules`), the pilot uses it, and A5's existing hello clip includes the name so the learner hears it; a wrong one is fixed through Salma ("say my name like ...") as a `voice.change_requested` write. Move the ASR-verified loop to P1 and only if the beta shows name complaints.
- **major** (7 Build plan, weeks 1 to 4): The plan assumes a dedicated engineer on this domain for five weeks. Week 1 alone is five adapters with two vendors each, the language package, the coverage port with regression tests, fourteen tables and the price book; week 3 adds four on-demand kinds, casting, name verification, feed tag, ideas, why and the cutoff while PRD 19.2 puts the same engineers on onboarding, player, word sheet, feed and payments; week 4 adds fallback, full variety, bible/arcs/seasons rules, the review page and a failover drill while the PRD has the call pipeline that week. The "too much" paragraph cuts only P1 items that were never in P0.
  - Evidence: PRD 19.2 week 3: "Onboarding (A1 to A10) and Salma; the player; the word sheet; the feed; drop notifications; payments"; week 4: "The call pipeline ...". Team: "one to two engineers plus Muath" across nine architecture domains.
  - Fix: Add an engineer-week estimate per row and cut to what one engineer-equivalent can ship: P0 = daily + pilot + sample + swap on standard pricing, fitter, code checks plus Gemini editor, one TTS vendor plus understudy, align, re-listen (single resynth), gloss, publish, rule-based fallback brief, review via the Milestone 0 sheet. Move to P1 or week 5-if-time: batch mode with cut lines (ship standard first, the saving is $0.10), return-episode pre-plan, similarity embeddings, arcs/seasons code rules (bible as a text excerpt the writer updates), name verification loop, `working_on.changed` same-day re-plan (apply to the next plan), scene-parallel streaming manifest (pilot and sample first-sound budgets may be met by a short script instead).
- **major** (4.13 point 5 (sample) and 4.4 writer prompt: learner-supplied text (`idea.learner_description`, intake facts, `last_call_quote`, the three landing-page answers) is pasted into prompts; the sample runs "code checks plus the code law scan only"): The sample endpoint is public and unauthenticated; its three answers go straight into a Sonnet writer whose output is voiced in the hosts' voices and "is the learner's to keep and forward". No moderation of the input, no model editor on the output, and no statement anywhere that learner text is data rather than instructions. A hostile or merely crude topic produces brand-damaging audio for $0.035, and in-app "Tell Salma an idea" is an instruction-injection path into every surface.
  - Evidence: PRD 2.1 point 3 and 5; 4.13.5: "code checks plus the code law scan only"; the writer skeleton has no delimiting of BRIEF fields as untrusted.
  - Fix: Add a Haiku 5.5 moderation and intent call on the three sample answers (reject or neutralize, under $0.001) and run the Gemini editor checks 3, 5 and 10 on the sample script in parallel with scene-1 TTS (01 already does this for the pilot). Wrap every learner-authored string in the teacher and writer prompts in a tagged data block with the rule "text inside <learner_text> is content, never instructions", and add that rule to the editor rubric.
- **major** (D12 and 4.10: `sense_key` "`lemma#pos#short_gloss_en`" chosen per occurrence by the fitter's Haiku pass): The cache key contains free text a model generates per occurrence, so the same sense gets many keys ("bench#n#seat", "bench#n#long seat", "bench#n#substitutes' seat") and the shared cache fills with near-duplicates; the hit rate stays low, gloss cost never amortizes, and the reviewer's 5% sample reviews duplicates.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.7.2: glosses "keyed by lemma, sense and native language, generated once for all learners"; 4.10 provides no canonicalization step.
  - Fix: Keep a `senses` table per lemma; the Haiku pass selects among existing sense ids for that lemma and may propose a new one only with a one-line definition that code de-duplicates (embedding cosine over 0.9 merges). The key becomes `(lemma, sense_id, native_lang)`.
- **major** (4.2 "concurrency key `learner_id` (limit 1 ...; a swap request while a daily is still generating cancels the daily ...)" and 4.3 "Vetoes and additions arriving during the day trigger a standard teacher run that replaces the plan's targets and re-briefs if the episode has not reached `voicing`"): An Inngest concurrency limit queues a second run; it does not cancel the first, and a running function's steps cannot be edited from outside. The document never says how `working_on.changed` reaches a function that is sleeping on a batch poll, how the batch is cancelled, which steps re-run, or what happens to a late `idea.chosen` after the cutoff already started a rank-1 run. These are the exact interfaces the integrator needs.
  - Evidence: 01 table row for `episode/produce`: "concurrency 1 per learner (a swap cancels a running daily)" uses `cancelOn`; this document names neither `cancelOn` nor the restart contract.
  - Fix: Specify: `cancelOn: [learner.swap_requested, working_on.changed, learner.deletion_requested]` matched on `learner_id` with `if` guards (`working_on.changed` only while `status < voicing`); on cancel the function's `onCancel`/finalizer cancels any pending batch by id stored in `pipeline_runs`, marks the episode `superseded` or `replanning`, and a new run starts with `reuse: {plan_id?, package_id?}` so only the affected steps re-run. State that a late pick after the cutoff is ignored (PRD 3.1.5) unless the run is still before `writer`.
- **major** (4.1 `pilot` row: "teacher (rule-based brief from intake)" skipped; 2 requirements table row 2.3 "Day-2 episode carries at least one verbatim callback from onboarding; the editor checks it on day 2"): No teacher run exists between onboarding and the day-2 episode: the pilot skips it, A10's three ideas must appear at the end of onboarding, and the day-2 plan needs intake facts marked usable plus a callback the editor checks. The document neither produces the A10 ideas nor tells the editor about the day-2 rule nor says how intake facts become `facts_usable` on day 1 (the 8.4 ladder makes a once-said fact "never spoken").
  - Evidence: PRD 2.2 A10: "Three ideas for tomorrow's episode, written from the intake"; PRD 2.3; PRD 8.4.2: a fact viewed on a card and left unedited is usable (the A3 cards qualify, but nobody states it here).
  - Fix: Add a `teacher` run of kind `onboarding` started by `onboarding.intake_completed` after the pilot's writer step (standard pricing, Sonnet-class is enough) producing the A10 ideas and the day-2 plan with `callback` mandatory; record that A3 cards count as viewed so intake facts are `usable`; add `editor.day2_callback_required` to the code checks for `season.day == 2`.
- **major** (4.9 Audio re-listen: "a line fails if word error rate is above 0.15, or any target word or planted new word is missing or substituted ... Failing lines are re-rendered alone (same vendor, a new seed where the vendor supports it)"): Lines average 10 words, so two recognizer slips fail a line; the recognizer, not the voice, will miss Arabic and Saudi proper nouns (Al Hilal, Riyadh, Faisal's manager's name) in most episodes, and re-rendering with the same vendor and the same text is a no-op on deterministic TTS. The result is two wasted resyntheses per affected line, then a synonym rewrite for a name that has no synonym, then a transcript mark, nightly.
  - Evidence: 4.9 text; the learner-name case already needed a stored "expected ASR form" to work, which shows the general case fails without one.
  - Fix: Score only the words that matter: target words, planted new words, the learner's name (against its expected ASR form) and a global WER over lines with no proper nouns; exclude `script.names` from the diff. Resynthesize with a change (an alternate respelling, a style tag, or the understudy voice for that line), never the same request twice. Cap the loop at one attempt in P0.
- **minor** (6 "cache reads a tenth of input price"): Opus 5.5 cache reads are $0.20 per MTok, one twentieth of $4, not a tenth; Sonnet 5.5's $0.20 is a tenth. The error is in the document's favor, but the price book (08) and this table should agree, and Haiku 5.5's cache price is marked unverified in 08.
  - Evidence: API reference: Opus 5.5 cache reads $0.20; 08 prices.yaml rows 174 and 182.
  - Fix: Quote the per-model cache-read prices and cite 08's price book as the single source.
- **minor** (4.3 teacher skeleton (`max_tokens` 16000, effort `high`, thinking) and 4.4 writer skeleton ("effort default", `max_tokens` 16000)): Thinking tokens count against `max_tokens`; a structured-output call that hits the cap returns truncated JSON, which D4 turns into a full-price retry. Sonnet 5.5's default effort is `high`, so the writer thinks at length on a creative task where it adds cost without evidence of better scripts.
  - Evidence: API reference: Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 thinking cannot be disabled, effort is the only control; streaming recommended for large `max_tokens`.
  - Fix: Writer at `output_config.effort: medium`, `max_tokens` 24000, streamed in the standard path; teacher `max_tokens` 24000 streamed; log `stop_reason` per step and alert on `max_tokens`.
- **minor** (6 "the dropped episode starts under 2 s because it is a static file behind a CDN"; 3.3 "word-sheet cuts ... cut by byte range on the client"; 9.1 "range requests enabled for the word-sheet audio cuts"): 01 stores audio with SSE-C per-learner keys and serves media through `api` with Range support, which a CDN cannot cache in plaintext without breaking the key-deletion guarantee. And a byte range of an AAC/M4A file is not independently playable; the client plays `[s, e]` by seeking the already-loaded audio element.
  - Evidence: 01 D7 and requirements table ("`api` ... serves media with Range support").
  - Fix: Say "served by `api` with Range support from the learner's cached download" and describe the word cut as a seek on the loaded audio, not a byte cut.
- **minor** (4.8 Mix: "Scene files are kept for on-demand streaming and deleted after the full file exists"; 4.13 point 3 streaming manifest of `scene_{n}.m4a`): A client mid-way through scene 2 gets a 404 on scene 3 when the full file lands first; separate M4A files in an HTML audio element are not gapless, so every seam clicks unless the client uses MSE or Web Audio, which the client domain is not told.
  - Evidence: 4.8 and 4.13 text; 9.5 only says the player "consumes `AudioManifest`".
  - Fix: Keep scene files 24 h; state the handoff rule (the manifest's `app_audio.url` becomes non-null and the client switches at the next seam); require MSE-based sequential playback in the client assumptions.
- **minor** (4.2 "`teacher` (batch): run submit + `sleep`/poll every 5 min"): Each poll is a durable step; a 10-hour batch wait is about 120 steps per batch step, two batch steps per run, which approaches Inngest's per-run step budget and clutters replay. Cancelled in-progress batch requests are still billed, so "about $0.08 more" is a floor.
  - Evidence: Section 4.2 and 5 ("Cancel batch, run standard (about $0.08 more)").
  - Fix: Poll with `sleepUntil` at 15-minute intervals that tighten near the cut line, or wait on a batch-completion event emitted by one shared `batch/watch` function; note the double-billing case.
- **minor** (4.12 "`publisher` runs in one transaction: `episodes.status=ready`, artifacts written ..."): Object-storage uploads are not part of a Postgres transaction; a crash between upload and commit leaves orphan objects, and a commit before upload exposes a ready episode without audio.
  - Evidence: 4.12 text; 3.3 object storage layout.
  - Fix: Upload all objects first (idempotent keys), then one DB transaction that flips `status=ready` last; a retention job sweeps orphans by `pipeline_run_id`.
- **minor** (4.7 Similarity: "`embed-adapter.embed(script_text)` (default: an embeddings vendor behind the adapter ...)"): Anthropic has no embeddings endpoint, so this is a seventh vendor in the nightly path left unnamed, with no cost row beyond "<$0.001" and no failure row.
  - Evidence: 4.7, 6 and 9.7.
  - Fix: Name Google's embedding model (a vendor already contracted for the editor) as the default and ElevenLabs/Voyage-class as the swap; add the failure-table row (already partly covered by `variety` exhaustion).
- **minor** (4.11 `SeriesBible.last_episodes[].summary` and 4.6 editor input `LAST_EPISODES: {5 summaries}`): Nothing in `EpisodePackage` produces an episode summary; the editor's sameness check and the writer's bible excerpt depend on one.
  - Evidence: 4.4 `EpisodePackage` has `title` only.
  - Fix: Add `summary: string` (two sentences, plot and setting) to `EpisodePackage`, written by the writer and stored at publish.
- **minor** (4.2 `editor` exhaustion: "second vendor unavailable: code checks only, `verdict.degraded=true`, episode ships"): Shipping with no language-correctness check at all is worse than a same-vendor model check with a flag, which 08's adapter rules already permit.
  - Evidence: 08 section 4.7: same-vendor writer and editor allowed with a `note` and a `same_vendor_edit` review item.
  - Fix: Degrade to a Claude Haiku/Sonnet editor with `verdict.same_vendor=true` and the review item; code-only is the second fallback.
- **minor** (4.6 editor prompt: the full package with `FACTS_ALLOWED`, `NEVER_MENTION` and the learner's name, age, job and city goes to Google): Learner life facts leave the Anthropic processor for a second vendor; the document does not note the data-processing terms (paid tier, no training) or PDPL cross-border implications, which the Milestone 0 kit already flagged for AI Studio.
  - Evidence: 04-production.md setup step 4; PRD 12.7 (PDPL cross-border transfer OPEN).
  - Fix: Add one line in section 9.6: the editor vendor must be on paid API terms with no training use, listed in the privacy domain's processor register; send the learner's first name only and strip `age`/`city` unless the pitch test needs them.
- **minor** (4.15 Configuration keys): 08 defines `spend_mode` levels (`conserve`: Sonnet teacher, one rewrite round, forced batch TTS; `halt`: no new on-demand generation except return) that this pipeline must honor, but no key here reads them.
  - Evidence: 08 section 4.5.2.
  - Fix: Add `spend_mode` handling to `episode-producer` (model and round caps per mode) and list the keys.

Missing:
- PRD 9.1 trigger 3 (a follow-up coming due rewrites one idea) and PRD 3.1.4 (today's life update rewrites one idea before it is shown tonight): no event, no step and no latency budget for the idea rewrite that must happen between the after-session pass and the end of tonight's chat.
- The return episode when `claims_passed` is empty (a learner away 5 days in week 1): PRD 3.8.2 requires exactly one real win; the document must say the line is omitted, never invented, and the editor checks that.
- A TeacherInput token budget and the contract for compacting it (per-section caps, `count_tokens` check) so the teacher's cost and latency are bounded.
- How the pipeline is tested in weeks 2 to 4 when the after-session pass, analyst and memory keeper only land in week 5: which stub `TeacherInput` fixtures, which fields are empty, and how `p_understood` behaves with an empty word bank.
- Idempotency of paid side effects on step retry: a retried `voice` or `relisten` step re-bills the vendor; the step should check `pipeline_steps` for a completed attempt with the same `custom_id` before calling.
- The rehearsal format (PRD 7.6.5, P0): the writer prompt's format library names it but gives no pattern, and the call brief has no 'scene' shape (who the host plays, the scene goal), which the call domain needs.
- 'Tell Salma an idea' mapping to the closest format pattern with the learner's description winning on conflict (PRD 6.5 format library): `WriterBrief.idea.learner_description` exists but no step maps it to a `Format` or tells the fitter and editor that the format rule is relaxed.
- Hello clips per voice pool (3 voices x 2 hosts x 2 accents x 2 vendors, plus understudies): who renders them, when, and the one-time cost; the document only says 'pre-rendered once per voice, cached'.
- A reconciliation plan between the Milestone 0 binary coverage checker and the probabilistic server fitter, including which one defines 'coverage accuracy' in the offline suite and the 'how much did you follow?' comparison.
- Expected batch escalation rate and its cost: the cut lines are defined but no estimate of how often a one-request batch misses a 2-hour window (cutoff-triggered episodes) and what that adds per session; 08's `escalation_rate` alarm needs a baseline.
- Structured-output failure handling: what happens when the model returns schema-invalid JSON or `stop_reason: max_tokens` (retry count, cost cap, whether the retry adds a 'return valid JSON' instruction), and how often this is expected.
- Operational ownership at night: who is paged by `pipeline.human_page` at 02:00 and what the runbook step is, given the document's own observation that nobody is awake.

Strengths:
- One durable function for every episode kind with explicit per-step timeouts, retries and exhaustion behavior is the right shape for a two-person team, and the failure-mode table with explicit degraded flags (`degraded`, `timing_quality`, `relisten`, `fallback_rules`, `failover`) makes silent degradation impossible to miss.
- The code-versus-model split of the eleven editor checks and the fitter (coverage as arithmetic, structure realization as a small tagged model call, rewrites always by the writer) follows PRD 8.1 'models propose, code decides' and keeps the expensive, non-deterministic parts few.
- Per-step cut lines ahead of the PRD's drop-minus-90 switch, with the reasoning that rewrites, resynthesis and re-listen need their own headroom, is a real improvement over the PRD's single rule.
- Paired understudy voices chosen in the blind test, sticky automatic failover and a single alignment path that makes the player independent of the TTS vendor are exactly the vendor-risk posture the PRD asks for.
- The schemas, prompt skeletons with a single cache breakpoint, the configuration-key table and the per-episode cost roll-up through `pipeline_steps` give a developer something concrete to build from; the open items for Muath (ElevenLabs economics, the Gemini doubling, soft editor fails, the review budget) are the right ones.
- The document is honest where it falls short of the budgets (on-demand first sound under 30 s only at median in P0) instead of asserting compliance.

## review:skeptic:03-live-call-and-voice

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (4.15 Latency budget, host reply: "`@moves` header parsed | 120 ms | 250 ms | about 40 tokens at streaming speed" and "Total | about 950 ms | about 2.0 s"): The latency arithmetic is wrong by roughly a factor of two on the one P0 gate this document owns. (a) The example header in 4.3 (`@moves {"recast":{"heard":"I go","form":"went"},"clarify":false,...,"ai_disclosure":false}`) is about 55 to 65 tokens, not 40, and 40 tokens in 120 ms implies 330 tokens/s; a Sonnet-class model streams at roughly 60 to 90 tokens/s, so the header alone costs 650 to 1,000 ms before the first word reaches TTS. (b) The table omits the vendor round trips that 01 §3.6 counts explicitly ("vendor round trips 3 × 120 to 180 ms = 400 to 550 ms from me-central-1"); 01 sums the same path to "1.1 to 1.6 s with little headroom" before the header cost. (c) The 350 ms p50 first-token figure is the bottom of D7's own "300 to 600 ms" range. Summed honestly: 150 + 10 + 500 + 800 + 200 + 120 + 450 = about 2.2 s p50 against the 1.5 s gate, and that is before the governor's 600 ms re-request on some turns. The same header inflates host output tokens (90 per turn, not 60) and risks `max_tokens: 120` truncating the spoken sentence (`stop_reason: max_tokens` is not handled anywhere).
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 15 ("Host reply after end of learner turn: under 1.5 s median, under 2.5 s p95"), PRD-v2 14.4 #3; 01-system-topology.md §3.6 latency row; token count of the 4.3 example header; typical Sonnet-class output throughput (to be measured in week 1, but nowhere near 330 tok/s).
  - Fix: Replace the full JSON header with a sparse one that names only the moves used and defaults everything else to false, for example `@m recast="I go">went gap=rg2` (8 to 14 tokens, 100 to 200 ms) with an explicit grammar in layer A; raise `max_tokens` to 200 and specify the `max_tokens` stop-reason path (speak what was produced, log `truncated`). Add a vendor-RTT row to 4.15 and adopt 01's `CALL_WORKER_REGION` choice (worker next to the STT/LLM/TTS endpoints). Add preemptive generation: start the host request on the interim transcript when VAD silence reaches about 600 ms and the end-of-utterance probability is high, cancel if speech resumes (LiveKit documents preemptive generation; verify the option in week 1). Rewrite the table with measured Sonnet 5.5 time-to-first-token and tokens/s from the week-1 quickstart, not list figures.
- **blocker** (7. Build plan for 2026-11-02 to 2026-12-06 (weeks 1 to 5) and "Too much for one to two engineers, and the cut"): The plan needs one engineer full time on the call for all five weeks (week 1 worker skeleton plus `call-bench` plus five vendor plugins; week 2 governor, recorder, verbatim path, Salma persona; week 3 live onboarding, captions, client audio contract; week 4 the entire main call plus voice-note fallback plus autoscaling; week 5 pronunciation, recordings, crisis, load test). 01 §7, the agreed two-engineer critical path, gives the call half of E2, who in the same weeks ships the web shell (week 2), onboarding screens A1 to A10 and the player (week 3), the full call pipeline (week 4) and Controls plus the recording screen (week 5). 01 also puts the throwaway 30-clip scripts in week 1 and a minimal worker in week 2 with the 50 calls due 2026-11-15; this document builds a production worker skeleton in week 1 for a test that needs no LiveKit at all, and runs the gated 50-call test in week 4. The cut list is honest but too shallow: everything it keeps as P0 still does not fit.
  - Evidence: 01-system-topology.md §7 ("Team assumption: two engineers (E1 backend...; E2 web and call)"; week 1 "Throwaway scripts on the vendor SDKs under tools/vendor-lab (not production adapters)"; week 2 "Minimal call-worker on staging ... the 50 scripted calls by 2026-11-15"); 01 §9 item 2 ("the 50-call test runs in week 2 on the minimal worker, not week 4 (03 schedules it in week 4...)"); PRD-v2 14.4 ("in the beta build's first two weeks"), 19.2.
  - Fix: Rewrite section 7 against 01 §7's E2 column: week 1 = LiveKit quickstart locally plus the 30-clip scripts (no worker, no call-bench); week 2 = minimal worker (stock plugins, static brief, no governor, no talkback) and the 50-call run on it by 2026-11-15; week 3 = A3/A7 on the minimal worker; week 4 = governor, per-turn upload, talkback, hold-to-talk, patience; week 5 = fallback, crisis, cost. Move to P1 or cut outright: the second Salma audio track, word-level captions, the reverse gloss index, in-call `p` adaptation, headphone-button mappings, the understudy mid-call switch (already listed), plus `resumed_after_drop` gap clip variants, the retell ring, the driving-phrase path, autoscaling (fixed 2 tasks, as 01's one-engineer row says), `calls.metrics` percentiles beyond reply latency, and the headless-WebRTC scripted caller (feed M0 audio files into the AgentSession input in-process instead). State plainly which PRD 5.4 rules ship after 2026-12-07.
- **major** (4.5 Endpointing: "LiveKit's `AgentSession` is configured with `min_endpointing_delay = p`, `max_endpointing_delay = p + max_extend`, the multilingual turn detector, `min_interruption_duration = 0.5 s`, `min_interruption_words = 2`, and `allow_interruptions = true`" and "the `turn-controller` flips `session.input.audio` to a buffering mode where frames still reach STT but end-of-turn is suppressed"; D1: "LiveKit's multilingual turn model covers Arabic for talkback"): The turn-taking design is written against an API that does not match current LiveKit Agents documentation, and it has two owners of end-of-turn. The current option names are `min_delay`/`max_delay` under `turn_handling`, `InterruptionOptions.min_duration`/`min_words`, and interruption enablement on `turn_handling.interruption`; there is no "buffering mode". More seriously, the text-based `MultilingualModel` turn detector is marked deprecated (removal in Agents 2.0) and its language list does not include Arabic; only the separate audio turn detector lists Arabic. So D1's justification is false, and 4.9's onboarding call (Arabic learner turns through the same endpointing) has no supported turn model. The custom table in 4.5 (backchannel at 1.2 s, hold state, retell extension, in-call `p` changes) cannot be expressed through the built-in endpointing; a developer cannot tell whether AgentSession or `turn-controller` decides end-of-turn.
  - Evidence: docs.livekit.io/agents/build/turns/ (manual mode: `turn_detection="manual"`, `session.commit_user_turn()`, `session.clear_user_turn()`, `session.interrupt()`, `session.input.set_audio_enabled()`); docs.livekit.io/agents/build/turns/turn-detector/ (text model languages exclude Arabic; "deprecated, slated for removal in version 2.0"; audio detector supports Arabic; CPU inference about 50 to 160 ms, 396 MB model); PRD-v2 5.4.4 rules 2 to 4.
  - Fix: Make `turn-controller` the single owner: run `AgentSession` with `turn_detection="manual"`, feed Silero VAD events and the audio turn detector's end-of-utterance probability into the controller, and have the controller call `commit_user_turn()` (with `stt_flush_duration` tuned) for end-of-turn, `clear_user_turn()` on talkback, and `interrupt()` on barge-in. Hold-to-talk becomes "never commit while `hold.down`". Pin the Agents SDK version in week 1 and record the option names verified against that version. For onboarding (Arabic), use VAD plus a fixed 1.8 s delay, no text turn model. Measure the detector's "incomplete" rate on the M0 learner transcripts in week 1 so `max_extend` is set from data, not 1.5 s by assumption.
- **major** (4.2 `CallBundle`: "crypto: { dek: string /* base64, 32 bytes, memory only */; key_version: number }"; 3.2 step 6 "uploaded ... with SSE-C under the learner's DEK"; 4.10 "The worker holds the DEK in memory only"; 9.6 "`api` unwraps the DEK for the worker for the call's duration"): The document hands the raw per-learner DEK to the most exposed service, which the topology explicitly rejected ("handing the raw DEK to the worker (the first draft of this document)" is in 01's rejected column) in favour of a per-call key `HKDF(DEK, "call:" + call_id)` recorded in `media_objects.key_derivation`. It also leaves worker-to-`api` authentication unspecified: `GET /internal/calls/{callId}/brief` returns a learner's key material keyed only by a `callId` that sits in LiveKit dispatch metadata, and `POST /internal/events` carries no credential; 01 specifies a per-call JWT (5-minute TTL, audience `call-worker`) in the dispatch metadata and the `Authorization` header. Route names also diverge (`POST /internal/calls/{id}/end` here; `POST /internal/calls/{id}/turns` in 01), and the `media_objects` row shape omits `key_derivation`, so a Range read of `call_full` by the notes path (05 §4.7) could not re-derive the key.
  - Evidence: 01-system-topology.md D7 and §3 interface table rows for LiveKit → call-worker and call-worker → api/S3 ("per-call JWT", "HKDF(DEK, \"call:\" + call_id)", "never holds ... a static secret that unwraps keys"); 07-security D2 (`keys.get_dek` "never `call-worker` (it receives the bytes)"); PRD-v2 12.6.
  - Fix: Replace `crypto.dek` with `crypto: { call_key: base64, key_derivation: "call:{call_id}", key_version }` derived by `api`; add `jwt` to the dispatch metadata and require it on every `/internal/*` call from the worker; write `key_derivation` on every `media_objects` row the recorder creates; align route names with 01 (`/turns`), and record the deviation in section 9 if `/end` is kept. Specify how the S3 spool file (`forone-voice/spool/{callId}.ndjson`, "encrypted") is encrypted and who can read it back.
- **major** (4.5 Pause profile: "`p = clamp(P90 of within-turn pauses over the last 3 calls, 1.0, 2.5)` where a within-turn pause is a gap between two verbatim words inside one learner turn"): The formula measures a censored distribution. Any silence of at least `p` (or `p + max_extend`) ends the turn, so no within-turn pause longer than the current endpoint can ever be observed except inside a held floor. P90 of a distribution truncated at `p` is always below `p`, so the profile can only ratchet downward toward the 1.0 s floor, which is exactly the "host cuts the learner off" failure the voice lens warns about and PRD 5.4.6 forbids ("Never ... interrupts a learner mid-sentence"). The only upward force is the in-call cut-off rule, which section 7 lists as a cut candidate.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.4.4 rule 3 (adapt per learner within 1.0 to 2.5 s); voice-speech lens ("Learners pause inside sentences ... about 756 ms on average ... Many single pauses are much longer"); the formula's own definition of a within-turn pause.
  - Fix: Use two signals with explicit direction: cut-offs (learner resumes speech within 700 ms of the host starting, gated by the 0.5 s / 2-word interruption threshold so echo does not count) push `p` up by 0.25 s per call; the P90 of pauses observed during held-floor turns and of pre-endpoint pauses between 0.6 s and `p` pushes it down only when no cut-off occurred in the last 3 calls. Keep the cut-off rule in P0 (it is a counter and one `+=`), and report both counts in `call_ended.metrics` so the learner-model domain can audit the direction of drift.
- **major** (4.15: "Measured from the end-of-turn decision. The silence window `p` is the learner's and is reported separately as `silence_to_sound`"; 4.5 table row "`s >= p + max_extend` ... end of turn regardless"): The number the learner feels has no gate. With `p = 1.5 s` and the document's own 0.95 s (really about 2 s, see the blocker) reply, silence-to-sound is 2.5 to 3.5 s on a completed sentence; when the text detector judges the sentence incomplete (common for B1 learners whose finished sentences look unfinished: "I go to... the mall"), the turn waits `p + 1.5 s` = 3.0 s before the reply even starts, so those turns land at 4 to 5 s of silence. 01 accepted the end-of-turn-decision clock as the PRD 15 gate, but PRD 14.4 says "after end of turn" and no document commits to a ceiling on `silence_to_sound_ms`, and nothing in the plan measures how often the detector says "incomplete" on learner speech.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 15 and 14.4 #3; voice-speech lens ("A June 2026 study ... learners took fewer, longer turns ... needed longer to start speaking"); the 4.5 table; 01 §3.6 ("Clock starts at the end-of-turn decision ... adopted as the PRD 15 gate").
  - Fix: Add a second gate to 4.13 and `call-bench`: `silence_to_sound_ms` p50 under 2.5 s and p95 under 4.0 s on completed sentences [OPEN for Muath: confirm which clock PRD 15 means]. Close the gap with preemptive generation (start the host request at about 600 ms of silence when the detector says complete; cancel on resumed speech), and cap the incomplete-sentence extension at 1.0 s for `p >= 1.5`. In week 1, run the turn detector over the M0 learner transcripts and report the incomplete rate; if it is over 30%, drop the text detector and use VAD plus `p` only.
- **major** (9.2 Episode generation pipeline: "the voice studio renders the opening-line clip (normal and `resumed_after_drop` variants) and the backchannel clips per host voice at casting time, and the package's static Salma and crisis clips once ... the gloss precompute also writes a reverse index ar→en for talkback"; D4, D11, 4.7.1 step 1): None of these exist in 02-generation-pipeline.md: it has no opening-line clip, no backchannel clips, no `talkback_intro_ar`/crisis clips and no reverse gloss index (its voice studio renders episode segments, hello clips and name candidates only). Three decisions this document relies on for connect-under-3-s (D11), zero-latency backchannels (D4) and 10 ms talkback resolution are therefore unowned work that no week in either plan contains.
  - Evidence: 02-generation-pipeline.md §4.8 voice studio, D12 gloss cache (keyed lemma→gloss, no ar→en index), §9 assumptions about this domain (none of the clips named); grep of 02 for "opening", "backchannel", "reverse index" returns nothing.
  - Fix: Own them here or cut them: (1) the recorder/worker renders the 8 to 10 backchannel clips and the two opening-line variants through `voice-studio.render(RenderJob)` (02's public API) the first time a `voice_id` is used and caches them in `forone-media/package/voices/{voice_id}/`, with a one-line [DECISION] and a week-4 task; (2) the opening line is rendered by the brief compiler at episode publish via the same API (add it to 02's `episode/produce` publish step as a named ask in section 9 with a date); (3) drop the reverse gloss index for the beta (Haiku resolves every ask, already in your cut list) and say so in D6.
- **major** (4.4: "Fallback order on a model error or a first-token wait over 1.2 s: retry once on the same model with a 1.5 s timeout; then `claude-haiku-5-5` with the same prompt"; 4.3: "appends a system message `Do not use: nudge. Reply again.` and re-requests once"): Two request-shape errors will 400 in production under exactly the conditions they are meant to handle. (a) `thinking: {type: "between_tools"}` is accepted only by Claude Sonnet 5.5; re-sending the same body to Haiku 5.5 is rejected, so the fallback path fails on its first use. (b) A mid-conversation `role: "system"` message must follow a `user` message and be either last or followed by an `assistant` turn; the re-request appends a second system message after the operator note (two consecutive system entries after one user turn), and if the aborted partial reply is kept as an assistant turn the system message after it is also disallowed. Also unspecified: how the operator notes are rendered for the Gemini fallback ("plain system prompt" loses the per-turn allowances, so the governor's contract is unenforced on that path), and effort on Haiku 5.5 (default `medium`, not `low`).
  - Evidence: claude-api skill, Claude Sonnet 5.5 migration ("`between_tools` ... Claude Sonnet 5.5 only ... client-side code that re-sends the same body to another model ... must drop the field first"); mid-conversation system messages placement rule ("must follow a `user` message ... and must be either the last entry in `messages` or be followed by an `assistant` turn"); Haiku 5.5 effort default `medium`.
  - Fix: Give the host adapter a per-model request builder: Sonnet 5.5 → `between_tools` + `effort: low`; Haiku 5.5 → omit `thinking`, `effort: low`; Gemini → fold the operator note into the user turn as a prefixed line. For the re-request, discard the partial reply and rebuild the single operator note with the prohibition (`allowed: ...; forbidden this turn: nudge (you declared it; reply again without it)`), never a second system entry. Add a 20-line fixture test that sends each shape to each model in week 2.
