- **major** (3.1 "Host LLM adapter | LiveKit LLM plugin | Anthropic `claude-sonnet-5-5`"; 4.4 "cache breakpoints on the two system blocks and the last user turn" and "Operator notes are `{"role": "system"}` entries inside `messages`"): D7's latency and cost ("cache warm across the whole fleet", $0.034 input per call) depend on placing `cache_control` breakpoints at three specific positions, inserting `role: system` entries into `messages`, and passing `output_config.effort` and `thinking.between_tools`. The stock LiveKit Anthropic plugin converts LiveKit's `ChatContext` to the API itself and is unlikely to expose all four; nothing in the plan verifies it before week 2's governor work, and a custom `llm.LLM` node is not budgeted. If the breakpoints are not where the document says, every turn re-writes the whole history at $2.50/M and first-token latency rises.
  - Evidence: claude-api skill, Prompt Caching ("Any byte change anywhere in the prefix invalidates everything after it"; "Verify with `usage.cache_read_input_tokens`"); section 6's cost assumes "cached reads about 90k tokens".
  - Fix: Add to week 1: a 30-line probe through the plugin logging `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` per turn; if the plugin cannot place the breakpoints or the system entries, write a thin `llm.LLM` subclass over the Anthropic SDK (one file) and say so in 3.1. Add `cache_read_tokens` to `CallTurnPayload` so a silent cache miss is visible in `calls.metrics`.
- **minor** (4.3 Phase machine versus `Governor` code: "ARGUE | ARGUE_AFTER ─(t >= close_begin_s)─► CLOSING", "RETELL ─(learner turn ends, or 90 s)─► ARGUE_AFTER", `check()` "if moves.get("grades") or moves.get("explains_grammar")", `allowances()` `retell=... c.recall_gaps >= 1 and self.phase == "ARGUE"`, "A missing or malformed header is treated as `{}`"): The diagram and the code describe different machines: the diagram moves to CLOSING on time, the code only when the host declares `close`; the RETELL→ARGUE_AFTER transition and the 90 s timer are not in the code; `recall_gaps_min` is never used, so the PRD's "2 to 4 per call" has no lower-bound pressure; retell is gated on a prior recall gap and a 2:00 to 4:30 window the PRD does not have, so the mandatory once-per-call retell can be skipped; `grades`/`explains_grammar` are not keys in the declared header, so the "never" check is dead code and the never-list is prompt-enforced only; a malformed header is spoken unchecked, which is a Law 7 hole in the one place the governor exists to close; `anxiety.last_call_abandoned` is in the bundle but not consumed by `Governor.__init__`.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.4.6 (recall gaps 2 to 4; retell once per call; never list), 5.4.7 (three anxiety triggers including last call abandoned), 1.2 law 7.
  - Fix: Make the code the single source: add time-driven `phase` updates in a `tick(t_s)` method, the RETELL exit, and an `owed_recall_gaps` line in the operator note after 4:00; remove the recall-gap precondition on retell and widen the window to 2:00 to `close_begin_s`; delete the dead `grades` check and state that the never-list is enforced by the prompt, `forone.core.guard` and the offline suite; treat a malformed header like a violation (re-request once, then speak and log); pass `last_call_abandoned` into `enter_anxiety("abandoned")` and add that value to `CallMetrics.anxiety_mode`.
- **minor** (4.10 "the result updates the `call_turn` event's payload with `verbatim_text`" and 9.3 "either satisfies this domain, the data domain chooses"; 4.9 ""Type" mode sends `text.turn {text}` over the data channel; the worker injects it as the learner turn without STT"): Both are already decided elsewhere and this document contradicts them: 04 D2 appends a separate `call_turn_verbatim` event joined on `(call_id, turn_index)` (nothing updates an event), and 01 serves typed onboarding from `api` (`POST /v1/onboarding/{id}/messages`, one streamed Sonnet call), not from a LiveKit room. Two typed-onboarding implementations and two verbatim shapes is wasted work for this team.
  - Evidence: 04-learner-model-and-data.md D2; 01-system-topology.md §3.6 first-sound row and §7 week 3 ("talk on the minimal worker, type through `api`"); 05 §4.1 already consumes `verbatim.turn_completed`.
  - Fix: Adopt `call_turn_verbatim` (append) in 4.10 and the payload types, with `turn_index` naming matching 04; delete `text.turn` and `fact_card.*` typed handling from the worker (keep `fact_card` edits for the talk path only) and reference 01's typed route.
- **minor** (4.10 Deletion windows: "S3 lifecycle on the `ephemeral/` prefix at 1 day" versus the upload path "`forone-voice/learners/{lid}/calls/{callId}/turn-{n}.ogg`"): The lifecycle backstop applies to a prefix nothing is written under. Turn objects for storage-off learners live at `learners/{lid}/calls/...`, so if `call/verbatim-turn` and `audio/expire` both fail (api outage, Inngest incident) the audio persists until someone notices; 07's `privacy/verify` also scans "every S3 object under an `ephemeral/` prefix".
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.4.11 and 12.5.2 ("in any case within 15 minutes"); 07-security Flow B.
  - Fix: Write storage-off turn audio under `learners/{lid}/ephemeral/calls/{callId}/turn-{n}.ogg` and storage-on audio under `learners/{lid}/calls/...`; the recorder picks the prefix from `consent.voice_storage` at upload time; a consent flip mid-call moves nothing (the call is treated as storage-off at `call.ended`, as 4.10 says, so the full recording is simply not written).
- **minor** (4.7 Talkback: "open talkback STT stream (Soniox, languages [ar, en], code-switch on)" on S-down; 4.7.1 step 2 "Timeout 800 ms; on timeout Salma says, in Arabic, "say it again, slower""; 4.7.3 latency budget): Opening a vendor websocket on S-down puts 200 to 500 ms of connection setup on the critical path of a 1 to 2 s hold, and a short press can release before the stream is ready; the 800 ms Haiku timeout (structured output, 30 to 60 output tokens) will fire on a meaningful share of asks and the "say it again" fallback turns a latency blip into a failed interaction. The cost saved by closing the stream is about $0.013 per call.
  - Evidence: 4.7.3's own figures (Haiku 400 to 700 ms before the first output token at p50); Soniox $0.002/min × 6.5 min; PRD-v2 15 talkback under 2 s.
  - Fix: Open the talkback stream at job start alongside the host stream and gate frames by the S state (cost +$0.013); set the resolver timeout to 1.5 s and play a 300 ms Salma filler clip ("لحظة") at 700 ms so the 2 s budget is felt as a pause, not a failure; keep `unresolved` logging.
- **minor** (4.6 "It replays the host's last line from the client's audio buffer (no vendor call, no delay)"; 4.11 Echo row "the client additionally mutes the mic track for 150 ms after each host sentence starts when no earphones are detected"; 4.5 cut-off rule): Capturing a remote WebRTC track into a replayable buffer on iOS Safari (MediaRecorder or an AudioWorklet on a remote MediaStream) is unreliable and is work the client domain has not planned (06 only registers the handler). The 150 ms client mute cannot be timed (the client learns of a sentence start only from a caption message that arrives after the audio) and it defeats barge-in and the cut-off signal. The cut-off rule as written (any learner speech within 700 ms of host onset) will count speaker echo as cut-offs on phones without earphones and inflate `p` to 2.5 s for the call.
  - Evidence: 06-client-and-delivery.md §4 (Media Session handoff registers "previous track replays the host's last line" with no buffer design); voice-speech lens on iOS audio; 4.5's own interruption threshold.
  - Fix: Replay from the worker: keep the last host line's PCM in memory and re-play it on the `host` track on `replay.host_line` (the client sends the message only). Delete the client-side mute. Gate cut-off detection on the same 0.5 s / 2-word threshold as interruptions.
- **minor** (4.8 Captions: "the client does not even subscribe to the worker's learner transcription events"): AgentSession publishes user and agent transcriptions to the room by default; "not subscribing" still ships the learner's words to the phone, which is the PRD 5.4.8 rule by the letter, and leaks them to anything else in the room. Disabling must be a worker setting, not a client choice.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 5.4.8 ("The learner's own words are never captioned live"); LiveKit room output options.
  - Fix: Set the session's room output to disable user transcription publishing (and agent transcription, since captions go over `forone.ui` with the recast span) and add a `call-bench` check that no `lk.transcription` stream carries a learner segment.
- **minor** (6. Cost table: "Host LLM output | 25 turns × 60 tokens (header plus text) = 1.5k × $10/M | $0.015"; "Call (main and round two) $0.16 holds at ... $0.14 to $0.16 with Inworld or Gemini"; "capacity is 8 calls per task ... 1 warm task per environment"): With the JSON header the output is about 90 tokens per turn ($0.022, or $0.011 with a sparse header). The Inworld/Gemini claim does not follow from the table: $0.11 (live, low bound) + $0.05 (round two) = $0.16, so the range is $0.16 to $0.17, not $0.14 to $0.16; the PRD line is met only at the very bottom of the estimate. Capacity: with `min_tasks: 1` and 8 calls per task, the ninth concurrent call waits for a Fargate task to start (minutes), which is a connect-budget failure at the beta's stated peak of 15.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 14.5 ($0.16 call line); section 6 arithmetic; 01 §7 one-engineer row ("fixed 2 call-worker tasks, about 16 concurrent calls").
  - Fix: Recompute the output line for the chosen header; state the Inworld/Gemini case as $0.16 to $0.17 and the ElevenLabs case as $0.21 to $0.22 so the [OPEN] in section 10 carries the right delta; set `min_tasks: 2` for the beta (about $70/month) and leave autoscaling to P1.
- **minor** (D13 and 4.13: "The 30-clip test and the 50-call test run from one tool, `tools/call-bench`, against the staging worker"; 4.13 "50 scripted calls ... from a headless LiveKit client that plays Milestone 0 learner turns"): 01 and 08 already split this: `tools/vendor-lab` (throwaway scripts on vendor SDKs, week 1) for the 30 clips and the voice tests, `tools/call-bench` for the 50 calls; 09 owns the caller profiles. Routing 30 audio files through a WebRTC worker to test four batch/streaming APIs is a week of plumbing for a one-day script. The headless WebRTC caller is itself a sizeable tool; the Agents SDK can be driven in-process with audio frames pushed into the session input, which is enough for latency and governor measurement.
  - Evidence: 01-system-topology.md §7 week 1 and week 2; 08-cost-model-and-vendors.md `vendor-lab` row; 09-evaluation §4.2.5 suites `calls-50` and the caller profiles; PRD-v2 10.14 ("before the beta build's second week").
  - Fix: Replace D13 with: `tools/vendor-lab recognizers` (week 1, scripts) and `tools/call-bench calls` (week 2, in-process audio injection; the WebRTC-path `connect_ms` measured separately by hand on real phones). Keep the shared report directory 09 already references.
- **minor** (4.4 and 4.3 (the turn path from `@moves` parse to TTS); 5. Failure table row "Host LLM slow ... a slightly longer pause, filled by one backchannel at 1.2 s"): Two things the sibling documents require are absent from the turn path: 07's output guard on every host and Salma line (`forone.core.guard`, budgeted at under 20 ms) is never called before TTS; and the turn machine has no backchannel in `THINKING`, so the failure table's promise that a slow model is "filled by one backchannel" is not implemented (backchannels fire only in `LEARNER_SPEAKING`).
  - Evidence: 07-security-privacy-and-compliance.md §3 budgets ("the output guard on a host line must add under 20 ms") and `forone.core.guard`; 4.3 turn machine.
  - Fix: Insert `guard.check_host_line(text)` between the governor's `check()` and the TTS flush (fail → re-request once, then the package line); add `THINKING ─(1.2 s, no audio yet)─► FILLER clip ─► THINKING` to the turn machine with a separate 2 to 3 clip set ("hmm", a breath) so it never reads as a floor-taking word.
- **minor** (4.14 vendors.yaml and 4.13 30-clip metrics; 4.11 Earphones row): The talkback path's recognizer is tested only indirectly: the 30 clips are English learner speech with at least 6 code-switch spans, and the pass line is "Arabic spans transcribed as Arabic". Nothing measures whether Soniox turns a Saudi-dialect ask ("وش أقول line-up بالإنجليزي") into text the resolver can use, which is the whole talkback feature and Salma's onboarding intake. Separately, when the learner uses Bluetooth earphones with the mic, the audio session drops to the hands-free profile (narrowband, 8 to 16 kHz) for both directions; the 30 clips were not captured that way, so the recognizer winner may be chosen on audio the beta will rarely see.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.14 and 5.4.5; the 4.13 metric table; voice-speech lens ("Deepgram's [code-switching] covers 10 languages and not Arabic").
  - Fix: Add a 20-ask talkback fixture (recorded by two M0 learners on their phones, half through AirPods) with the expected lemma, scored as exact-lemma resolution rate through the full 4.7.1 path (target 0.8), and add 6 of the 30 clips re-captured through Bluetooth earphones; report both in the week-1 table and suggest wired earphones in the one-time PRD 5.4.1 hint if the Bluetooth numbers are bad.

Missing:
- A statement of which end-of-turn clock PRD 15 means and a gate on the learner-felt `silence_to_sound_ms`; today only the favourable clock is gated.
- Verification of every LiveKit Agents API claim against a pinned SDK version (option names, manual turn mode, audio versus text turn detector, preemptive generation, publishing a second audio track, disabling transcription output), scheduled in week 1 with a written result.
- Ownership and schedule for the pre-rendered assets the design depends on: opening-line clips (two variants per episode), backchannel clips per voice, `talkback_intro_ar`, `salma_intro_1/2_ar`, crisis clips, and the ar→en reverse index.
- Worker-to-`api` authentication and the per-call key, as 01 and 07 specify (per-call JWT in dispatch metadata, HKDF call key, `media_objects.key_derivation`).
- Anthropic rate-limit and tier check for 15 concurrent calls (about 60 requests/min with 6 to 10k-token prefixes; cached tokens and ITPM accounting), and the Soniox/TTS concurrency contracts tied to a date; only the TTS/STT side is in section 10.
- Idempotency of `POST /v1/calls` (double tap creates two rooms and two `calls` rows) and what the client does if the LiveKit token (10-minute TTL in 01) expires during an 8-minute call plus reconnect.
- Handling of `stop_reason: max_tokens` on the host reply (the 120-token cap with a 60-token header), and `cache_read_input_tokens` logging per turn so a silent cache miss is visible.
- An onboarding-specific endpointing design for Arabic learner turns (no supported text turn model; VAD plus fixed delay) and a reply-latency figure for Salma at A3.
- A talkback ask fixture and a Bluetooth-earphone capture condition in the recognizer test; a measured "incomplete" rate of the turn detector on M0 learner transcripts.
- Abuse and spend controls on the call path beyond the 12-minute watchdog: reference to 07's `forone.core.costguard` per-learner daily spend and the invite gate on `POST /v1/onboarding/start` (anonymous rooms cost about $0.10 each).
- The behaviour of `audio/expire` and `learner/purge-audio` when `api` is unreachable and events are spooled (the 15-minute clock still runs; the spool ingestion by `retention/daily` is too late), and how the spool file is encrypted and read back.
- A per-turn latency breakdown in `CallTurnPayload` for every stage of 4.15 (STT final, first token, header parsed, TTS first byte, vendor RTT), not only `first_token_ms` and `tts_first_byte_ms`, so the 50-call test can say which stage missed.

Strengths:
- D9: uploading and verbatim-transcribing each learner turn during the call is the right mechanism for both the 90-second notes and the 15-minute deletion window, and it matches 05 D1 exactly; keep it in P0 as the document says.
- D2: declare-then-speak with a code governor, one re-request and `violations` as a tracked quality metric is the correct way to make PRD 5.4.6 and Law 7 enforceable without delaying first audio; the fix is the header's size, not the idea.
- D4 and D11: pre-rendered backchannels and the pre-rendered opening line remove a model round trip from the two moments where latency is most felt; cheap, correct, keep (once ownership is fixed).
- The failure table in section 5, with a column for what the learner sees and what evidence survives, and the honest cost statement that ElevenLabs on the call breaks the $0.16 line, are what a design review wants to see; section 10's [OPEN] items are real owner decisions with dates.
- The client/worker message protocol (4.12) and the event payload types are concrete enough for the client domain to build against today, and 06 already references them.
- The explicit "too much for one to two engineers" cut list and the "do not cut" list show the right instinct; they need to go further, but the structure is right.
- D15's crisis protocol with two independent triggers and fixed, counsel-reviewed lines is the correct shape for PRD 7.9 and SB 243 logging.

## review:skeptic:04-learner-model-and-data

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (4.1.2 DDL and 4.1.3 Idempotency: "Uniqueness is (learner_id, event_id); the primary key includes occurred_at only because Postgres requires the partition key in it." together with 4.1.4 "occurred_at is replaced by recorded_at"): The idempotency guarantee does not exist as written. On a range-partitioned table Postgres cannot enforce unique (learner_id, event_id); the only enforceable key is the PK (learner_id, event_id, occurred_at). The skew rule in 4.1.4 rewrites occurred_at on the first arrival, so a later replay of the same event_id with its original timestamp (the offline queue, the worker spool, an Inngest retry) has a different PK, is not a conflict, and is inserted twice. The D3 promise "duplicates are ignored" and the active-day derivation ("a replay never double-counts a play", topology 4.x) both break.
  - Evidence: Postgres partitioning rule: every unique constraint on a partitioned table must include all partition-key columns. The two statements quoted are mutually exclusive; the doc already admits the PK is wider than the dedupe key. A duplicate with a corrected timestamp lands in a different (or the same) partition with a different key either way.
  - Fix: Never rewrite occurred_at. Keep the producer's value as the key, add occurred_at_corrected (nullable) for ordering, and compute replay order as coalesce(occurred_at_corrected, occurred_at). Alternatively, add an unpartitioned events_ids(learner_id, event_id) table with a real unique constraint written in the same statement (one extra index, no partition problem). State which one and update D3 and flow A.
- **blocker** (4.1.2 DDL: "seq bigint generated always as identity" on "create table events (...) partition by range (occurred_at)"): This DDL does not execute on the Postgres the project runs. Identity columns on partitioned tables arrived in Postgres 17; the topology's compose file pins pgvector/pgvector:pg16 and no RDS engine version is stated. Week 1's first migration fails.
  - Evidence: 01-system-topology.md line 850: `postgres: {image: pgvector/pgvector:pg16}`. PG16 raises "identity columns are not supported on partitioned tables".
  - Fix: Either pin Postgres 17 everywhere (RDS supports it) and say so in 4.13, or declare `seq bigint not null default nextval('events_seq')` with an explicit sequence. Also add an index on (learner_id, seq): versions.bump runs `select max(seq) where learner_id=:l` on every write and the only index starting with learner_id orders by occurred_at.
- **blocker** (4.11 and D15: "forone-voice/{learner_id}/ephemeral/calls/{call_id}/turn-{index}.ogg ... S3 lifecycle: expire 1 day (backstop to audio/expire)" and 4.11 "the voice bucket has the single lifecycle rule above"): The lifecycle backstop cannot be expressed. S3 lifecycle rules filter by a literal key prefix or by object tags; there is no wildcard. With the learner id first, matching `*/ephemeral/` needs one rule per learner (hard limit 1,000 rules per bucket, and 10,000 learners is the stated scale). The last line of defence for the 15-minute deletion promise (PRD-v2 5.4.11, 12.5.2) is therefore a rule nobody can write. The topology (01 line 648) has the same mid-path `{ephemeral/}` layout, so both are wrong together.
  - Evidence: AWS S3 lifecycle configuration: Filter supports Prefix, Tag, ObjectSize and And-combinations; no glob. Bucket limit: 1,000 lifecycle rules.
  - Fix: Put the segment at the bucket root: `forone-voice/ephemeral/{learner_id}/calls/{call_id}/turn-{n}.ogg` with one rule on prefix `ephemeral/`, or tag ephemeral objects `ephemeral=true` on PUT and filter the rule by tag. Per-learner prefix listing for export and purge then needs two prefixes (`{learner_id}/` and `ephemeral/{learner_id}/`); say so in 4.12.2 step 3. Reconcile 01 and 03's key layout at the same time (see the separate key-layout issue).
- **blocker** (4.5.1 Priors: "p0 = clip(sigmoid((size_est - rank) / (0.35 * size_est)), 0.02, 0.98) (the pipeline document states this shape; it is owned here)"): The pipeline document states a different shape and says 04 adopted it. 02-generation-pipeline.md 4.5 defines `sigmoid((1.25*size_est - rank)/(0.10*size_est))` (0.92 at rank = size_est, 0.5 at 1.25x) and its week-1 coverage self-checks (a 97%-top-3000 script scores 0.958 and passes) are computed on that curve. With 04's curve a learner at size_est 3,000 gets p=0.5 at rank 3,000 and about 0.73 at rank 2,000, so a top-3,000 script scores far below the 95% audio floor and the fitter rejects every script. 02 section 9 item 3 also says the config keys prior.known_fraction / prior.center / prior.width move to 04; 04's 4.15 has none of them.
  - Evidence: 02-generation-pipeline.md lines 474-478 and 922; 04 4.5.1 and 4.15. Arithmetic: sigmoid((3000-2000)/1050) = sigmoid(0.95) = 0.72.
  - Fix: Replace 4.5.1 with 02's saturating prior verbatim, add the three config keys to 4.15, and make the week-1 test ("same numbers within 0.5 points" against the kit's coverage checker) the acceptance test for the shared curve.
- **blocker** (4.10 Claims DDL and D13: "claims table owned here, written only by the assessment domain's claim gate"): The owner's table does not fit the writer's model. 05-assessment-and-after-session.md 4.8.3 defines a status lifecycle candidate -> passed | failed(failed_rule) -> shown -> retracted(reason) with notice_pending, keeps failed candidates 90 days for the audit, and its DDL (line 844) has status, failed_rule, retracted_reason, notice_pending. 04's claims has none of these: no status column at all, so a failed candidate cannot be stored, and 'retracted' is modelled as a separate claim_invalidations table keyed by rebuild_run_id, which cannot represent 05's `retracted(reason="learner_disputed")` from memory/correct. Two domains will ship two incompatible tables named claims.
  - Evidence: 05 lines 652, 783, 791, 844-853 versus 04 4.10.
  - Fix: Adopt 05's DDL as the single claims table (status, failed_rule, retracted_reason, notice_pending, gate jsonb, learner_model_version), drop claim_invalidations, and keep 04's guarantee as a trigger or a check: a row with shown_at set may change status only to retracted and is never deleted. Keep rebuild_runs.
- **major** (3.3 flow D: "replays sessions in order by calling the assessment domain's pure functions over the stored verbatim turns and chat messages (no new vendor calls: transcripts are events)" and 6: "rebuild about 2 s per session of history"): The analyst and memory keeper are Haiku calls, not pure functions; PRD-v2 8.5 says a rebuild happens precisely when "the analyst, edit classifier or measures change version", which means the analyst must be re-run. 05 4.14 does re-run analyst.full at about $0.004 a session with a $50 ceiling and 5 learners in parallel, and explicitly does NOT re-run the memory keeper so learner-confirmed facts and deletions survive. 04's rebuild re-runs all nine store writers including facts, claims a cost of zero and a duration of 2 s a session, and defines its own function learner/rebuild beside 05's assessment/rebuild. Two rebuild runners with different semantics, and 04's cost and time numbers are off by one to two orders of magnitude.
  - Evidence: 05 4.14 steps 2 and 5; 05 timeline line 153 (memory keeper 10-25 s, analyst similar). 100 sessions x ~20 s of model calls is 30+ minutes, not 4.
  - Fix: Delete learner/rebuild; keep the shadow-schema mechanics as a library that 05's assessment/rebuild calls. State that facts, interests, goals, canon and claims with shown_at are carried over, not regenerated. Replace the 2 s figure with 05's per-session cost and time, and cut the P0 runner to a script (the doc's own one-engineer cut) since 05 already made the same cut.
- **major** (3.3 flow D: "replays learner edits at their positions in the log" with 4.3.6 `fact_id uuid primary key`, 4.3.7 `goal_id`, 4.3.9 `canon_id`, 4.10 `claim_id`): Learner edits reference row ids (`fact_card_edit {fact_id}`, `wrong_memory_flag {fact_id|canon_id}`, `notebook_edit {item_id}`, `claim_shown {claim_id}`). Only structure_observations.id is deterministic (uuid5). A rebuild that regenerates facts gets new fact_ids, so every replayed edit points at a row that no longer exists: deletions, 'don't bring up' flags and ground-truth corrections are silently lost, which is the exact failure the trust contract (1.3 #7) forbids. Even 05's 'carry facts over' design needs stable ids for canon and goals.
  - Evidence: 4.1.1 payloads for fact_card_edit, wrong_memory_flag, notebook_edit; 4.3.2 is the only deterministic id in the document.
  - Fix: Mint every store row id as uuid5(learner_id, store, quote_event_id, normalized key) so a replay reproduces it, or never regenerate learner-governed stores (facts, goals, out_in_world, canon, claims) in a rebuild and say so in D14. Add a test: replay a learner with 3 edits, assert the edits land.
- **major** (4.9 Compiled briefs: "teacher | The pipeline's TeacherInput exactly (02-generation-pipeline.md 4.3), compiled here" and the volatile tail "last_14_days, energy, claims_passed"): TeacherInput cannot be compiled from the DDL in this document. 02 4.3 requires interests[].entities, facts_usable[].entities, facts_usable[].session_at, taste.swap_count_30d, onboarding_session_id and last_14_days; 04's interests and life_facts tables have no entities column, taste has a lifetime swap_n not a 30-day count, and last_14_days (05 writes session_summaries in its finish step) is never named as a source anywhere in 04. 05 section 9 item 4 also expects briefs.compile('teacher') to read session_summaries. A developer starting week 2 cannot write compile('teacher') from these three documents.
  - Evidence: 02 lines 222-236 and 922; 05 line 103 and 989; 04 4.3.5, 4.3.8, 4.9.
  - Fix: Add an entities jsonb column to interests and life_facts (filled by the memory keeper's normalizer, already present as topic_key), compute swap_count_30d from swap events in compile, name session_summaries (owned by 05) as the source of last_14_days with its columns, and put the full TeacherInput field-to-table map in 4.9 as a table.
- **major** (4.13 Roles and RLS: "forone_purge (delete on everything, used only inside learner/delete...)" with 4.1.2 "create policy events_by_learner on events using (learner_id = current_setting('forone.learner_id', true)::uuid); create policy events_service on events to forone_workflows using (true)"): RLS applies to DELETE. The only permissive policies are the per-learner one (needs forone.learner_id set) and a service policy granted to forone_workflows only. learner/delete runs as forone_purge, which the document never gives a policy or BYPASSRLS, and never says it sets forone.learner_id. Every `delete from <table> where learner_id=...` in 4.12.2 step 5 therefore deletes zero rows and reports success; the account is marked deleted with all its data still in place. The reverse hole: `current_setting(..., true)` returns '' when the GUC was set to an empty string, and ''::uuid raises, so "a missing setting yields zero rows" is only half true.
  - Evidence: Postgres RLS semantics: without a matching policy, a non-owner non-BYPASSRLS role sees no rows for any command. 4.12.2 verifies S3 prefixes are empty but never verifies row counts.
  - Fix: Give forone_purge an explicit `using (true)` policy on every learner table (or `alter role forone_purge bypassrls`) and make step 5 assert `rowcount > 0` where rows are expected and record the per-table counts in deletion_ledger. Wrap the GUC read in nullif(..., '').
- **major** (4.1.3 "lets the server reject ids more than 7 days old" and 4.1.2 partitions starting at events_2026_11, versus section 7 week 1: "Import the 15 Milestone 0 learner files and Scribe transcripts as source='import' events"): The week-1 acceptance test cannot run. Milestone 0 events are dated 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-01; there is no October partition (inserts fail with 'no partition of relation events found for row') and the 7-day UUIDv7 age check rejects every imported id. Nothing in the document exempts source='import'.
  - Evidence: 4.1.2 partition list; 4.1.3 age rule; 19.1 dates; section 7 week 1 test column.
  - Fix: Create events_2026_10 (and a catch-all default partition for safety) in the baseline migration, and state that the age check applies only to source in ('web','worker'). Give tools/m0_import.py its own id-minting rule (uuid5 of file path and row).
- **major** (3.3 flow B and 4.3.0: apply_after_pass "in one transaction ... then versions.bump" with 4.2 bump taking `select lm_version from learners ... for update` as its first statement): The per-learner lock is taken at the end of the transaction, after all store reads and the Beta/FSRS arithmetic were done on an unlocked snapshot. Two after-passes for the same learner (main call and round two close minutes apart; a retried Inngest step racing its predecessor; a learner edit on word_bank.kept during a pass) both read the same word_bank row, both compute a+delta, and the second commit overwrites the first: evidence is lost without any error. The document's own failure table ("Version bump contention") claims "none" as the degraded mode.
  - Evidence: 4.2 bump() code; 4.3.0 order 'facts, words, ..., counts; then versions.bump'; nothing in 04 or 05 sets an Inngest concurrency key per learner for session/after-pass.
  - Fix: Take the learners row lock as the first statement of apply_after_pass and apply_learner_edit (move it out of bump, or call a lock() first), and additionally set Inngest `concurrency: {key: event.data.learner_id, limit: 1}` on session/after-pass. Rewrite the failure-table row to describe the lock wait and the p99 (an edit can wait up to the pass's 1-3 s).
- **major** (6 Cost: "Per-session share | under $0.002 | under $0.003" with "RDS instance | db.t4g.medium Multi-AZ, about $120/month"): The beta per-session figure is wrong by about thirty times. 100 learners x 20 sessions = 2,000 sessions a month; $120 of RDS alone is $0.06 a session, plus S3 and the embedding call. The scale column is right ($600 / 200,000 = $0.003). The topology deliberately reports fixed infrastructure as a separate line so it does not trip the $1.00 stop line, which is the right move, but this document presents the fixed cost as if it were variable and tiny.
  - Evidence: Arithmetic above; 01 line 912 ([DECISION] on what the 16.1 number includes).
  - Fix: Split the row into variable per session (S3 requests, embedding: under $0.002) and fixed per month amortized at the actual beta learner count ($0.06 at 100 learners, $0.003 at 10,000), matching 01's reporting rule.
- **major** (4.5.3 Scheduler: `return (1 + t / (9 * card.stability)) ** -1  # FSRS forgetting curve` and `_s.next_interval(new.stability)`; D7 "py-fsrs ... FSRS-4.5 defaults"): The hand-written retrievability is the FSRS-4.5 curve, but current py-fsrs (v4/v5+) implements FSRS-5/6 with a different curve ((1 + FACTOR*t/S)^DECAY, DECAY -0.5, FACTOR 19/81, with per-card decay in FSRS-6) and different default weights. Scheduling with the library's weights while computing 'due' with a foreign curve means the 0.85 threshold is systematically wrong (at t = S the two curves differ by about 10 points). `Scheduler.next_interval` is not a public method of py-fsrs; `Card.get_retrievability(now)` is. The fractional-review hack then recomputes due from an interval function that does not exist.
  - Evidence: py-fsrs README and source (open-spaced-repetition/py-fsrs): public API is Scheduler.review_card and Card.get_retrievability; weights are FSRS-6 21-parameter defaults.
  - Fix: Use card.get_retrievability(at) for R, pin the py-fsrs version in the document, and implement the weight w as a probabilistic review (apply the full update with probability w, logged, deterministic by seeded hash of event_id so rebuilds reproduce it) or drop weights for the beta and let 05's four grades drive it. Also resolve who maps evidence to ratings: 05 4.x already defines again/hard/good/easy per evidence kind; 04 4.5.2 defines a second mapping with weights. One owner.
- **major** (4.9 `briefs` cache "keyed by (learner_id, role, learner_model_version, scope_id)" and D12 "A new version invalidates by construction; no TTL logic" alongside the retention job "delete rows older than 14 days or more than 20 versions behind"): The cache has a near-zero hit rate by construction. The version bumps after every after-pass and every learner edit; the teacher compiles once per episode (after the pass, new version), the host brief is compiled at episode time and again at call start "if the version moved" (it always has, after the pass), and the proof compiles weekly. Nine roles, a table, a GC rule and a 'cache miss storm' failure row for a cache that saves a 300-500 ms query a handful of times a week. This is build time for nothing, in a five-week plan.
  - Evidence: 4.9 last paragraph; 6 latency commitments (compile 300-500 ms); 02 already persists the teacher input and brief per episode (brief_ref) for audit.
  - Fix: Cut the briefs table. compile() computes on read and the consumer stores the compiled body with the artifact it fed (02's brief_ref, 03's call bundle), which is where the audit trail belongs. Keep the stable-prefix ordering rule (that is what prompt caching actually needs).
- **major** (4.1.1 taxonomy 'Who' column and 4.1.2 `source text not null check (source in ('web','worker','wf','api','import'))`; 3.3 flow A "validates each payload against its type schema"): Nothing binds an event type to its permitted producer. A learner's own browser can POST a `call_turn_verbatim` with fabricated text, a `claim_shown`, a `margin_note`, a `crisis_flag` or an `lm_write` through /v1/events; the quote check (4.7.1) accepts verbatim turns as quote sources, so a fabricated verbatim turn passes a fabricated life fact into the model, and a fabricated `recording`/`call_turn` can feed claims. RLS limits the blast radius to the learner's own model, but the evidence chain the claim gate relies on ('clean evidence', PRD-v2 10.10 rule 4) is only as trustworthy as the registry's producer check, which is not specified.
  - Evidence: 4.1.1 lists producers as documentation only; the DDL takes `source` from the request. 01 line 101 says api appends learner and worker events and workflows appends after-session events, but enforcement is not described in either document.
  - Fix: The registry entry per type carries `allowed_sources`; api sets `source` from the authenticated principal (session cookie = web, call JWT = worker, Inngest signature = wf), never from the body, and rejects type/source mismatches with 422. Say it in 4.1.1's rules paragraph and test it in week 1.
- **major** (4.12.1 Export step 8: "email kind export_ready with the presigned URL (7 days)" and D16 "presigned link valid 7 days"): A 7-day presigned S3 URL needs long-lived IAM user credentials. workflows runs on Fargate under a task role; URLs presigned with temporary credentials expire when the credentials do (hours), regardless of the requested expiry. The emailed link dies silently well before the learner opens it, and the retry is a support ticket.
  - Evidence: AWS S3 presigned-URL documentation: with temporary security credentials the URL is valid until the credentials expire, at most 36 h for IAM-user-derived STS tokens and the task role's rotation window on Fargate.
  - Fix: Email a link to `GET /v1/exports/{id}` that requires the learner's session and streams or re-signs for 15 minutes on click. This also removes the only long-lived secret the design would otherwise need. The AES zip (step 6) is a separate problem: Python's zipfile cannot write encrypted archives (pyzipper is needed) and AES zips do not open in macOS Archive Utility or Windows Explorer; a working adult in Riyadh will not install 7-Zip. Prefer the authenticated link over the passphrase and let Muath's open item 7 fall away.
- **major** (4.11 key layout versus 01 line 648 and 03 line 101 ("forone-voice/learners/{lid}/calls/{callId}/{ephemeral/}turn-{n}.ogg"), 02 line 129 ("episodes/{learner_id}/{episode_id}/episode.m4a, episode_feed.m4a, episode_feed.mp3, scene_{n}.m4a"), 06 line 397 ("forone-media/{lid}/episodes/{episodeId}/episode_feed.mp3"); and 01's per-call HKDF key (`media_objects.key_derivation = 'call:{call_id}'`)): Four documents, four key layouts and three file-name sets for the same objects (04: full.m4a, feed.m4a, seg-{n}.m4a; 02: episode.m4a, episode_feed.mp3, scene_{n}.m4a; 06 serves episode_feed.mp3 which 04 never writes). 04 also says every voice object is SSE-C with the learner DEK and export step 5 decrypts 'with the DEK', but 01 D7 encrypts call turns under a per-call HKDF(DEK, call_id) key and the worker never holds the DEK. Export, purge and the orphan audit all read by key and by DEK, so this is not cosmetic: export of call audio fails on every call-turn object.
  - Evidence: Quoted lines above; 01 line 143 and 283.
  - Fix: Declare 04 the owner of the key layout (it already claims it in D15), copy 02's file names and 01's `learners/{lid}` prefix or get 01/02/03/06 to adopt 04's, and add `key_derivation` handling to export step 5 and to search_callbacks' clip reads: derive the per-call key when media_objects.key_derivation is set.
- **major** (4.8 "embedding vector(1536)" and section 10 #3 "the embedding vendor (OpenAI by the topology's default)"): Vendor conflict and avoidable lock-in. 07's processor register, as summarized in 01 line 1009, "excludes OpenAI"; the embedding step would be the only OpenAI call in the system and the only learner-text processor not on the register. Hardcoding 1536 dimensions bakes that vendor into the DDL. And the feature itself is marginal for the beta: D11 says a learner has under 2,000 chunks in year one, and the teacher's callback can be served from show_canon rows and a pg_trgm search over call_turns_v for the first season without any embeddings, vector extension or extra processor.
  - Evidence: 01 line 1009; PRD-v2 14.3 allows a vector index but does not require one in the beta; section 7's one-engineer cut list does not include it.
  - Fix: Move conversation_chunks and search_callbacks to P1 and serve beta callbacks from show_canon plus trigram search. If kept, store `embedding vector` without a typmod (pgvector allows it; index later), pick a vendor on the register, and name it in 4.8.
- **major** (4.11 "versioning off on forone-voice and forone-media (a delete must be a delete)" and 5 failure table (no row for a deletion bug)): One bug in audio/expire, the ephemeral-prefix logic or the purge step erases every kept recording of every affected learner, irreversibly, by design, and the learner paid for exactly that recording ('the tape', the then-and-now). The document protects against RDS loss with 30-day PITR but gives consented voice storage zero recovery window. 'A delete must be a delete' is the 15-minute rule for storage-off audio only; consented audio has no such latency promise.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.5.1 (kept 'until the learner deletes it or their account') versus 12.5.2 (15 min when off). The 15-minute rule never applies to kept objects.
  - Fix: Keep versioning off for the ephemeral prefix (or bucket) and turn it on with a 7-day noncurrent-version expiry for the kept prefixes; make learner/delete and purge-audio delete all versions explicitly (one extra ListObjectVersions call) so the 24 h and 30-day promises still hold. Add the failure row.
- **major** (7 Build plan: week 1 ships "events DDL ..., the type registry and all payload schemas (about 45 types); learner_model_versions; lexicon loaders; word_bank DDL, priors, p_understood_many; py-fsrs wrapper, due_list; CallMeta wired; Alembic baseline; roles" and the M0 import tool and its comparison test): This plan assumes an engineer who does nothing but this domain. 01's week 1 for the same two people is Terraform, CI, monorepo, api skeleton, Inngest, Sentry, Langfuse and the vendor lab; 02, 03 and 06 each have their own week-1 list. Five weeks of this document alone is roughly 25 tables, 45 Pydantic schemas, nine store writers with rebuild variants, a scheduler wrapper, a quote validator with Arabic normalization and trigram matching, nine brief compilers, export, purge, retention jobs, RLS for every table, pgaudit, a shadow-schema rebuild runner, a PITR drill and a PII test. The one-engineer cut (rebuild to P1, research set to P1, no .txt transcripts) removes perhaps three days.
  - Evidence: Section 7 table; 01 section 7 week-1 row; the standing team assumption (one to two engineers plus Muath).
  - Fix: Cut for the beta and say so in section 7: briefs cache table, conversation_chunks and embeddings, claim_invalidations/rebuild_runs (use 05's claims statuses), skill_snapshots, lm_write mirror events, research_transcripts, pgaudit, the weekly pg_dump drift check, the structure avoidance job (05 also cuts it), the fractional-review weighting, and the 20-versions-behind GC. Keep: events, versions, word_bank, structure_observations/map, error_profile, skills, interests, life_facts, goals, taste, show_canon, notebook_counts, consents, claims (05's), export, purge, RLS. That is still a full five weeks.
- **major** (4.3.2 `structure_acc_14d` view: "where observed_at > now() - interval '14 days'" and D8 "windows must be recomputable after a rebuild"; 4.6 taking->stable rule "with the window spanning >= 14 days"): A view anchored on now() cannot replay state transitions at their historical positions, which is the stated reason the window is a query. The stable rule also needs the window span (min and max observed_at), which the view does not return. structures.step(map_row, observations, now) is named as if it takes a time, but nothing it can call accepts one.
  - Evidence: 4.3.2 DDL; 4.6 table; 3.3 flow D.
  - Fix: Replace the view with a SQL function `structure_acc(learner_id, structure_id, as_of timestamptz, days int)` returning contexts, correct, sessions, clean_contexts, first_at, last_at, and call it with the replay cursor during rebuilds and now() in the live path.
- **minor** (4.1.4 Ordering: "if the skew is over events.client_clock_skew_max_s (300 s) and the batch is not an offline replay (X-Forone-Replay: 1 header carrying the queue's original timestamps)" versus 01 line 459 "a skew guard of 24 h" and 06 line 357 "the 24 h window matches the data domain's clock-skew rule"): Three documents, two rules, and the 300-second rule is bypassed by a client-settable header, so it is not a guard at all. 01 also names the fields client_event_id and received_at where 04 has event_id and recorded_at.
  - Evidence: Quoted lines.
  - Fix: One rule: accept occurred_at within 24 h of receipt (the replay window), correct beyond that, no header. Align field names with 01's contract (client_event_id, received_at) or get 01 and 06 to change.
- **minor** (4.7.2 rung (c): "a fact_card_viewed with dwell_ms >= 4000 and no fact_card_edit for that fact in the same visit_id within 10 minutes (confirmed_by='viewed_unedited')" and "applied by a small step at the end of each notebook visit ... when the visit's last event is older than 10 minutes, or the next after-pass does"): The promotion to 'usable' (the rung that lets a host speak the fact) is decided by a client-reported dwell on a card in a scrollable list. If several cards are on screen, all of them accrue dwell while the learner reads one; a learner who leaves the tab open promotes everything. The trigger is also undefined: no timer exists in api; 'the next after-pass' can be days later. This is the one place where PRD-v2 1.3 #1 ('no memory is stated unless confirmed') rests on a UI heuristic.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 8.4.2 allows viewing to count, but the measurement is this document's choice.
  - Fix: Count dwell only for the single expanded or focused card (06 owns the widget; state the requirement in section 9 item 5), require the card to have been scrolled into the centre band, and promote in api when the batch containing the visit's last event arrives (visit_id end marker), not on a timer. Keep 4 s.
- **minor** (4.7.6 Interests decay: "After every pass: salience = salience * exp(-days_since_last_engaged / 21)"): The decayed value is written back and decayed again at the next pass using the full days_since_last_engaged, so decay compounds: after two passes a day apart with 10 days since engagement, salience is multiplied by exp(-10/21) twice instead of exp(-11/21) once.
  - Evidence: Arithmetic on the stated rule.
  - Fix: Store salience_base and last_engaged_at; compute salience on read as base * exp(-days/21) (or recompute from base on write). dormant becomes a predicate, not a column.
- **minor** (4.7.3: "sensitive_usable_until = the Sunday ending the ISO week of said_at (Saudi weeks start Sunday; use the learner's local calendar)"): Self-contradictory: ISO weeks end on Sunday; a week that starts on Sunday ends on Saturday. The two readings differ by a day on the one rule that limits use of health, money, relationship, religion and politics facts.
  - Evidence: ISO 8601 week definition versus the sentence's own parenthetical.
  - Fix: Define it as said_at local date + 6 days (a rolling seven days, which is what 02's 'session_at >= as_of - 7 days' already implements), and drop the calendar-week language.
- **minor** (4.12.2 step 7: "email kind account_deleted to the address captured at step 1 (the only PII the function still holds, in the step's input)" and D17 "a salted hash of the email" versus `deletion_ledger.email_hmac`): Step inputs and event payloads persist in Inngest Cloud (EU) for the run's retention; 01 promised Inngest holds 'ids only'. A salted hash cannot be looked up later (each row would need its salt), so the ledger's stated purpose (audit a re-signup against the 30-day window) needs the HMAC the DDL names, not the salted hash D17 names.
  - Evidence: 01 line 1019 (Inngest: ids and timestamps only); D17 versus 4.12.2 DDL.
  - Fix: Send the deletion email synchronously from api at step 1 ('we started; done within 24 h') and a second from a step that reads nothing but a token; pass no email through Inngest. Use HMAC with a server key everywhere and fix D17's wording.
- **minor** (4.8 "exact search is under 5 ms" and 6 "search_callbacks under 5 ms" with "vector(1536)" and "exact_search_max_chunks = 20000"): A brute-force cosine scan over 20,000 x 1,536 floats is about 120 MB of reads and tens to hundreds of milliseconds in Postgres, not 5 ms; even the year-one 2,000 chunks is 12 MB and closer to 10-30 ms. Not a budget problem (the teacher runs in batch), just a wrong number that will be quoted.
  - Evidence: Arithmetic: 20,000 x 1,536 x 4 bytes = 123 MB per query.
  - Fix: State 20-50 ms at 2,000 chunks and set exact_search_max_chunks to 5,000, or cut the feature (see the embeddings issue).
- **minor** (4.1.2 `create index events_call on events (call_id, (payload->>'turn_index'))` and the view `(v.payload->>'turn_index')::int = (s.payload->>'turn_index')::int`): The index is on the text expression; the view compares the integer cast, so the planner does not use the index for the join and the per-call join scans by call_id alone. Harmless at 25 turns, but it is the kind of thing the reviewer will have to fix later anyway.
  - Evidence: Expression-index matching requires the same expression.
  - Fix: Index on (call_id, ((payload->>'turn_index')::int)) or promote turn_index to a real column on call events.
- **minor** (4.12.4 research_transcripts: "names replaced by {NAME}" and PII table "Pseudonymous research ... names replaced; opt-in"): Replacing the display name and the people in life_facts leaves employer, city, team, dated plans and the learner's own distinctive phrasing in the transcript; calling this 'anonymized' in the consent copy (PRD-v2 12.5.5) is a legal claim the mechanism does not support. The document already pushes the research set to P1 for one engineer.
  - Evidence: 4.12.4 text; PRD-v2 12.5.5 wording 'anonymized transcripts'.
  - Fix: Make research_transcripts P1 outright, and when built, run the analyst's entity list (02's allowed-entity set already exists) through a redaction pass and have 07 approve the word 'pseudonymized' instead of 'anonymized'.
- **minor** (4.7.1 `QUOTE_SOURCES = ("call_turn_verbatim", "chat_message", "onboarding_answer", "small_door_voice_verbatim")` and the public API `verify_quote(fact_id: UUID)`): small_door_voice_verbatim is not in the taxonomy (4.1.1); the small-door voice note's verbatim text has no event type, so day-after facts from the small door can never be stored. The public signature takes a fact_id while the implementation takes (quote, quote_event_id); the editor's check 4 (02) calls it with a fact_id.
  - Evidence: 4.1.1 table versus 4.7.1 code; 3.4 signature.
  - Fix: Either emit small-door verbatim as call_turn_verbatim with mode='voice_note' (the call_turn payload already has mode) or add the type to 4.1.1. Provide both signatures or have the fact_id form look up quote and event id.

Missing:
- The Postgres major version (17 is required by the DDL as written; the topology's compose file pins 16) and the RDS engine version, stated once in 4.13.
- Deterministic ids for learner-governed rows (facts, goals, out_in_world, canon, claims) so replayed edits resolve after a rebuild; and a statement of which stores a rebuild regenerates versus carries over, agreed with 05 4.14.
- The source of TeacherInput's last_14_days (05's session_summaries), entities on interests and facts, swap_count_30d and onboarding_session_id: the field-to-table map for every brief role, which is what a developer needs in week 2.
- A producer-per-type enforcement rule in the registry and the rule that `source` comes from the authenticated principal, never the request body.
- An Inngest concurrency key per learner on session/after-pass and the lock-first ordering in apply_after_pass; the document assumes exactly one writer per learner without arranging it.
- An October 2026 partition and an import exemption from the 7-day id age check, without which the week-1 Milestone 0 test cannot run.
- A single owner for the evidence-to-FSRS-rating mapping (05 4.x defines one, 04 4.5.2 defines another with weights) and for the rebuild runner (04 learner/rebuild versus 05 assessment/rebuild).
- Store-DDL migration policy beyond 'bump a code version and rebuild': adding a column to word_bank for 10,000 learners should not require re-running Haiku over every session; say which changes are additive migrations and which are rebuilds.
- Row-count verification in the deletion ledger (deleted rows per table) alongside the S3 prefix check; today only objects are verified.
- A recovery window for consented voice audio (versioning with a short noncurrent expiry on kept prefixes) and the matching all-versions delete in purge.
- The word_bank sizing rule: when a row is created (on first exposure, or only on evidence) and what p_understood_many returns for units with no row (the prior, computed in memory) stated as a contract the fitter can test.
- pgbouncer or connection-pool guidance for `set local forone.learner_id`: with transaction pooling and any autocommit read outside the request transaction, RLS silently returns zero rows; one sentence in 4.13 prevents a week of debugging.
- Which Postgres role Alembic runs as (table owner bypasses RLS and the events trigger's revoke list does not include it) and that the owner is never used at runtime.

Strengths:
- One append-only events table with monthly partitions and derived stores, no stream, no second source of truth: exactly the right scale of ambition for two engineers, and the rejected-alternatives table says why.
- call_turn_verbatim as a second event joined in a view (D2) keeps the log honest where the topology and 03 would have mutated a row; 01 has already accepted it.
- verify_quote as a database lookup against the verbatim event, with an Arabic-aware normalizer and a hard 'never from the streaming transcript' rule, is the strongest single defence in the system against the LongMemEval/HaluMem failure the personalization lens documents; the 'dropped and counted, never retried' rule is the right stance.
- The per-learner integer learner_model_version with high-water mark and code versions, carried on briefs, CallMeta, model-produced events and claims, is cheap and gives real traceability from a bad episode back to the state that produced it.
- structure_observations as per-context evidence rows with a deterministic uuid5 id, so claims can open to the exact turn and replays are idempotent; this is the pattern the facts and claims tables should copy.
- Beta(a,b) per unit and surface gives ranges without a second 'confidence' column and makes the frequency prior and the word check one representation; the concentration choice (k=4, two taps outweigh the prior) is sensible and tunable.
- The PII classification as code (forone.learner.pii) used by export, Langfuse scrubbing and tests, and the 30-day backup promise met by RDS retention plus a CI check that the config equals the privacy text's number, are the kind of 'true by configuration' decisions that survive a small team.
- The explicit 'what is cut for one engineer' paragraph and the [OPEN for Muath] list are honest; they just need to cut deeper.

## review:skeptic:05-assessment-and-after-session

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (3.3 'Tables this domain owns: ... claims ...' and 4.16 'create table claims (...)'; 4.14 'assessment/rebuild'): Two incompatible owners and schemas for the same table and the same job. 04-learner-model-and-data.md D13 owns `claims` with different columns and kind names (`words_owned`, `structure_learned`, `fluency`, `accuracy`, `can_do`, `flat`, `worse`, `gate jsonb`, `learner_model_version integer`, `claim_invalidations`), and 04 D14 defines `learner/rebuild` with a `stores_shadow` schema, `rebuild_runs` and 'no new vendor calls'. 05 redefines the DDL with `did_it`, `first_ever`, `word_owned`, `fluency_change`..., `status/failed_rule/retracted_reason`, and a second rebuild function `assessment/rebuild` that creates `word_bank_shadow` etc. and re-runs Haiku per session ($0.004 × sessions). The proof brief, `mark_claim_shown`, the purge order and the export in 04 are written against 04's shape. A developer cannot build either without a decision, and a two-person team cannot afford two rebuild mechanisms.
  - Evidence: 04 §4.10 DDL vs 05 §4.16 DDL; 04 D14 'replays sessions ... (no new vendor calls: transcripts are events)' vs 05 4.14 step 2 're-run analyst.full (Haiku)'; 04 §1213 'the claim gate writes claims through record_claim only'.
  - Fix: Delete 05's `claims` DDL and `assessment/rebuild`. Adopt 04's `claims` and `claim_invalidations` as the single table (one place for kind names: propose 05's finer kinds be the enum, and have 04 adopt them), write only through `record_claim`, and contribute the pure stage functions to 04's `learner/rebuild`. Decide explicitly whether a rebuild re-runs the analyst (vendor cost, non-deterministic) or replays a stored `AnalystOutput`: store every validated `AnalystOutput` as an event (`analyst_result`, ~3 KB) so rebuilds are free and deterministic, and re-run the model only on an analyst-version bump.
- **blocker** (4.1 `await events.update_call_turn(evt.event_id, {...})` and 'The events write is the one allowed late-binding update per call_turn event'; 4.14 step 4 'the event's verbatim_status is set to disputed'): The design mutates event-log rows. The data domain made the log append-only with a trigger guard and decided the verbatim result is a second event `call_turn_verbatim` joined by `(call_id, turn_index)`; the topology's function table says the same ('append call_turn_verbatim through events.append() (04 D2; nothing updates an event)'). 05 treats the append as a fallback and adds a second mutation (`disputed`). As written the code path does not exist.
  - Evidence: 04 D1 'append-only (no UPDATE/DELETE grant, trigger guard)'; 04 D2; 01 row `call/verbatim-turn` line 566.
  - Fix: Rewrite 4.1 to append `call_turn_verbatim` (payload per 04: `call_id, turn_index, text, words[], vendor, disfluencies_kept, low_confidence_reason`) and read through `call_turns_v`. For a disputed quote, append a `quote_disputed {event_id, claim_id}` event and make `openable()` and the candidate generator exclude any evidence event with a dispute row; never set a status on the original event.
- **blocker** (3.2 Diagram A step 4 and 3.4 'update_stores: word bank, structure map, error profile, skills, taste, scheduler grades (one transaction)' then 'memory_keeper' then 'finish: bump learner_model_version'): The store-write contract contradicts the data domain. 04 specifies one `AfterPassResult` handed to `stores.apply_after_pass(result)` at the end of stage 7, which validates, applies all nine stores, runs the scheduler, bumps the version and writes `learner_model_versions` in one transaction. 05 writes stores in step 4, writes facts in step 5, and bumps the version in step 7, so a memory-keeper failure or the 6-minute deadline ('finish runs with whatever stages completed') leaves stores updated under the old version, and a step retry re-applies writes. Inngest retries steps by default; nothing here says the writes are idempotent.
  - Evidence: 04 §B 'At the end of stage 7 it calls stores.apply_after_pass(result), which in one transaction ...'; 04 D4 'bumped by one in every store-writing transaction'; 05 section 5 row 'Stages 2 to 7 exceed 6 min'.
  - Fix: Make stages 2 to 6 pure functions that build an `AfterPassResult` (04 §4.3.0) and make stage 7 the single `apply_after_pass` call. On the 6-minute deadline, apply a partial result with `evidence_quality=partial` in one transaction or apply nothing; never half-apply. State the idempotency key of the function (`session_id` plus trigger) and set `retries=0` on model-call steps.
- **blocker** (3.3 Events consumed: `call.ended`, `chat.closed`, `voicenote.received`, `recording.completed`; 4.2 'analyst.full ... receives everything below' (all turns plus chat); 4.5.4 `row.observations.append(...)`): No idempotency or evidence watermark. The topology triggers `session/after-pass` on four events per session, and `analyst.full` re-reads all learner turns each time, so the chat-close run re-extracts the call's edits and appends them again: pattern observations double (the 3-across-2 rule is met from one session), word-bank pseudo-counts double (+3 per unprompted production), `contexts_14d` doubles, and the Kalman step runs twice on the same data. Nothing dedupes by source event and span.
  - Evidence: 01 row `session/after-pass` trigger list; 05 4.5.4 code appends per edit with no key; 05 4.5.1 evidence weights table.
  - Fix: Key every observation by `(source_event_id, span_start, span_end, structure_id)` with a unique constraint; each run processes only events after the learner's `high_water_seq` of the previous `learner_model_versions` row (04 has it). Simpler for the beta: stage 1 on `call.ended`; stages 2 to 7 once per session on `chat.closed` or session close, with `voicenote.received`/`recording.completed` handled by their own small functions.
- **major** (3.4 stage-1 table, final row '**46** Typical end | **88 worst**'; 'The step has a 60 s Inngest timeout on the model calls'): The worst-case column sums to 3+20+25+1+25+13+10+1 = 98 s, not 88, so the stated worst case already breaks the 90 s budget. The 60 s step timeout rescues it only by degrading; an Inngest step timeout triggers a retry unless `retries=0`, which would re-call Haiku, Sonnet and TTS. The Sonnet notes call has no `effort`/thinking setting: Sonnet 5.5 runs adaptive thinking at default effort `high`, which can add 10 to 30 s and 1 to 3k billed output tokens; the latency row assumes none. Polish-line TTS (3 to 10 s) sits on the critical path although nothing needs the audio before the learner opens the card.
  - Evidence: Arithmetic on the table; PRD-v2 15 'Salma's notes after a call: under 90 s'; model facts: Sonnet 5.5 thinking on by default, effort default high; Haiku 5.5 'low' valid.
  - Fix: Fix the arithmetic and budget to 75 s worst. Set `output_config={'format':..., 'effort':'low'}` (or `thinking: {type:'between_tools'}`) on the notes call and record thinking tokens in the cost line. Set `retries=0` and an `asyncio.wait_for` inside the step. Emit `notes.ready` with text and byte-range refs immediately and render the natural-version line in a follow-on step (`notes.audio_ready`); the client shows ▶ when it arrives.
- **major** (4.8.2 'if c.kind != "then_and_now": ... (for then_and_now, "repeated" is met by rule: two recordings are two points)'; candidate generation 'a candidate is generated for both improvement and decline' per metric × task type): Multiple comparisons make the 5% false-claim gate (PRD-v2 16.1) unlikely to hold. With threshold 2×sem on a difference of two 2-point means (SE = sem), each candidate has about a 4.6% two-sided false-positive rate. The finale runs 3 to 5 metrics × 2 recording tasks with rule 3 waived (PRD 10.10 rule 3 exempts only first-ever events, not then-and-now), so the chance of at least one false 'measured difference' per finale is roughly 25 to 40%. Weekly candidates (about 15 to 30 per learner per week) are protected by rule 3 only partially because the two after points share the same before mean.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.10 rule 3; 16.1 'False-claim rate ... under 5%'; binomial arithmetic on the gate's own thresholds.
  - Fix: Do not waive rule 3 for then_and_now: require the same direction beyond threshold on both recording tasks (rec_retell and rec_opinion), or corroboration from the weekly retell segments in the same window. Raise `NOISE_K` for single-pair claims to about 2.8, or apply a per-proof family correction (rank candidates, admit only the top one at K=2 and the rest at K=2.8). Record the expected false-pass rate per claim kind in 4.8.4 so the audit has a target.
- **major** (4.8.2 '`sem` for a metric is sd(x2 − x1)/√2 over the test-retest pairs in `metric_calibration` (same learner, same task type, 2 to 7 days apart)'; 4.16 `test_retest_pairs` table; section 10 item 1): No function writes `test_retest_pairs`, and the only source named (8 to 10 M0 learners re-recording the same form 3 to 5 days later) yields at most 10 `rec_*` pairs (below MIN_PAIRS=12) and none for `retell` or `round_two`, so weekly metric claims can never unlock. Repeating the same form measures practice effect, not noise. Two daily retells 2 to 7 days apart are different stories at different difficulty, so their differences include topic variance and growth, violating the rule's own like-for-like premise. The primary key `(metric, task_type, assessment_version)` resets `n_pairs` to 0 on every version bump, so any bump in the first beta weeks closes metric claims again for weeks.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.10 'estimated per metric from learners who recorded twice a few days apart'; 05 4.16 DDL; 05 D11.
  - Fix: Specify the writer: `calibration/pairs-nightly` builds pairs per task type with explicit rules (recordings: forms A/B 3 to 5 days apart, not the same form; retell/round_two: consecutive sessions 2 to 7 days apart with the same `difficulty_tag`, stated as pseudo test-retest). Carry `metric_calibration` across versions that do not change measures code (version the measures module separately from the analyst prompt). State the statistical caveat that sd from 12 pairs has about ±21% relative error and consider 20 as the bar.
- **major** (4.9 step 2 'precision ≥ 0.8 for every (structure_id) tag that feeds a claim'; 4.10 step 4 'six anchor transcripts per band with their adjudicated bands'): The calibration bars cannot be computed as specified. 150 to 200 samples spread over a few hundred structure tags give 0 to 3 instances per tag, so per-tag precision is undefined for almost all tags; the rule either blocks every grammar claim or is meaningless. The rater needs 30 anchor transcripts (6 per band × 5 bands) with adjudicated bands, but Milestone 0 produces about 30 band-rated recordings in total, which are also the only test set, so anchors and evaluation overlap and QWK ≥ 0.7 on 30 items has a confidence interval too wide to pass or fail.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.11; Milestone 0 scale (15 learners × 2 recordings); 05 build plan week 3 'the first SEM numbers'.
  - Fix: Calibrate precision at the 12-category level plus the specific structures that are or were targets, and set `claim_eligible=true` only for tags with ≥ 10 adjudicated instances (expect 10 to 20 structures in the beta, which is enough for claims on active targets). Split anchors from the test set (anchors from M0 week-0 recordings, test from week-3), state the QWK confidence interval in `calibration_runs`, and treat the rater bar as provisional until beta samples reach about 100.
- **major** (4.3 'errant aligns original to corrected (both tokenized with spaCy en_core_web_sm)'; 4.2 prompt 'Quote them exactly as given, including fillers'; 4.3 fallback `OTHER: word_choice`): ERRANT is built for written, punctuated, cased text. The analyst's `original` includes fillers and repetitions by rule, the `corrected` form drops them, so every alignment carries extra U: edits that consume the '4 token edits' validation budget and fall into the `OTHER → word_choice` bucket, inflating that category. spaCy's tagger and dependency parse on lowercase, unpunctuated ASR text degrade the rule features the table depends on (`corrected_tag: VBD`, `sentence_is_question`, `original_prev_dep_any: relcl`). The rule table needs bespoke feature extractors (`head_noun_countable`, `corrected_irregular`, `pron_object`) that do not exist in errant.
  - Evidence: ERRANT (Bryant et al. 2017) operates on written corpora; assessment lens warns the recognizer is the first measurement error; the verbatim path stores raw verbatim text.
  - Fix: Normalize before alignment: strip the package filler list and immediate repetitions from both strings (the pruned list in 4.4 already does this), ignore U: edits on filler tokens, and request punctuation and casing from the verbatim vendor (`format_text`/`punctuate` options) or run a punctuation restorer before parsing. Define each custom match feature as a named Python predicate with a unit test, cap the week-1 table at about 30 target structures plus the 12-category fallback, and grow it from calibration misses, not from the Grammar Profile wholesale.
- **major** (4.1 'thr = package.asr.confidence_threshold  # 0.70 placeholder'; 01 row `call/verbatim-turn` 'retries 2 with the secondary vendor on the second retry'): One threshold for two vendors. Vendor confidence scores are not calibrated probabilities and differ in scale between AssemblyAI and ElevenLabs Scribe; a turn transcribed by the secondary on retry gets `clean` under a threshold set for the primary, so `clean=true` means different things across turns of one session and across sessions, and claims may rest on it.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.14 sets the threshold from the 30-clip test per recognizer; 08 vendors.yaml `verbatim_stt` fallback.
  - Fix: Make it `asr.confidence_threshold[vendor]` set per vendor by the 30-clip test; store `verbatim_vendor` on the segment and the evidence row (already stored on the turn); in the gate, require claim evidence from a vendor with a calibrated threshold (`clean_evidence` fails otherwise).
- **major** (4.9 `calibration_samples {learner_ref (opaque) ...}`; 4.16 `test_retest_pairs (learner_ref text ...)`, `calibration_runs`): Cross-learner tables keyed by an opaque `learner_ref` with no `learner_id` cannot be purged on account deletion or data-use revocation, and are outside RLS. 04's purge order does not list them. PRD-v2 12.5.8 requires live purge within 24 h; trust promise 8 makes data use revocable.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 12.5.5, 12.5.8, 1.3 promise 8; 04 purge list in `learner/delete` step 5 omits these tables.
  - Fix: Add `learner_id` (RLS-exempt role for the calibration job) or a `learner_ref → learner_id` map in a purge-aware table; add the three tables to `learner/delete` and to `learner.consent_changed {data_use:false}` handling; `calibration_runs` keep only aggregates. Say explicitly that `calibration_samples.text` is pseudonymized by 07's `research/anonymize` before a human tagger reads it.
- **major** (4.5.6 Skill estimates (feature tables, R and Q values, `skill_scoring.yaml`); 4.9 step 5 'calibrated ... and the external test correlation exists with r ≥ 0.5 on ≥ 20 learners'): The four-skill Kalman machinery delivers nothing learner-facing in the beta: calibration needs the week-8 DET sitting (early February 2027 at the earliest for 2026-12-07 starters), so `skill_answer()` returns None until after the beta ends. Meanwhile the observation `z` depends on `skill_scoring.yaml` band anchors that are not given anywhere (how `errors_per_100 = 6` maps to 0 to 100 is undefined), and listening/reading features like 'huh_per_min relative to predicted coverage' have no formula. This is a week of work with no beta payoff and an under-specified interface the teacher's difficulty dials (PRD 9.6) would consume.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.8.3, 10.13 (week 0 and week 8), 17 'where do I stand for skills not yet calibrated' out of scope; 05 4.5.6 table.
  - Fix: Beta: keep one scalar per skill from the band prior, moved only by the word check (listening, reading) and the recording rater band plus `errors_per_100`/`mlr` (speaking); fixed R, no feature tables. Publish the anchor table for the two speaking features used. Defer the full four-skill scoring to P1 after the first DET correlations exist, and say so in the build plan.
- **major** (4.2 'Two variants of one prompt ... analyst.fast (stage 1) ... analyst.full (stage 2)'; 4.7.1 'nf = [c for c in nf if claims.gate(c).passed]'): Stage 1 passes a `first_ever`/`did_it` claim from the fast analyst and shows it; stage 2 re-analyzes the same turns with a different prompt and non-deterministic output, so the full pass may not reproduce the edit behind the polish card or the moment behind the shown claim, or may produce a second candidate for the same event. Which variant's tags the calibration set certifies is not stated. No dedupe rule between stage-1 claims and stage-2 candidates exists.
  - Evidence: 05 4.2; 4.8.3 status lifecycle; PRD-v2 10.11 'calibration set re-run on every model change' presumes one analyst.
  - Fix: Make stage 2 incremental: it receives stage 1's validated edits for the call turns and analyzes only the chat and the turns stage 1 skipped (`skipped_spans`), so call edits are extracted once. Stamp claims with `analyst_run_id` and dedupe candidates by `(kind, subject, source_event_id)`. Calibrate the one prompt used on call turns.
- **major** (7 Build plan weeks 1 to 3 (structure_rules v1 with 120 structures, errant, classifier with tests, measures incl. MTLD/CMU/clause parsing, claims module, all migrations in week 1; analyst, updater, word check, after-pass end to end in week 2; memory keeper, SEM recompute, recording rater, calibration CI in week 3)): The plan assumes a dedicated engineer from week 1, while 01, 02 and 03 schedule the same one to two engineers on the event log, pipeline and call in those weeks and put the after-pass in week 5 (PRD-v2 19.2 also puts it in week 5). The cut list (margin note, avoidance, external import, rebuild automation) removes the cheap items and keeps the expensive ones: the Beta word bank with 13 evidence weights, four-skill Kalman, 12 comparable-segment measures, a few-hundred-row rule table, embedding-based fact matching, 30-anchor rater, calibration CI and a second rebuild.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 19.2 week table; 01 weeks 4 and 5 rows; 04 week 5 row 'apply_after_pass complete'.
  - Fix: Re-sequence to match 01/04: weeks 1 to 2 only the verbatim turn body, confidence filter and the 30-clip threshold; weeks 3 to 4 analyst, classifier (about 30 structures plus categories), measures limited to speech_rate, mlr, mid_clause_pause_rate, errors_per_100, and the updater as counts; week 5 notes, memory keeper, event claims and the simple proof. Move the recording rater, SEM machinery, avoidance and metric claims into the beta (they are P1 by PRD). Name what one engineer ships if there is only one: notes, event claims, memory keeper; everything else waits.
- **major** (4.14 memory/correct step 1 'no match → status=unresolved and a review-queue item (human reads it within a day)'; step 2 'Fix at once'; step 3 fan-out): An unresolved flag leaves the wrong fact live for up to a day, against 'fixed at once' (PRD 8.4.7, trust promise 7). The fix path never bumps `learner_model_version`, yet 04 caches every brief by that version, so a corrected fact can still reach a host through a cached brief. The trigger event `memory.flagged {learner_id, event_id}` carries no `claim_id` or disputed quote, so step 4 ('every claim whose evidence contains that quote') cannot be implemented from the payload.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 8.4.7; 04 D12 briefs cache keyed by `learner_model_version`; 05 3.3 event shape.
  - Fix: On an unresolved flag, put every fact used in the last call's callbacks and Today's memory line `on_hold` (never spoken) until resolved. Route the fix through 04's `apply_learner_edit` so the version bumps and caches invalidate by construction. Extend the event to `memory.flagged {learner_id, event_id, fact_id|null, claim_id|null, disputed_text}`.
- **minor** (4.10 'Form rotation per learner: season 1 "then" = A or B ... "now" = the other; season 2 uses C and D'; 4.16 `recordings unique (learner_id, season_number, kind)`): The schema implies a separate 'then' and 'now' recording per season, so a learner records twice at every season boundary; the PRD has one recording every 4 weeks and the finale compares it with the last one. Doubling the one performance moment doubles the chore risk the assessment lens warns about. Also the M0 learners' baseline forms (A/B by ID parity) may collide with the beta parity rule.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.9.2.3 'Afterwards: every season finale'; 11.2.2 'played next to the last one'; assessment lens 'The anchor sample becomes a chore'.
  - Fix: One `recordings` row per boundary (`sequence_n`), the comparison is consecutive rows; drop `kind`. Rotation A→B→C→D→A by sequence, with the M0 baseline counted as sequence 0 and its form recorded.
- **minor** (4.5.1 'A later edit on an owned word does not un-own it (counts only grow, law 1)'): Law 1 bans game furniture; it does not say evidence may never be revised. A word shown under 'words you own' that the learner now gets wrong repeatedly is a standing untrue statement under trust promise 1. The PRD is silent, so this needs a decision, not a law citation.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 1.2 law 1 text; 1.3 promise 1.
  - Fix: Keep `owned_at` (the two sentences were real) but add `shaky_since` when ≥ 2 clean edits on the word occur after ownership, and let the notebook show the word with its later sentence rather than silently; mark [OPEN for Muath].
- **minor** (4.7.3 Law scan 'any digit outside the header and the quote'): Arabic text may carry Eastern Arabic digits (٠–٩) and number words; the scan as written misses them, and the proof scanner's exception for 'cumulative counts' needs the same handling.
  - Evidence: PRD-v2 10.12 'never a number or a grade'.
  - Fix: Scan `[0-9٠-٩]` and a package list of Arabic number words outside the allowed slots; unit-test with Eastern digits.
- **minor** (4.2 TargetUse.prompted computed by the analyst ('the host said the word in the two turns before ...')): Prompted-ness is computable from host turns and events in code (host `call_turn` text, `recall_gap_id`, `talkback_word`, `say_it_once`), yet the model decides it, against the document's own 'models propose, code decides'; `unprompted` drives the owned rule and `first_ever` claims.
  - Evidence: 05 section 1 principle; PRD-v2 10.6 'unprompted'.
  - Fix: Have code set `prompted` from the two preceding host turns and the event log; the analyst only reports the use and the quote.
- **minor** (4.15 `rater.pinned_until 2026-12-31`; section 10 item 7 recommends 2027-01-31; 01 §1007 says `pinned_until: 2027-01-03`): Three documents, three dates for the first rater window.
  - Evidence: 01 line 1007; 08 vendors.yaml line 464; 05 4.15.
  - Fix: Settle one value in `vendors.yaml` (2027-01-31 aligned to the beta cohort, as 05 recommends) and have 01 and 08 adopt it.
- **minor** (6 Cost table (Haiku 'effort low', Sonnet notes 3k in 0.4k out, proof batch 'when the proof day is ≥ 6 h away')): Output-token estimates omit thinking tokens, which both Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 produce by default and bill as output; at Sonnet's $10/MTok an unconstrained notes call can cost 2 to 4× the $0.006 shown. Batch turnaround is up to 24 h, so a 6 h margin does not guarantee the proof lands on the proof day. Totals still fit the $0.14 allowance, so this is a measurement gap, not a budget break.
  - Evidence: Model facts: adaptive thinking on by default on Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5; Message Batches up to 24 h.
  - Fix: Set effort explicitly on every call, log `usage.output_tokens` including thinking into `vendor_calls`, and use 02's cut-line logic (batch only when > 24 h away, else standard) for the proof.
- **minor** (4.1 'evidence_quality is full when at least 80% of learner words are clean, partial when 40 to 80%'; 4.5.1 priors (k=1500, n0=4, 0.6); 4.5.5 thresholds): Several numbers are presented as decided without the [ASSUMPTION] tag the writing standard requires, and none has a stated test that will revise it.
  - Evidence: Task writing standard; PRD-v2 10.14 makes only the confidence threshold empirical.
  - Fix: Tag them, put each in 4.15 with a 'revised by' column naming the week-1 or week-3 check.

Missing:
- A single `AfterPassResult` definition (or a pointer to 04 §4.3.0) and the statement that all store writes go through `stores.apply_after_pass`; today the document has schemas for analyst and memory output but not for the object the data domain consumes.
