- Idempotency and dedupe rules: the function's idempotency key, the observation uniqueness key, and what happens when `session/after-pass` runs twice for one session (call.ended then chat.closed) or a step is retried.
- The writer of `test_retest_pairs` (which function, which pairing rules per task type, how practice effects on repeated forms are handled) and whether `metric_calibration` survives an `assessment_version` bump that does not touch measures code.
- A multiple-comparisons policy for the gate (candidates per proof, expected false-pass rate per kind, the finale's rule-3 treatment) so the 5% false-claim audit target is reachable by design.
- `skill_scoring.yaml` anchor values (or a statement that it is deferred) and the formula for 'huh_per_min relative to predicted coverage'.
- Per-vendor confidence thresholds and the rule for evidence transcribed by the secondary vendor.
- Purge and revocation handling for `calibration_samples`, `test_retest_pairs` and `calibration_runs` (PRD-v2 12.5.8, trust promise 8).
- Text normalization before ERRANT alignment (fillers, repetitions, casing, punctuation) and the named feature predicates the rule table needs.
- The `openable()` contract: which claim kinds allow a text-only fallback when voice storage is off, and what 'New for you ▶' does without audio.
- Where `AnalystOutput` is persisted (as an event or table) so a rebuild can replay without vendor calls, as 04 assumes.
- A one-engineer version of the build plan: what ships if the team is one person splitting time with the call and pipeline domains.

Strengths:
- Per-turn verbatim transcription during the call (D1) is the right structural call: it is what makes the 90 s notes and the 15-minute deletion window both feasible, and it matches 03 D9.
- The claim gate as a pure code module with pre-rendered Arabic claim sentences and the `no_standard_error` failure state is exactly PRD-v2 10.10 and law 12 made concrete; 'the proof producer only ever receives sentences' makes a fabricated win a code bug rather than a prompt failure.
- 'Models propose, code decides' is carried through consistently: analyst quotes are validated as exact substrings, the memory keeper's quote check plus `text_overlap >= 0.6` blocks inferred facts, card selection and the law scan are code.
- The failure-mode table (section 5) is unusually complete and each row names what the learner sees and what happens to claims; the degraded notes path keeps the 90 s promise.
- Cost headroom is real and the arithmetic per model call checks out against current list prices; the domain is not where the session budget is at risk.
- The correction propagation shape in D16 (fix the fact, invalidate dependents by event, retract disputed-quote claims with a notice) is the right design even though its details need the fixes above.
- The honest-sentence table (4.11.2) and the 'worse week with a supported reason' rule turn the assessment lens's advice into fixed slots the model cannot soften.


---

# Reviews of domains 07 to 09 (2026-11-06)

Written by the "Finish the architecture documentation" thread against PRD-v2, concept-v2 (updated 2026-10-23), decisions.md (updated 2026-10-23) and the other eight domain documents. Same two lenses and the same format as the reviews above. One fact bears on all six reviews: the nine documents were written on 2026-10-16 and 10-17, before Muath's decisions of 2026-10-23 (no launch country or group, a language gate, monthly USD tiers of 1, 2 or 3 episodes a day, invisible teaching with no correction notes, evidence instead of tests, 4-week seasons) and before the pilot kit re-dated the build to 2026-11-18 and the beta to 2026-12-23. Findings that follow from that drift are marked "(decision drift)" so the cross-domain pass in 10-decision-records.md can fix them once.


## review:conformance:07-security-privacy-and-compliance

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (Whole document; most visibly 3.2 `packages/package_ar_en/consent/consent_texts.yaml` and `privacy/notice_ar.md`, 4.3 consent copy in Arabic only, 4.8 "Saudi resources (911, 937, the national mental-health line)", 4.9 "What I understand the Saudi Personal Data Protection Law to ask", 4.15 "SDAIA within 72 h", 4.14 "Saudi consumer e-commerce rules") (decision drift): The domain is designed for one jurisdiction (Saudi Arabia, PDPL, SDAIA) and one native language (Arabic). Decision 1 of 2026-10-23 removed the launch country and group: learners come from anywhere, the beta opens in every language that passes the gate, and the legal review must cover "the countries learners come from (GDPR, Saudi PDPL and others)". As written, an EU learner's consent is shown in Arabic under a Saudi notice, the breach clock names the wrong regulator, and the crisis card shows Saudi phone numbers to a learner in Berlin.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §1 "no first learner, no launch country or group"; concept-v2 §11 row 12 "legal review for the countries learners come from (GDPR, Saudi PDPL and others) and California SB 243"; concept-v2 §7 "The learner's own language needs only interface text, glosses, and a recognizer"; 07 §4.9 table is PDPL-only; 07 §4.8 keyword list and clips are `en` and `ar` only.
  - Fix: Make jurisdiction and native language per-learner attributes, told not inferred: `learners.residence_country` asked at A9 (one question, "which country do you live in", never derived from IP) and `learners.native_language` from onboarding. Move consent texts, the privacy notice, the crisis clips and the resources card into a per-native-language package (`packages/native/<lang>/consent/`, `privacy/`, `crisis/`), with English as the universal fallback. Replace 4.9's single obligations table with a regime table (EU and UK GDPR: lawful basis, Art. 33 72-hour notice to the lead supervisory authority, a DPIA for voice processing and ability profiling, SCCs for US vendors; Saudi PDPL as written; a "not yet reviewed" row that lists what a learner from an unreviewed country is told). Crisis resources per country from one `crisis/resources.yaml` keyed by ISO country, with an international directory entry as the fallback when the country is missing. Add [OPEN for Muath, with counsel]: which regimes the beta covers on day one (recommended: EU/UK GDPR plus PDPL plus the generic baseline) and whether signups from uncovered countries are accepted with the generic notice.
- **major** (4.14 Payments: `plan in ('founding','monthly','yearly')`, `founding_seq` 1 to 100 "yearly plan price", "prices in `plans.yaml` are VAT-inclusive SAR per PRD 1.4", "Tap, Moyasar or HyperPay carry Mada"; section 2 row "Payments: SAR, VAT invoices, Mada, Apple Pay, Visa, Mastercard, recurring") (decision drift): The subscription model is PRD-v2's (founding SAR 349 a year, monthly SAR 89, yearly SAR 449). Decision 2 replaced it with three monthly USD plans by new episodes a day ($19.99, $29.99 recommended, $44.99), monthly only, a 7-day trial on the 2-a-day plan, founding members keeping their plan's price for as long as they stay subscribed, one USD list at launch, cancellation in one tap and no yearly plan.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §2 table and "Still adjustable: the exact price points, the trial plan, regional prices"; concept-v2 §8 "Three plans differ only in how many new episodes are made each day", "Founding members keep their price", "One USD price list at launch", "Cancelling takes one tap".
  - Fix: `subscriptions.plan in ('one_a_day','two_a_day','three_a_day')`; `price_cents`, `currency = 'USD'`; `founding_locked boolean` with `founding_seq` 1 to 100 assigned on the first successful payment and the locked price copied into the row; `trial_plan = two_a_day`; state machine unchanged except that `canceled` keeps access to the period end. Replace the Mada and SAR requirement with: cards, Apple Pay and Google Pay worldwide, local methods per region later. Because there is no launch country, sales tax and VAT fall due in many countries; recommend a merchant-of-record processor (Paddle, or Stripe with Stripe Tax and registrations as the fallback) and tag the choice [OPEN for Muath] with the fee difference (about 5% MoR versus about 3% plus tax operations). Keep hosted checkout, webhook replay protection and SAQ-A scope as written.
- **major** (D11 and 4.11: "The allowance already bounds a learner to one episode, one swap, one call, one round two per day"; `COST_GUARD_SOFT_USD = 2.50`, `COST_GUARD_HARD_USD = 5.00` "about four and eight times the $0.60 budget"; rate table rows "6 call starts per learner per day", "Tell Salma an idea 3 per day") (decision drift): The abuse and cost controls assume one new episode a day. Decision 2 sells 1, 2 or 3 new episodes a day, each a full session with its own call. A 3-a-day learner who uses the allowance spends about $2.00 a day at the beta cost before any swap, so the soft guard trips on ordinary use and the hard guard is one return episode away; the "6 call starts" and "3 ideas" limits become per-episode limits in disguise.
  - Evidence: concept-v2 §5 law 11 "One, two or three new episodes a day depending on the plan"; concept-v2 §8 "a 2 or 3 a day learner who uses every episode costs more than they pay until the cost falls below $0.42"; 08 §6.1 per-session cost $0.64 to $0.67.
  - Fix: Scale every per-day limit by `plan.episodes_per_day`: soft guard = 2.5 × episodes × `cost.session_budget_usd`, hard guard = 4 × episodes × budget (so $1.50/$2.40 on 1 a day, $4.50/$7.20 on 3 a day); call starts = 6 × episodes; ideas = 3 per episode slot. Keep swaps at 1 per day (PRD 3.6) and say so. The `learner_spend_daily` counter gains `plan` so the weekly report can read spend by plan.
- **major** (3.4 `guard.check_output` "Salma notes before publish"; 4.3 `notes_mode: always` preference; 4.8 "Salma's notes for that session never contain a polish card quoting the distress turn"; 4.3 voice-storage sentence "we still listen to each call for a few minutes to write your notes") (decision drift): Salma's after-call notes, the polish card and the chat margin note were dropped on 2026-10-23 (decision 3: teaching is invisible; "the after-call correction notes, the polish card, the chat margin note" are gone; Salma "never corrects, never sends notes"). The consent sentence gives a reason for the 15-minute analysis window that no longer exists. More important for this domain: law 7 ("nobody tells the learner they were wrong") is now the product's defining rule and the output guard does not enforce it; it scans for game words and progress claims but would pass "the correct form is 'went'" from a host.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §3 "Dropped from the first version: the after-call correction notes, the polish card, the chat margin note"; concept-v2 §5 law 7 "No pop-ups, no red marks, no 'the correct answer is', no correction notes"; 07 §4.10 output guard items 1 to 5 contain no correction check.
  - Fix: Remove the notes and polish paths and the `notes_mode` preference; the guard's surfaces become host lines, chat lines, Salma's explanations-when-asked and the weekly proof script. Add guard item 6, the **correction guard**: a per-language phrase list (`guard/correction_words.yaml`: "the correct form is", "you should have said", "that's wrong", "mistake", "actually it's", "we say ... not ...", and the native-language equivalents for Salma) → `block` and re-request, counted as `moves.violations` and in a new measure `explicit_corrections_per_100_turns` (09). Change the consent sentence to the true reason: "we still listen to each call for a few minutes to understand what you said and plan your next episodes, then delete the audio within 15 minutes".
- **major** (4.8 "the host track plays `crisis_host_en` ... then `crisis_salma_ar` (the same in Salma's voice, in Arabic)"; `crisis/keywords.yaml` with `en` and `ar` lists; 4.10 item 5 "a host line that is mostly Arabic script is blocked (hosts speak English only)") (decision drift): The crisis path and the language guard hard-code English hosts and an Arabic producer. With the language gate, hosts speak the learner's target language (English in the pilot, more later) and Salma speaks the learner's native language, which varies per learner.
  - Evidence: concept-v2 §7 "Languages as packages ... The learner's own language needs only interface text, glosses, and a recognizer"; concept-v2 §8 the five gate checks; decisions.md §1.
  - Fix: Render crisis clips per (target language, native language) pair at package build time from counsel's wording; the keyword list lives in both the target-language package (what the learner says in the language being learned) and the native-language package (talkback and chat in their own language); the guard's script check reads `target_script` from the target-language package and blocks a host line whose majority script is not in it. `exclude_contexts` becomes per language.
- **major** (D7 and 4.9 Residency: "Accept `me-central-1` (UAE) as the default; the AWS Saudi region replaces it if generally available"; 10 #1 "whether the UAE region is acceptable for data at rest") (decision drift): The residency question is framed for Saudi residents. With a worldwide cohort the question inverts: for EU and UK learners, data at rest in the UAE is itself a cross-border transfer with no adequacy decision, while an EU region needs only the AWS DPA for them and standard contractual clauses for the US vendors that every option already needs. Latency does not decide it (01 §3.6 lets `call-worker` run near the vendors).
  - Evidence: 01 §10 #1 asks counsel "may Saudi residents' voice recordings rest in the UAE or EU"; concept-v2 §11 row 12; GDPR Chapter V (adequacy list does not include the UAE or Saudi Arabia).
  - Fix: Recommend `HOME_REGION = eu-central-1` as the default for a global beta and keep `me-central-1` as the option if counsel and the first cohort's make-up favour it; rewrite 10 #1 as "where should a worldwide cohort's voice and transcripts rest" with the two options costed (a one-day config move either way, per 01). Keep this [OPEN for Muath, with counsel]; it stays open in 10-decision-records.md.
- **major** (4.9 processor register row "OpenAI (embeddings, 01 [ASSUMPTION]) ... [DECISION] embeddings are computed on scripts only"; versus 02 §9 item 7 "change the embeddings row from OpenAI to Google (07 excludes OpenAI)"): Two documents read this register in opposite ways: 02 believes 07 excluded OpenAI and asked 08 to switch to Google; 07 includes OpenAI with a scripts-only rule; 08 lists OpenAI. One ruling is needed, and the cheaper one is fewer processors.
  - Evidence: 02 §9.7 quoted; 08 §4.2 price row `openai text-embedding-3-small`; 08 §4.7 `embeddings: {vendor: openai}`.
  - Fix: Rule: embeddings run on Google's embedding model under the existing Google processor row; delete the OpenAI row here and in 08; 02's similarity check and `senses` table use the same model. Record as an ADR.
- **major** (4.2 "fails the build if ... any new table without `learner_id` is not on the allowlist `{gloss_cache, ... consent_texts}`"): The allowlist omits every table the cost and evaluation domains define without a `learner_id` (`price_book`, `role_vendor_costs_daily`, `cost_reports`, `vendor_invoices`, `eval_runs`, `release_manifest`, `alert_state`, `incidents`, `feature_flags`, `claim_audits`, `measure_points` at cohort scope), so the CI test as written fails the first build of week 2.
  - Evidence: 08 §4.1, §4.4.1; 09 §4.2, §4.3, §4.6, §4.7.
  - Fix: Make the allowlist a file each domain appends to in its own pull request (`db/rls_allowlist.yaml`), and keep the hard rule only for tables that carry `learner_id` (both RLS policies present). State that `vendor_calls`, `session_costs`, `learner_costs_daily`, `learner_spend_daily`, `call_review_items`, `crisis_log` and learner-scope `measure_points` carry `learner_id` and must have RLS.
- **major** (4.14 `payment_events.payload_ref -- raw body stored in forone-exports/payments/{id}.json (SSE-KMS) for 13 months (tax)`): Raw webhook bodies carry the learner's name and email and are kept 13 months regardless of account deletion, which breaks the 24-hour live purge and the 30-day backup promise (PRD 12.5.8) for identity data. The processor, not For one, is the tax record of record.
  - Evidence: PRD 12.5.8; 04 D17 purge order; 07 §4.4 "Deletion: access ends at once, live purge within 24 h".
  - Fix: Store only `provider_event_id`, `type`, amounts and the processor's invoice id; if raw bodies are kept for debugging, 30 days and deleted by `learner/delete` through a `payments/{learner_id}/` prefix. Add the check to `privacy/verify`.
- **minor** (4.9 obligations table "Data protection officer and controller registration"; no DPIA row): Under GDPR, processing voice recordings of individuals and profiling their language ability for a paid service is a likely trigger for a data protection impact assessment (Art. 35). It is a document counsel helps write, not code, but it belongs on the list of counsel artifacts in 3.2.
  - Evidence: GDPR Art. 35(3)(a) systematic evaluation based on automated processing; 07 §3.2 lists four counsel artifacts.
  - Fix: Add `docs/dpia.md` as the fifth counsel artifact, drafted from this document's threat model and processor register, due before the paid beta.
- **minor** (4.13 role table "`reviewer` (paid native English reader, PRD 6.8)"): With several target languages the reviewer is per language, and the same native speaker is the language gate's third check ("a native speaker reads 10 scripts"). Fix: `staff_accounts.languages text[]`; `review_items` routed by `episodes.target_language`.
- **minor** (Section 7 build plan dated 11-02 to 12-06; D7 "by 2026-11-16"; 10 #1 "by 2026-11-16") (decision drift): The pilot kit re-dated the build to 2026-11-18 to 12-22 and the beta to 12-23. Fix in the cross-domain pass: every date moves by 16 days; counsel's region answer is due before build week 1, so by 2026-11-17.


## review:skeptic:07-security-privacy-and-compliance

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (4.1 `keys.get_dek`: "10-minute in-process LRU, `audit_log` row on miss"; `destroy()` pops only the local `_cache`; D2 "writes an `audit_log` row per unwrap"): The cache defeats both promises D2 makes. (1) The audit row is written only on a cache miss, so every read inside a 10-minute window is unlogged; the learner's `access_log.json` and the "one audit row per unwrap" claim are incomplete by design, and a break-glass read that follows a service read within 10 minutes leaves no `admin_break_glass` row at all. (2) `destroy()` evicts the cache of the process that runs `learner/delete` (a `workflows` task) and no other; `api` runs at least two tasks and `workflows` several, so a destroyed key keeps decrypting that learner's audio elsewhere for up to 10 minutes, after the deletion ledger says the key is gone.
  - Evidence: 07 §4.1 code: `if hit and hit[1] > now: return hit[0]` precedes the audit write; `destroy()` is `_cache.pop(learner_id, None)` on one process; 01 §3.3 `api` "2 tasks min", `call-worker` "autoscaled to 40 tasks"; 07 D2 "an audit row per unwrap (not per read) is cheap and complete".
  - Fix: Cache TTL 60 seconds in `api` and `workflows`, no cache for `purpose = admin_break_glass` or `export`; write an `audit_log` row per `(learner_id, purpose, actor)` per cache window with a `uses` count, so the access log is complete at the granularity the learner is told; `destroy()` first sets `destroyed_at`, then every cache hit re-checks `destroyed_at` with one indexed read (cheap at 60 s TTL), or the cache is keyed by `(learner_id, key_version)` and `destroy` bumps the version. Say in D2 that completeness is per window, not per byte.
- **major** (4.1 `_zero(b)`: "best effort in CPython; the point is not to keep references"; `Dek` holds `key`, `key_b64` and `md5_b64`): `del b` on an immutable `bytes` object zeroizes nothing; three copies of the key material live in the dataclass, in the cache and in every `sse_c_headers()` dict passed to boto3, which keeps its own copies. The document claims zeroization in the component list ("DEK create, unwrap with purpose, cache, zeroize") and in the worker contract ("zeroed at job end").
  - Evidence: CPython `bytes` are immutable and freed by refcount with no overwrite; 07 §3.2 and §9 item 3 quoted.
  - Fix: Drop the zeroization claim and state the real control: short lifetime (60 s cache), no logging, no exceptions carrying the object, the worker holding the key only for the call's duration, and the KMS encryption context binding. If zeroization is wanted later, it needs `bytearray` buffers and a native helper, which is not beta work.
- **major** (D1 and D3: "S3 SSE-C for every learner-scoped audio object", "In the beta, every learner-scoped object stays SSE-C, including show audio"; 4.1 "SSE-C Range reads cost nothing extra"; D3 "01 and 06 meet the 2 s budget with prefetch"): SSE-C requires the key in request headers on every GET, so no SSE-C object can ever be served by a presigned URL, a CDN or a browser `<audio src>` element: every byte of every episode, read-aloud and recap must stream through `api` on Fargate, for the beta and forever. 06's player uses `main.src = network URL` with `api`'s Range support, so this is already what happens, but D3 presents the CDN as a later option and D1 as free, when the real consequence is that produced show audio (no learner voice in it) is locked behind application bandwidth: at 10,000 learners about 100 GB a day through `api` tasks, and the lock-screen podcast feed's enclosures too.
  - Evidence: AWS S3 SSE-C requires `x-amz-server-side-encryption-customer-key` on GET, which presigned URLs cannot carry for anonymous clients; 06 §4.4.4 "the network URL with `api`'s Range support"; 01 §6 fixed cost table has no media egress line through `api`.
  - Fix: Split the key model by kind from day one: learner-voice kinds (`call_turn`, `call_full`, `recording`, `voice_note`, `say_it_once`, `shadow`, `clip`, the name clip) stay SSE-C under the learner's DEK; produced show kinds (`episode_segment`, `episode_full`, `read_aloud`, `recap`, `feed_tag`, `proof`) move to SSE-KMS under the environment key with short-lived presigned URLs (and a CDN when needed), deleted by prefix in `learner/delete` as 04 already does. Key destruction then deletes the voice, which is the promise; the show audio is deleted by the purge. Amend D1, D3 and 4.1 and tell 01 and 06 (media route, player source selection) in the cross-domain pass.
- **major** (4.4 `privacy/verify` check `ephemeral_15m`: "`media_objects where ephemeral and deleted_at is null and created_at < now() - interval '15 minutes'`"; "daily 02:00 Riyadh"): The check is wrong on both axes. The promise is 15 minutes after the call ends, so a turn uploaded at minute 1 of an 8-minute call is legitimately alive 22 minutes after `created_at`; every call in progress or just ended at check time is a false violation. And "02:00 Riyadh" assumes a quiet hour; with learners anywhere there is none.
  - Evidence: PRD 5.4.11 and 15 "within 15 min of call end"; 03 §4.10 sets `expires_at = call_end + 15 min`; 09 §4.6 already uses `expires_at < now() - interval '2 minutes'` for the same check.
  - Fix: `where ephemeral and deleted_at is null and expires_at < now() - interval '2 minutes'`; run `privacy/verify` hourly over the last 25 hours (idempotent, cheap at beta scale) rather than once at a local night-time; the same for every "yesterday" job in this document.
- **major** (4.2 "pgaudit.log = 'read, write' for `forone_readonly` only"; versus 09 D2 "Server-rendered pages in `api` under `/admin/...` read from `measure_points` and the sibling tables through the `forone_readonly` role"): If the admin pages use `forone_readonly`, pgaudit records every dashboard query (about 40 per `ops/watch` run plus every page load) into CloudWatch at 400-day retention, drowning the human-SQL audit it exists for; if they do not, 09's access story needs a role this document does not define.
  - Evidence: 07 §4.2 quoted; 09 D2 and §3.1 "the `/admin` pages read everything through `forone_readonly`".
  - Fix: Define `forone_admin` (RLS bypass for aggregate reads, no write except the admin tables) for the pages, with content reads logged in `audit_log` as 4.12 already requires; keep pgaudit on a `forone_human` role used only by psql sessions. Record as an ADR and change 09.
- **major** (4.11 "Trial abuse. `trial_ledger` keyed by `hmac(secret, identity)` for the email, Apple `sub` and Google `sub`"): The email identity is defeated by plus-addressing and Gmail dot variants, and Apple's "Hide My Email" relay gives a fresh address per sign-up (the Apple `sub` is stable and should be the key for Apple, which the text does say, but the email path is not normalized).
  - Evidence: 07 §4.11 "normalized email" is named but the normalization is not specified.
  - Fix: Normalize before hashing: lowercase, strip `+tag`, strip dots for `gmail.com` and `googlemail.com`; for Apple sign-in hash only the `sub`; accept that a determined person with many real inboxes gets several trials (already in the residual-risk table).
- **major** (4.10 output guard item 4 "Refusal detection: ... three in a row → the governor plays the in-character close and ends with `end_reason: policy`"; 4.10 item 2 "Law-word scan ... → `block` and re-request once"): A re-request costs one host turn of latency (300 to 600 ms of LLM time plus TTS first byte) inside a 1.5 s median budget, and the law-word list contains ordinary English ("level", "test", "score", "points") that a conversation about football or a job interview uses constantly. 09's corpus demands precision 0.95, so about one clean host line in twenty is re-requested, which the latency budget has no room for.
  - Evidence: 03 §3.6 and 01 skeptic finding on the host-reply arithmetic (no headroom); 09 §4.2 "precision at least 0.95 on the clean half".
  - Fix: Split the list into hard words (correction phrases, humanity claims, progress claims, guilt lines: always block) and soft words (level, test, score, points: block only when the sentence is about the learner, detected by a second-person pronoun within six tokens). Measure the re-request rate in the 50-call test and set the precision floor at 0.99 for soft words.
- **major** (4.8 Trigger 2 "Package keyword list ... exact phrase match after normalization"; `en: ["want to die", ...]`, `exclude_contexts` only for English): Learners of English produce the listed phrases in idioms ("I wanted to die of embarrassment", "I'm dying to try it" does not match but "I want to die laughing" does), and the exclude list covers only fiction. Each false positive stops the show mid-call and plays a crisis clip, which is exactly the "feel tested or judged" moment the product promises never to create.
  - Evidence: 07 §4.8 "a false positive costs one interruption and a 'continue' tap"; concept-v2 law 7.
  - Fix: Two-stage trigger: the keyword hit sets a flag the host model must confirm or deny in its next header (`@moves.crisis`), with a 2-second cap after which the keyword alone wins; measure false positives on the Milestone 0 transcripts before the list is allowed to fire alone (the document already asks Muath this; make the default "confirm first").
- **minor** (4.1 key table "`RESEARCH_PSEUDONYM_SECRET` ... Never rotated (rotation unlinks deletion)"): Correct, but then a leak of the secret plus the research set re-identifies every opted-in learner. Fix: keep the pseudonym map in Postgres (`research_pseudonyms(learner_id, pseudonym)`, deleted with the learner) so the secret can rotate and deletion still finds rows; one table, no HMAC dependency.
- **minor** (4.4 table row "Backups within 30 days ... `job: retention_daily`, `verified_by: config_assertion`"): RDS automated backups are deleted on schedule, but the Multi-AZ standby and any read replica carry the same data and are not "backups"; fine. The gap is the `vendor_calls_spool` bucket (08) and the worker spool (01), which hold learner ids and turn audio pointers with no lifecycle rule here. Fix: add both prefixes to the lifecycle rules and to `privacy/verify`.
- **minor** (Section 6 "About $30 a month"): Omits the WAF's per-request charge (about $0.60 per million requests; feed polling by podcast apps is the heavy route) and CloudWatch for 400-day pgaudit retention at 10,000 learners. Fix: say about $30 to $45 in the beta, about $150 at scale.


## review:conformance:08-cost-model-and-vendors

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (Whole document prices "one daily session"; 4.4 roll-ups have no plan; 4.5 `SPEND_CAP_USD_PER_DAY` "recomputed as learners × $1.50"; 4.9 volumes "20 sessions a learner a month"; section 6 has no margin table) (decision drift): Decision 2 made the cost model the thing that sets the prices: three monthly USD plans of 1, 2 or 3 new episodes a day, with the rule that no plan may lose money on a learner who uses every episode at the planning cost of $0.35, and the known beta caveat that a 2 or 3 a day learner who uses everything costs more than they pay until the cost falls under $0.42. Nothing in 08 computes cost per learner-month by plan, margin per plan at measured usage, the plan mix, or the blended margin the public-launch gate needs; the daily cap and the volume table are sized for one episode a day and are 50% to 200% low for the upper plans.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §2 margin table (1 a day $19.99, 63% typical / 38% full use; 2 a day $29.99, 59% / 18%; 3 a day $44.99, 63% / 18%; "Highest cost per session that still breaks even at full use: $0.57 / $0.42 / $0.42"); concept-v2 §9 public launch gate "the plan mix keeps the blended margin above about 50%"; 08 §6.4 floor stack "$0.39 to $0.43" after the 2027-01-01 doubling, which is above the $0.42 break-even of the 2 and 3 a day plans at full use.
  - Fix: Add `plan` to `session_costs`, `learner_costs_daily` and `learner_spend_daily`; add a weekly report section "Margin by plan": net price (price less about 15% tax and fees, replaced by the processor's real fee row), measured episodes used, cost, margin, and the break-even cost per session at full use; add `plan_mix` and `blended_margin` lines; make `tools/cost-model/estimate.py` print the decisions.md margin table from `assumptions.yaml` and from measured data. Daily cap = Σ over learners of `plan.episodes × $1.00`. Volumes in 4.9 at 100 learners: about 3,000 sessions a month (a 50/50/0 mix at the concept's usage rates), 9,000 at full use of the top plan. State plainly in section 6 that at the post-doubling floor of $0.39 to $0.43 the 2 and 3 a day plans lose money on full-use learners and that either the price points or the floor stack must move; tag [OPEN for Muath].
- **major** (3.3 "1 Salma notes, 1 to 2 polish TTS, ... 0 to 1 margin note"; 4.4.1 mapping row "Salma's notes and chat: `salma_text`, `chat_host`, polish-line `salma_tts`, `margin_note`"; 6.1 row "Salma's notes and chat ... $0.037"; D6 roles) (decision drift): The after-call correction notes, the polish card and the chat margin note were dropped by decision 3. Their cost lines (about $0.016 a session) and their roles should go; what remains of Salma per session is the rare explanation when asked, the weekly proof voice (already in the proof share) and onboarding (acquisition).
  - Evidence: decisions.md §3 "Dropped from the first version: the after-call correction notes, the polish card, the chat margin note"; concept-v2 §7 Producer row "Never corrects, never sends notes".
  - Fix: Rename the line "Salma (when asked) and chat", remove `margin_note` and polish TTS, add `salma_explain` (Sonnet, on demand, assume 0.3 calls a session, about $0.002); the chat host stays ($0.021). Carry the new line into the PRD-v2 14.5 mapping with a note that the PRD line itself is revised by the PRD-v3 thread.
- **major** (4.6.1 "10 raters (5 Saudi learners from the Milestone 0 pool ..., 5 native English speakers)"; "one 20-second Salma passage (Arabic ...) Gulf-dialect voices and neutral educated voices"; 4.6.3 "30 clips of real Saudi learners ... Arabic code-switching"; 4.7 `talkback_stt: {languages: [ar, en]}`; `packages/language/ar-en/recognizer.yaml`) (decision drift): The vendor tests are the first three checks of the language gate (concept-v2 §8) and must be parameterized by target language, not by one learner nationality. Salma's voice is one per native language, chosen when that native-language package is built; the Gulf-versus-neutral Arabic test is an Arabic-package question, not a beta-wide one.
  - Evidence: concept-v2 §8 gate table ("5 native listeners rate two-host episodes from 3 vendors blind"; "On 30 real learner clips, the verbatim transcript keeps the learner's errors ... and catches words said in the learner's own language"); concept-v2 §9 "In parallel, run the language gate on the candidate languages".
  - Fix: Rewrite 4.6 as `vendor-lab gate --target <lang>`: raters are 5 native speakers of the target language plus 5 learners of it from anywhere; clips are 30 real learner clips per target language with code-switching into whatever the learner's own language is; `talkback_stt.languages = [learner.native_language, target]` set per call from the learner row; paths become `packages/lang/<target>/recognizer.yaml`; the Salma voice choice moves to the native-language package build. English is the pilot target and the only language whose gate must pass before the beta.
- **major** (D3 "Langfuse Cloud EU, `user_id` = learner id, 100% sampling"; 4.3.2 "Prompts and completions in full for text roles ... The learner's name appears inside prompts ... allowed under the topology's processor approval"): 01 was revised on 2026-10-17 after this document was written and now says the opposite: Langfuse receives span metadata and pseudonymous ids by default, and prompt text only for learners with the data-use switch on, scrubbed, with 30-day retention. 07's register row still says `learner_id` as `user_id`. Three documents, three rules for the same processor.
  - Evidence: 01 §10 #2 "Langfuse Cloud (EU; span metadata and pseudonymous ids for every learner; scrubbed prompt and completion text only for learners with the data-use switch on; 30-day retention)"; 02 §4.16 "Langfuse (01 D15) receives metadata only unless the learner opted into data use"; 07 §4.9 Langfuse row.
  - Fix: Adopt 01 D15 here: `user_id` is the research pseudonym, never the learner id; prompts and completions are sent only when `consents.data_use` is true at call time and after `anonymize()`; otherwise the observation carries ids, versions, usage, cost and latency, and debugging reads the prompt from the artifacts the pipeline stores in the home region (02 keeps every prompt and completion as a `pipeline_steps` artifact; the call brief and turns are in `events`). Deletion by pseudonym. Record as an ADR and amend 07's row.
- **major** (4.2 `prices.yaml` "Cache write = 1.25x input (5-minute TTL)"; versus 02 §4.16 "1-hour cache writes at 2x input" and 02 §9.7 "add the 1-hour cache-write price rows"; 02 §9.7 also asks 08 to "re-adopt section 6's table (writer 9k output, alignment always with Gemini, the pre-write and idea-refresh lines, cached and uncached columns)"): The two documents price caching differently and 08 ignores the pipeline's revised unit table, so the writer and teacher lines in 6.1 are not the pipeline's. A 1-hour cache is what a 6-to-8-minute call and a batch pipeline need; the 5-minute rate underprices every cache write by 37.5%.
  - Evidence: 02 §4.16 and §9.7 quoted; 08 §6.1 teacher and writer arithmetic uses 02's older numbers.
  - Fix: Add `cache_write_1h_tokens` rows at 2x input for Opus, Sonnet and Haiku; adopt 02 §6's unit table (writer 9k output, pre-write and idea-refresh lines, alignment with Gemini) in `assumptions.yaml`; recompute 6.1 and say which document's numbers it now reproduces.
- **major** (4.7 `editor` fallback `{vendor: anthropic, model: claude-haiku-5-5, note: "breaks second-vendor rule; review item"}`; `embeddings: {vendor: openai}`): 02 §9.7 asks for the editor fallback to be `claude-opus-5-5` (a stronger judge when the second vendor is down, since the whole point of the editor is to catch what the writer's vendor misses) and for embeddings to move to Google. Both are pipeline rulings about pipeline roles; 08 should carry them.
  - Evidence: 02 §9.7; 07 register (OpenAI row) and the 07 conformance finding above.
  - Fix: Editor fallback → `claude-opus-5-5` standard (about $0.05 a session on the rare fallback day, flagged `same_vendor_edit` for review); embeddings → Google's embedding model under the Google processor row; delete the OpenAI rows.
- **major** (6.1 note 4 "Fixed infrastructure (01) is on top of these numbers: about $1.30 a session at 15 learners, $0.25 at 100, $0.10 at 250, $0.05 to $0.06 at 10,000"): 01's revised section 6 says $2.30 to $2.80 at 15 learners, $0.35 to $0.43 at 100, $0.14 to $0.17 at 250, and $0.03 to $0.05 at 10,000; and its fixed budget is $800 to $1,100 a month, not the $400 to $600 this document was written against.
  - Evidence: 01 §6 "Fixed infra per session" row and §10 #4.
  - Fix: Adopt 01's numbers and print them as the report's "fixed" line; at the top plan's usage the per-session share falls faster, which the margin section should show.
- **major** (4.8 Vendor risk calendar and section 7 build plan: recognizer deadline 2026-11-08, voice decision 2026-11-13, "2026-12-07 Beta starts", "2027-01-10 Beta gate review"; D10 "both finalists implemented in week 1") (decision drift): The pilot kit re-dated everything on 2026-10-23: Milestone 0 runs 2026-10-24 to 11-18, the build 11-18 to 12-22, the beta 12-23 to 2027-01-26. The vendor tests are now part of the language gate that runs in parallel with Milestone 0, so the voice and recognizer decisions can still land before build week 1, which is better than the old plan, but every date in this document is wrong and the Gemini doubling now falls in beta week 2 rather than week 4.
  - Evidence: milestone-0/03-schedule.md header; concept-v2 §9 phase table.
  - Fix (applied in the cross-domain pass): recognizer test by 2026-11-10 (during Milestone 0, on the clips the pilot already produces), voice decision by 2026-11-17, `vendors.yaml` committed before build week 1 (11-18), spend cap recomputed from 12-23, the next-week-prices report from 12-21, gate review 2027-01-26.
- **minor** (4.4.2 "Mondays 06:00 Asia/Riyadh"; 4.4.4 "the 2nd of each month"): Learners are in every timezone; report in UTC with each learner's local date for the session rows. Fix: Mondays 06:00 UTC.


## review:skeptic:08-cost-model-and-vendors

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (3.2 step 2 "writes one `vendor_calls` row in the same transaction as the caller's own write when there is one (the pipeline's `pipeline_steps` row, the worker's event batch)"; "Live-path rows are buffered in the worker and flushed every 5 seconds or at call end"; 5 "flushed to a local file and re-ingested"): The cost record is coupled to the caller's transaction, so it is lost exactly when it matters most. A vendor call happens before the step's write; if the step's transaction rolls back (Inngest retry after a timeout, a validation failure, a crashed task), the money was spent and the row is gone, so a retry storm, the one failure the spend guard exists to catch, is the case where spend is undercounted. The worker's 5-second buffer and local-disk spool die with the Fargate task. 07's `learner_spend_daily` is "reconciled nightly" against these rows, so the per-learner guard runs on a counter that drifts for a day.
  - Evidence: 08 §3.2, §5 row "Postgres write of `vendor_calls` fails in the live path"; 07 §4.11 "`learner_spend_daily` ... reconciles it nightly against `vendor_calls`"; 01 §5 "Spend runaway (loop, retry storm)".
  - Fix: Write `vendor_calls` in its own short autocommit transaction immediately after the vendor responds, never inside the caller's transaction, idempotent on `(inngest_run_id, step, attempt)` or `(call_id, turn_n, capability)`; update `learner_spend_daily` in the same statement (one upsert) so the guard counter and the ledger cannot diverge; in the worker, append each turn's rows to an S3 spool object per call (the bucket 01 already uses for event spooling) before the 5-second flush, so a dead task loses nothing. Reconcile hourly, not nightly.
- **major** (4.5.2 `conserve` mode: "teacher falls to `claude-sonnet-5-5` batch; ... host falls to `claude-haiku-5-5`"; "none the learner can tell"; versus 09 D8 "flags exist only for P1 features, vendor levers and kill switches" and 09 §4.7 `host.model=haiku` "the call domain's measured fallback as a lever"): An automatic alarm silently downgrades the teacher and the host for every learner when daily spend passes 80% of a cap, a quality change the document itself elsewhere calls "a quality decision the 50-call test must make" (D6, 6.3 lever 6). 09 treats the same switch as a human-flipped flag after a passing suite. Two domains, two owners of the same lever, and the automatic one wins at the worst moment (a busy day, which is the product working).
  - Evidence: 08 D6 "Haiku host from day one (a quality decision...)" rejected; 08 §6.3 lever 6 "[OPEN for Muath] ... switch only if equal"; 09 D8 and §4.7.
  - Fix: `conserve` may change only what no learner hears: force batch where available, cap rewrites at 1, skip canaries and the nightly smoke, defer the research anonymizer; model downgrades are feature flags flipped by a person after `eval-gate` passes. `halt` stays as written (on-demand extras only). Record as an ADR.
- **major** (4.2 price rows marked "assumption", "verify", "unverified": Haiku cache read, Gemini Flash-Lite TTS, AssemblyAI batch, Scribe, Microsoft, LiveKit; 6.1 "Inworld ... timestamps included [unverified]"; D11 reconciliation "monthly"): About $0.10 of the $0.64 to $0.67 session rests on unverified rows, and the first check against a real bill is the monthly job on 2027-01-02, four weeks after the voice decision that these numbers drive and after the exit document that claims a measured cost.
  - Evidence: 08 §4.2 annotations; §7 week 5 "the exit document: measured cost per session".
  - Fix: A one-off reconciliation in build week 1: render 10 minutes of audio and transcribe 30 minutes on every finalist, then compare `vendor_calls` sums to each vendor's usage dashboard the same day, before `vendors.yaml` is committed; mark rows `verified: <date>` and fail `estimate.py` on any unverified row used by the chosen stack.
- **major** (4.9 "peak concurrency is about 15% of the day's calls in one hour"; 4.5.2 `spend_mode` "returns to `normal` at the start of the next day in the environment's clock"): Both assume one timezone. With a worldwide cohort, concurrency spreads (good: fewer streams to buy) but there is no "next day" boundary and no quiet window for the roll-up watermark or the daily cap. The daily cap as a UTC day is fine; the text should say so and the concurrency estimate should be recomputed from `learner_schedule` once the cohort exists.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §1; 09 §4.10 "The drop window is 05:30 to 08:30 (most learners pick a commute slot)".
  - Fix: Define the cap day as UTC; compute peak concurrency weekly from `learner_schedule` and print it in the report's vendor section; buy concurrency for the measured peak plus 50%.
- **major** (6.4 "with read-aloud and recap rendered on first tap instead of pre-rendered ... saves the audio for the 60 to 90% of sessions that never open them"): This is the biggest single lever to the scale target and it is presented as a PRD change for Muath, but the architecture cost of the alternative is not stated: on-tap rendering puts a 2 to 4 s synthesis on the learner's path (06's word-sheet and read-aloud contracts assume pre-rendered timings) and needs the streaming TTS path from 03 in the player. If Muath picks it, three domains change.
  - Evidence: 06 §4.4 read-aloud sync from precomputed timings; 02 §6 pre-renders read-aloud; 03 streaming TTS is a call-worker capability.
  - Fix: Add the engineering cost to the row (about four engineer-days across 02, 03 and 06, plus a 2 to 4 s first-sound wait) so the choice in 10 #2 is honest; propose the cheaper middle: pre-render the recap (90 s, always offered) and render the read-aloud on first tap only for learners whose history shows they never open it (the teacher's dial), which keeps the PRD's default for new learners.
- **major** (4.4.1 "The weekly proof's cost is divided by the learner's sessions in that week"; 6.1 "Proof $0.06 a week divided by 4 sessions") (decision drift): With 2 and 3 a day plans a learner can have 14 to 21 sessions in a week, so the proof share per session falls and the comparison with the PRD's $0.03 line stops meaning anything; the honest unit for the proof is per learner-week.
  - Fix: Report the proof as a per-learner-week line and divide only for the per-session gate; say so in the mapping table.
- **minor** (4.3.4 "Sampling is by trace, decided once at trace start from a hash of the trace id"): Fine, but the after-pass trace id `{session_id}:after` is not a hash input the SDK accepts without normalization; use a UUID derived with `uuid5`. Fix: one line.
- **minor** (5 row "Langfuse deletion API fails for a deleted learner ... daily retry 30 days"): With the pseudonymous `user_id` of the conformance fix, the deletion call is by pseudonym and the row is in `research_pseudonyms`; the "deleted" email no longer waits on a processor. Fix: close 04's open question with that.
- **minor** (7 "Milestone 0 itself is untraced ... about $7 a learner for the fortnight"): Useful, keep; add the pilot's measured minutes of learner speech per call to `assumptions.yaml` (`call.minutes`, `learner_turns`), since those two numbers move the call and verbatim lines more than any price.


## review:conformance:09-evaluation-observability-and-operations

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (D11 "the 05:00 to 08:30 Riyadh drop window as the protected hour"; D9 "release window Sunday to Thursday 10:00 to 16:00 Riyadh"; 4.10 "All times Riyadh ... The drop window is 05:30 to 08:30 (most learners pick a commute slot)"; `measures/daily` "06:30 Riyadh (yesterday's learner-days)"; 4.11 `protected_hours: 05:00-08:30`) (decision drift): The operational model assumes one timezone and one commute. Decision 1 puts learners in every timezone, so drops happen around the clock, "yesterday" is a different day for each learner, there is no protected hour and no deploy window that is outside every learner's drop. The alert rules that are already drop-relative per learner (`episode_not_ready_before_drop`) are right; the calendar, the release rule, the on-call rota and the daily jobs are not.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §1; concept-v2 §8 "Anyone, anywhere"; 01 §4.3 `day/next` is per learner in local time; 09 §4.3 release rule "never inside the 30 minutes before any learner's drop time ... the tool checks `learner_schedule` and warns".
  - Fix: Make every rule drop-relative and every job local-date-aware: `measures/daily` runs hourly and computes any learner-local-day that closed in the last hour (idempotent upserts); the digest and reports run at fixed UTC times; the release tool computes the deploy window from `learner_schedule` (deploy only when fewer than 2% of learners have a drop in the next 45 minutes, and never a `call-worker` deploy while `active_jobs > 0`, per 01) and makes the check binding, not a warning; on-call: P1 at any hour with the honest statement that a one-engineer beta accepts late drops in some timezones overnight (the product's fallbacks hold; tag [OPEN for Muath]); replace "protected hour" with "any learner's drop within 45 minutes".
- **major** (4.1 measure 13 `notes_opened_share`, `say_it_once_share` "say_it_once events / polish cards shown"; 4.2 suite `notes` with `arabic_share_ge_80`, `polish_on_active_target`; 4.6 rule `notes_late` and budget row "Notes under 90 s"; 4.7 flag `notes.enabled` "Salma is resting today"; R9) (decision drift): Salma's notes and the polish card were dropped (decision 3); "say it once" stays as light pronunciation (a settled point) but its denominator is gone. More important, law 7 is now the product's defining promise and nothing in this domain measures whether it holds on real calls except the governor's self-declared counters.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §3; concept-v2 §4 Pronunciation row "Tap to hear, shadow two lines; scored internally on a known sentence only"; concept-v2 §10 "Learners fix their errors when nobody tells them they were wrong" is the second riskiest assumption.
  - Fix: Delete the notes measures, suite, rule, flag and runbook steps; `say_it_once_share` = say-it-once events / say-it-once prompts offered; add measure 27 `explicit_corrections_per_100_turns` (the correction guard's blocks from 07 plus `call_review_findings.move_contract.never_rules_ok = false`) with a ceiling of 0 as the law-7 gate, and measure 28 `echo_uptake_share` (planted or observed errors recast by the host that the learner reproduces correctly within the same call or the next session) as the evidence the riskiest-assumption test needs; replace the `notes` suite with a `salma` suite (explanations when asked: law scan, correction guard, native-language share, no progress claims).
- **major** (4.4 gate rows "Trial to paid ... Founding price only"; "Second-month renewal ... not readable inside the beta for yearly founding members"; 10 #5; `referral_trials_share`) (decision drift): Decision 2 has no yearly plan and no founding year; founding is a price lock on a monthly plan, so renewal reads for every subscriber from the second charge. The gates page also lacks the two business lines the concept now asks for: plan mix and blended margin.
  - Evidence: decisions.md §2; concept-v2 §8 "Founding members keep their price"; concept-v2 §9 public-launch gate "the plan mix keeps the blended margin above about 50%".
  - Fix: `renewal_m2` over all paid learners whose first month ended; drop the founding exception and open item 5; add `plan_mix` (share by plan) and `blended_margin` (from 08's margin section) as reported measures, not gates, in the beta.
- **major** (4.1 measure 17 "`recognizer_err_by_l1` ... grouped by `learners.native_language` (one group in the beta ...)"; 4.3 `packages/package_ar_en/versions.yaml`; 4.2 law corpus "in English and Arabic"; D10 "the reviewer for calls ... Muath"; 10 #2 "native English reader"; 10 #10 "an Arabic reader") (decision drift): One language pair is assumed throughout the versioning, the corpus and the review staffing. The beta opens in every target language that passes the gate, to learners of many native languages.
  - Evidence: concept-v2 §7 "Languages as packages"; concept-v2 §9 beta row "in every language that passed".
  - Fix: `versions.yaml` per package: `packages/lang/<target>/versions.yaml` (prompts, structure rules, law words, crisis keywords, recognizer threshold) and `packages/native/<lang>/versions.yaml` (interface, consent, Salma); `eval-gate`'s path map keys on the package directory; the law corpus and the injection set are per language with English as the first; measure 17 is per native language from day one; reviewers are per target language (`staff_accounts.languages`) and the native-language law-scan reviewer is per native language, Muath for Arabic in the beta.
- **major** (4.3 release freeze "2026-12-05 12:00 to 2026-12-08 12:00 (launch)"; 4.4 "First readable 2027-01-04"; 4.10 dated rows 2026-12-07, 2027-01-04, 2027-01-10; section 7 weeks 11-02 to 12-06) (decision drift): The build is 2026-11-18 to 12-22 and the beta 12-23 to 2027-01-26. Fix in the cross-domain pass: freeze 12-21 12:00 to 12-24 12:00 and 12-31 to 2027-01-02; first cohort 12-23 (a Wednesday; the invite cadence becomes Wednesdays or the first cohort is Monday 12-28, [OPEN for Muath]); first "active in week 4" readable 2027-01-20; first finales from 2027-01-19; gate review 2027-01-26; the Gemini doubling lands in beta week 2.
- **major** (4.1 measure 8 and the full SQL: `word_uses` "[ASSUMPTION: it exists under that name or the word bank exposes an equivalent view]"; measure 22 `proofs.sessions_in_week`): 04 defines `structure_observations` but no per-use word table; 05's `AnalystOutput.target_uses` is a list inside a result object, not rows. Delayed recall (a 16.2 measure) cannot be computed as written.
  - Evidence: 04 §4 DDL (no `word_uses`); 05 §4.3 `target_uses` in `AnalystOutput`.
  - Fix: Rule in the cross-domain pass: 04 owns a `word_uses` table (`learner_id, unit_id, event_id, occurred_at, clean, correct, prompted, quote`) written by 05's `apply_after_pass`, the word twin of `structure_observations`; `proofs.sessions_in_week` is computed, not stored.
- **minor** (4.4 "`pmf_answer` ... the Arabic wording"; 4.5.1 "Salma one plain Arabic line on Today"): Copy is per native-language package. Fix: reference `packages/native/<lang>/strings`.
- **minor** (9 item 1 "`episodes.status` (02) versus `episodes.state` (01): this document uses `status` and will follow the integrator's ruling"): Ruled in the cross-domain pass: `state` (01 and 06 already use it); 02 and 09 change.


## review:skeptic:09-evaluation-observability-and-operations

Verdict: needs-revision

- **blocker** (4.6 rule `episode_not_ready_before_drop`: severity P1, `for: 1`, `dedupe: "{learner_id}:{next_drop_at}"`, joins `learner_schedule.expects_drop`; 4.1 measure 24 and the `heartbeat_late` rule): The one rule that protects the product's critical moment pages a phone once per learner, fires 45 minutes before a drop that the pipeline's own on-demand fallback (02: batch cancelled at drop minus 90 minutes) is still allowed to be finishing, and depends on a column (`expects_drop`) no other document defines. At 250 learners spread across timezones a slow vendor night produces a page every few minutes all night; at 10,000 it is unusable. A per-learner page also tells the engineer nothing a runbook can act on faster than the pipeline already does.
  - Evidence: 02 §4.2 escalation at drop minus 90 min; 01 §4.3 `day/next`; 09 §9 item 4 lists `expects_drop` as an assumption on 04; 09 D11 "phone paging only for P1 rules (the six in 4.6)".
  - Fix: Per-learner lateness is P2 to Slack with dedupe per learner; P1 fires when 3 or more learners, or 5% of learners with a drop in the next 45 minutes, have no `ready` episode (a systemic failure, which is what a human fixes); define `expects_drop` as "the learner's previous local day was active" from `day.became_active` and ask 01 to add it to `learner_schedule`. Keep `heartbeat_late` and `platform_down` as P1.
- **major** (4.3 "`eval-gate` ... against `staging` for `host` and `calls_50`"; 4.10 "Nightly 02:00 `eval/nightly` ... on staging"; versus 01 D14 and §6 "Staging ... about $80/month stopped between test windows ... Stopped outside the 50-call runs and the M0 end-to-end run"): The evaluation plan needs staging up every night and on every host-prompt pull request; the topology saves $180 a month by keeping it off. One of them is wrong about the budget.
  - Evidence: 01 §6 staging row; 09 §4.3 path map (host prompts: 20 calls on staging, 15 minutes).
  - Fix: Staging stays up from build week 2 through the beta (add about $180 a month to 01 §6 and §10 #4), because `eval-gate` on the host prompt is the only thing between a prompt edit and the law-7 promise on a live call; after the beta, the host suite runs against an ephemeral worker started by CI. Record as an ADR.
- **major** (4.6 rule `spend_cap_or_stop_line`: severity P1 with `sql_also` "mean session cost over 7 days > 1.00"; D11 "a two-person team is never paged for a trend"): The stop line is a seven-day trend and a business decision (08 §4.5.3 makes it a 48-hour plan window for Muath); paging a phone at 03:00 for it contradicts this document's own rule and 08's design. Only the hard per-learner guard and `daily_spend_100` are runaway signals.
  - Evidence: 08 §4.5.3; 09 D11.
  - Fix: Split the rule: `cost_runaway` (P1, from `cost_guard.tripped {level: hard}` and `daily_spend_100`) and `cost_stop_line` (P3 digest plus the ticket to Muath). Six P1 families stay six.
- **major** (4.9 "sanitized error messages" are allowed in logs; the scrubber "truncates any string field over 200 characters"): Vendor error bodies echo request content. Anthropic and Google 400 responses quote the offending input, and a 200-character truncation keeps the first 200 characters of a learner's turn. This is the one path by which transcript text reaches CloudWatch and Sentry through a field not named `text`.
  - Evidence: 09 §4.9 "error classes and sanitized error messages" allowed; vendor 4xx bodies include `message` fields with excerpts.
  - Fix: Log only the vendor's error class, HTTP status and the vendor's request id; never the message body; add a vendor 400 with an embedded learner sentence to `pii_corpus.jsonl` so the scrubber test covers it.
- **major** (4.2 "Synthetic learners ... caller profile ... rendered to audio once with a TTS voice of the vendor not under test"; suite `recognizers` fixtures `clips/recognizer` only): Correct that recognizer tests use real clips, but the `host` suite's `recast_rate_on_planted` runs on TTS-voiced callers with planted errors, so it measures the host's reaction to a text error cleanly read aloud, not to a learner's hesitant, code-switched turn that the streaming recognizer may have silently fixed (the voice lens's warning). The number will be flattering.
  - Evidence: concept-v2 §3 "Speech-to-text quietly fixes learner grammar"; 09 §4.2 synthetic profiles.
  - Fix: Report `recast_rate_on_planted` separately for synthetic callers and for the Milestone 0 real-audio replays, and gate on the real-audio number once 20 replays exist; label the synthetic number "upper bound".
- **major** (4.5.1 "Expected volume at 100 learners: about 1,000 items a month, 35 a day, about 4 hours of reviewer time a day") (decision drift): With 2 and 3 a day plans the shipped-episode count per learner doubles or triples; "10 per learner per month" (PRD 6.8) was set for one episode a day. Either the sample stays 10 (a smaller share, acceptable) or the hours triple. Fix: keep 10 random per learner per month plus every flagged item, and say that the sampled share falls on the upper plans; print the share in the monthly summary.
- **major** (Section 6 "`eval-gate` runs (3 to 5 version bumps a week × about $5 average) about $80 a month"; 4.3 path map prices the teacher and writer bump at $12 and the host bump at $4 plus 15 minutes of staging): During the five build weeks prompt edits are daily, not weekly; the realistic build-period figure is $300 to $500 a month, and every host bump holds a merge for 15 minutes. Fix: say so; allow `eval-gate` to run the cheap half (governor replay, law scan) on every push and the paid half on a `ready-for-review` label.
- **minor** (4.5.2 "The reviewer for calls in the beta is Muath himself (role `owner`) ... 2 calls per learner per month plus every call with `moves.violations ≥ 2`"): At 100 learners that is 200 transcripts a month plus flagged calls, about 20 hours of Muath's time. Fix: state the hours; start with 1 per learner per month and all flagged calls.
- **minor** (4.3 "a failed canary rolls back to the previous image automatically (ECS task definition revision minus one)"): Revision minus one is not always the previous good release (a failed deploy also registers a revision). Fix: roll back to `release_manifest`'s last `canary_passed = true` image digest.
- **minor** (4.10 "Weekly, Monday 10:00: Invite batch of about 50 goes out"; `cohort_monday`): With a 2026-12-23 (Wednesday) beta start the cohort key is the invite date, not a Monday. Fix: `cohort_key = invite_date`.
